i
2 CFFTS ORDERS
Updated July 2026

ii
2 CFFTS ORDERS
28 Jul 2026
The 2 CFFTS Orders are provided as a 2 CFFTS regulatory directive for Unit procedures and 
guidelines. They are intended to augment the hierarchy of orders that exist at a higher level, such 
as, but not limited to the National Flying Orders, RCAF FOM, Canadian Air Division Orders, 15 
WFO. 
It is the duty and responsibility of all 2 CFFTS personnel to know and comply with these orders. 
All suggested amendments shall follow the orders amendment process outlined in these orders.
C. Cloete
Lieutenant-Colonel
Commandant

1
BOOK 1 – 2 CFFTS ADMINISTRATION
CHAPTER 1 – GENERAL PROCEDURES
2 CFFTS Document Revision
1. All proposed amendments or revisions to 2 CFFTS Orders and other school 
documentation shall be submitted using the 2 CFFTS Orders/Document Change Proposal, found 
at Annex A. Once approved by the Commandant, the new order will be submitted to the Ops O 
for updating the Orders.
Correspondence and Administrative Procedures
2. Correspondence initiated by 2 CFFTS shall follow the format of correspondence detailed 
in the Defence Team Writing Guide.
3. All 2 CFFTS electronic correspondence shall follow the chain of command. E-mail 
content and format shall be IAW CAF Staff and Writing procedures and protocol. Outgoing 
correspondence concerning 2 CFFTS policies and procedures shall be sent via the Commandant, 
or their designate, for review and signature. Queries regarding this matter shall be directed to the 
2 CFFTS AdminO.
4. 2 CFFTS Admin is predominantly handled through the 15 Wing Orderly Room building 
160 (Bldg 160).
5. To effectively manage the daily workload of the Commandant, personnel shall submit all 
documents and correspondence through the chain of command to the Commandant's
administrative assistant using the 2 CFFTS staffing mailbox and 15 Wing MESH.
Clerical Personnel
6. The 2 CFFTS AdminO is responsible for co-ordinating all administrative and clerical 
support for the Unit. 
7. Funds for civilian employees are strictly controlled by the 2 CFFTS AdminO. 2 CFFTS 
section heads employing civilians shall liaise with the AdminO (2 CFFTS RC Administrator) 
before approving funded overtime.
General Safety
8. All members of 2 CFFTS shall adhere to the Wing General Safety Orders.

2
CHAPTER 2 – PUBLICATIONS
Control of Publications
1. Section Publication Officers will control section libraries. Individual publication holders 
will be responsible for their individual publication entitlements. For accountability purposes, all 
publication and amendment distributions will be made via vouchers, loan cards, or group receipts 
controlled by the NFTC Library.
2. A list of 2 CFFTS publications entitlements is at Annex B. 
Responsibilities
3. 2 CFFTS Publications Coordinator. The 2 CFFTS Publications Coordinator is responsible 
for liaising with the Library Specialist at the NFTC Library to ensure that any amendments are 
distributed to the appropriate Section Pubs O.
4. Section Publication Officers. The following sections require a Publications Officer: 2 
CFFTS Ops, Standards, FIS, A, B, C and D Flights. Each Section Pubs O shall maintain 
publications IAW Annex B and is responsible for ensuring that the publications are properly 
amended.
5. The 2 CFFTS Publications Coordinator will conduct an annual Publications Entitlement 
Review. The purpose of the review is to ensure sections are maintaining only essential 
publications.
Distribution of Flight Information Publications (FLIPs)
6. Serco AIS receives FLIPS. AIS is responsible for sorting these FLIPs for distribution to 
all Sections and ATC through the NFTC Library.
Electronic Flight Bag Publications
7. Each pilot is responsible to ensure all required publications are downloaded and available 
when disconnected from Wi-Fi.
8. Unit Electronic Flight Bag Administrators are responsible for ensuring most up-to-date 
versions of the SMM, flight manual, orders, and other documents outside of ForeFlight are uploaded 
onto the EFB.

3
CHAPTER 3 - 2 CFFTS SECURITY STANDING ORDERS
Introduction
Ref: NDSOD – National Defence Security Orders and Directives
1. This Chapter of the orders are to supplement the guidance reference, NDSOD. In addition 
to complying with these orders, each individual should be familiar with the references mentioned 
above, as well as any Flight/Section Security Directives.
Aircrew Training Documentation and Records Security Classification
2. In order to ease the burden of Security Clearance procedures, the designation 
PROTECTED has been developed and implemented for documents not pertaining to National 
Security. PROTECTED documents are accessible by civilian employees who do not hold a 
security clearance but have a reliability check, and by all military personnel on a 'need to know' 
basis. PROTECTED documents will be further identified as follows:
a. PROTECTED A. documents of a personal nature (e.g. contain SN, name, phone 
numbers, Course Reports, CT’d students, OD Assessments, End Course Flying 
Assessments, Aircrew Proficiency files, etc);
b. PROTECTED B. documents of a private nature that could cause harm if released 
(e.g. PRBs); and
c. PROTECTED C. private documents that could cause grave harm if released such 
as exceptionally sensitive medical records.
3. Documents that have information concerning 'National Security' are Classified 
documents and are accessible only to individuals holding a security clearance to the appropriate 
level on a 'need to know basis'. They are further classified as follows: 
a. Confidential (Level 1 Security Clearance required);
b. Secret (Level 2 Security Clearance required); and,
c. Top Secret (Level 3 Security Clearance required).
4. Personnel are reminded that if sensitive information is to be relayed verbally it is the 
individual's responsibility to inform the individuals receiving the information at the beginning 
and end of transmission (e.g. PRBs).

4
2 CFFTS Physical Security
5. Within 2 CFFTS, several areas have been designated as "Restricted Areas" and are out of 
bounds to all personnel not requiring access to perform their duties. These are the Ops Centres, 
hangar lines, flight areas, and repair work areas.
6. Unsupervised visitors shall not normally be permitted within 2 CFFTS work areas in
Bldg 160 or the Hangar line. Prior approval for visits or tours must first be obtained from the 
Commandant, DCO or Supervisor. After receiving the Commandant’s approval, NFTC form 
2288 located in the electronic Aircrew Reference Library - Administration or at the 
Commissionaires’ desk shall be completed. Spouses and other guests should normally be 
escorted by the military member or designate.
7. BLDG 160 is open 24 hours, seven days per week. Authorized personnel, students, and 
staff can use their identification card at any time to enter BLDG 160 to access unrestricted work 
areas.
8. Individually assigned lockers are available in BLDG 160 for the security of personal 
items. CMAT Program Services coordinates the issuing of lockers to incoming students and 
staff.
Custody of Classified Matter
9. IAW NDSOD, all personnel are responsible for the protection of classified matter that
they have been entrusted. When leaving a desk unattended, 2 CFFTS personnel shall secure all 
classified correspondence. Containers should not be marked externally with the security 
classification of the matter authorized to be stored within. In addition, any attractive item such as 
funds will not be kept in a container that is used to store classified matter.
10. For storage of classified matter, the minimum-security requirements for a PROTECTED 
area are:
a. SECRET or PROTECTED C. an approved filing cabinet with built-in hasp and 
approved combination padlock or an approved filing cabinet equipped with an 
approved locking bar set and approved combination padlock;
b. CONFIDENTIAL or PROTECTED B. an approved filing cabinet equipped with 
an approved locked bar set and an approved padlock; and,
c. RESTRICTED or PROTECTED A. a locked building, room, or container.
Combination Locks
11. The combination of a dial combination lock shall be awarded the same security 
classification as the highest classification of matter that it protects. The Deputy Unit Security 
Officer (D/USO) will ensure that the combinations are changed: (1) when the lock is first taken 

5
into use; (2) at least every six months thereafter; (3) when any person having knowledge of the 
combination is transferred, released or no longer requires access to the contents of the container; 
and (4) if the combination has or may have been compromised.
12. The combination numbers should be randomly selected and committed to memory. The 
combination shall be placed in a sealed envelope available from the Unit Security Officer 
(USecurO). The envelope shall be marked with the appropriate security classification, the 
description and location of the container, the names of persons authorized to have access, and the 
date of combination change. The envelope will then be forwarded to the USecurO.
13. A record of the dates when the combinations were changed shall be placed on the inside 
of the door or top drawer of every combination lock container.
14. To prevent illicit lock substitution, whenever combination or keyed padlocks are 
unlocked to permit entry to containers, they shall be re-locked and placed out of sight in their 
respective containers.
Transmission of Classified Matter
15. All classified matter will be channelled through the 2 CFFTS Admin O, IAW ref, and 
they will maintain a Classified Mail Register to record its movement and internal transfer.
16. Regarding transfer of classified matter within 2 CFFTS, it must be stressed that in 
accordance with NDSOD, if delivery is to be made between buildings and if carriage is other 
than by the consignor's own hand, any level of classified matter will be enclosed within an 
envelope which is gum sealed and additionally sealed with a form DND 709.
17. At any time should there occur a discrepancy, loss, damage or compromise of classified 
matter, it shall immediately be reported to the USecurO or D/USO.
18. Classified waste awaiting destruction shall be afforded protection in the manner 
prescribed for the highest classification of matter contained within the waste. All classified waste 
shall be forwarded in accordance with Ref.
Release of Defence Information
19. Classified information shall not be disclosed to the news media or to the public. 
Unclassified information that is of Canadian origin may be released to the news media or to the 
public only by the WComd or appointed Information Officers. NDSOD should be consulted for 
more detailed information regarding this and related subjects.

6
CHAPTER 4 - FINANCIAL
2 CFFTS Big 2 Fund
1. The Big 2 Fund is established to provide a source of funds from which to pay various 
social and ceremonial expenses incurred by 2 CFFTS. A sub-component of the Big 2 Fund is the 
Gift Fund. Each officer, NCM and Civilian taken on as staff at 2 CFFTS may pay into the Gift 
Fund in accordance with the Big 2 Fund constitution. Those that do not pay into the fund are not 
entitled to Gift Fund benefits.
2. The Big 2 Fund Chairperson, who is appointed by the Commandant, administers the Big 
2 Fund.
3. The Chairperson may authorize non-recurring expenditures to a maximum of $300. The 
Deputy Chairperson may authorize non-recurring expenditures to a maximum of $150.
4. The Chairperson is to direct, on behalf of the Commandant, the Big 2 Entertainment 
Committee. He/she is assisted by the:
a. Deputy Chairperson;
b. Treasurer;
c. Entertainment and Sports; and
d. Flight Reps.
5. The Chairperson shall represent 2 CFFTS at all Wing Fund meetings, and shall consult 
with the Commandant prior to and after each meeting.
6. The Chairperson or the Commandant shall sign all charges to the Big 2 General Fund.

7
CHAPTER 5 - 2 CFFTS AUTOMATIC DATA PROCESSING (ADP) ORDERS
Introduction
Ref: 15 Wing Information Systems Security Orders
1. These orders are published to act as a guideline for all 2 CFFTS personnel involved with 
the operation of DND sponsored ADP equipment and ADP media. Further information and 
regulations regarding the use of DND-sponsored equipment, software, and facilities are 
published at Ref, above. All 2 CFFTS personnel shall be familiar with these Refs. The 2 CFFTS 
ADP orders are designed to complement 15 Wing IS Orders, not supersede them. 
2. This order defines security provisions for 2 CFFTS with the intention of protecting the 
availability, integrity and confidentiality of these systems, and delineates the secondary ADP 
secondary duties for the school.
Responsibility of Users
3. All users of the ADP system in 2 CFFTS must be aware of the inherent dangers 
associated with the security of material when using computers. Users should also be aware, for 
their own protection, of the potential dangers of the Intranet and the Internet. This understanding 
will be confirmed by signing the ADP Security Accountability Form (Annex C). 
4. As a safeguard against the accidental or deliberate compromise of 2 CFFTS systems, all 
Internet users shall read Annex D and comply with all provisions of this order.
Processing of Classified Information 
5. None of the computers connected to either the CAE or the DND GP Net are accredited 
for processing classified information above the “Protected A” classification. Some DWAN 
computers can process “Protected B” information. Higher security information may only be 
processed on stand-alone computers - computers that are not attached to the network.
6. All hardware will be clearly marked to indicate the highest classification of material 
stored on it.
General Security
7. No one shall connect other hardware attachments to the computer system including USB 
drives or other peripherals without permission from the Unit ISSO Officer. Only DND approved 
software and applications created within the framework of such software shall be used on DNDsponsored systems. Users shall strictly adhere to all licence and copyright agreements.
8. Printers present a unique threat due to their portability and memory storage capability. 
Access to printers shall be controlled and access limited to authorized personnel.

8
Operational Security
9. Terminals are not to be left logged on or otherwise enabled when left unattended.
10. Computer System Relocation. DND and CAE systems (other than portables) shall not be 
relocated. If for any reason a computer system needs to be moved to another LAN drop, the 
section ADP representative affected member will contact open a trouble ticket with the 
appropriate agency - (ASSYST for DND, CMAT Support ticket for or CAE) who will action the 
request.
11. When selecting a new location for a computer system, the location shall be IAW Ref and 
any other applicable DND security orders. 
12. To prevent computer viruses on DND/CF owned facilities, users shall scan for viruses 
and other malicious code upon uploading or downloading files from the Internet; and, upon 
receiving or sending Internet e-mail attachments.
13. If a computer virus is suspected or detected, the following steps shall be taken: (1) shut 
the system down; (2) secure and isolate all computer media used on the system; and (3) notify 
the UADPO and the MP Security Section.
14. IF or when work is taken home, the user is required to scan the computer media for 
viruses before using it on a DND-sponsored computer.
Intranet and Internet
15. All users have access to the Internet. However, the Defence Intranet cannot be accessed 
from CAE computers; likewise, the CAE Intranet is only accessible from CAE computers. All 
users are reminded that Internet usage is a privilege that will only be granted if it is exercised in 
accordance with published Internet use policy as presented in Annex C.
16. DND/CAF sponsored Internet connections shall be used only by authorized users, for 
legitimate DND business-related purposes and professional development, and in accordance with 
the procedures covering Internet usage.
17. Users who encounter any Internet threats shall immediately discontinue their session and 
notify the Section ADP Rep, who will in turn notify the Cmdt through the Unit ADP Officer. 
Virtual Meetings
Refs: A. Mr Friesen (15 WTF) / 15 Wing All, DWAN email Protected B Discussion in Virtual 
Meetings, 12 2004L Jan 21
18. PROTECTED B discussions can be held during virtual meetings (MS Teams, Zoom, etc) 
so long as 4 conditions are met. Specifically these conditions are:

9
a. The ProB content is authorized for the audio component of Defence 365 virtual 
meetings only – other content types, such as text chat, files, pictures, and 
recordings at ProB level are not authorized at this time.
b. All participants within the call must be positively identified and confirmed to 
have need to know.
c. The virtual meeting host must remind all participants at the beginning of the call 
that Pro B information may be discussed and that respective surroundings 
conveying appropriate level of privacy must be ensured.
d. The virtual meeting host is responsible to monitor for any changes during the call, 
such as a participant initiating recording or new participants joining, and to 
terminate any active ProB conversations accordingly.
19. There is no requirement for the L1 ISSO to be informed of or to approve individual 
meetings.

10
CHAPTER 6 – POLICY DIRECTIVES
Incoming 2 CFFTS Personnel
1. To provide a service to incoming members of 2 CFFTS, the following policies and 
procedures have been instituted:
a. upon receipt of notification of an incoming staff member, the FIS Flt Comd will 
provide a welcome letter for the Commandant’s signature;
b. the CFI, in conjunction with the applicable Section Head, will assign a staff 
member of 2 CFFTS to sponsor the incoming member;
c. the sponsor will contact the new member and offer assistance as required.
Staff Reporting In
2. All new staff members reporting to 2 CFFTS must:
a. report to the 15 Wing OR to begin in-clearances;
b. meet with the CFI; and,
c. report to FIS to open a training file and to input Flt Pro currencies. New members 
will also be asked to sign an Assignment and Acknowledgment of Accountability 
Form (Annex E), and a 2 CFFTS Personal Relationships Policy form (Annex F).
Annual Medical Examinations
Ref: CFAO 34-44
3. In accordance with Ref, all aircrew personnel are required to have a medical examination 
once a year.
4. The member shall contact 23 Health Services to make appointments at least 1 month
prior to the expiration of their medical.
5. Sections Heads are to maintain a record of the expiry date of Aircrew Medicals within Flt 
Pro for all Aircrew personnel under their command.
Aeromedical Training (AMT)
Refs: A. B-GA-100-001/AA-000 Vol 3 (Flying Orders), Art 100, para 3
B. 1 Cdn Air Div Orders Vol 5, 5-301
C. RCAF FOM

11
6. All 2 CFFTS aircrew shall complete AMT as specified in Refs.
7. The 2 CFFTS Admin O and/or Student Admin O will maintain records and coordinate 
AMT for 2 CFFTS staff by loading aircrew for chamber runs; and, ensuring Standards receives a 
copy of the Record of AMT once received from CFSAT, Winnipeg for placement on Aircrew 
Training File and Flt Pro as per Refs B and C.
Posting Length
8. Normal Posting Length. A posting to 2 CFFTS as a QFI is for three years following 
completion of the Flying Instructors’ Course. Extended breaks due to long absences from flying 
such as deployments, career courses, parental leave or illness will normally extend the length of 
a posting to ensure members receive 36 months of actively flying as a QFI.
9. Internal Postings. Normally, the Unit Flight Safety Officer (UFSO), Course Director 
(CD), and Deputy Operations Officer (D/OpsO) / Operations Officer (OpsO) tours of duty shall 
be for a 12-month period. However, depending upon experience levels at 2 CFFTS, the 
requirements of the School, and the desires of the personnel involved, extensions may be 
granted. QFIs internally posted to FIS can expect their respective tours of duty to be extended to 
permit them to remain at FIS a minimum of two years.
10. [CT155 QFI conversion deleted].
11. Extensions. Depending upon the exigencies of the CAF, the requirements of 2 CFFTS 
and the member’s performance, posting extension requests may be supported by the Cmdt.
12. 431 Sqn. Following 3-years of QFI service or at the Cmdt’s discretion, 2 CFFTS QFIs 
may request that the Cmdt endorse Snowbird try-outs after the call for applicants is issued by 
431 Sqn. Cmdt’s approval will be dependant upon the needs of 2 CFFTS and will include 
assessment of the applicants’ displayed skill, particularly with respect to learning curve and 
situational awareness, NFTC and FIC course loading, QFI staffing, average experience level in 
the Flts, etc.
Instructional Flying – Supplemental / Affiliate QFIs
13. The OpsO and the D/OpsO will normally be affiliated with a specific flight and fly 
instructional / standards missions. Flt Comds will maintain a list of supplemental QFIs. Other 
supplemental QFIs, such as the Cmdt, DCO, CFI, D/CFI, Adj, WComd, COS, 15 OSS Staff, 
UFSO or WFSO will not normally be affiliated with a specific flight nor will they normally be 
assigned a student. These QFIs will fly student missions on an “as required” basis.
Posting Upon Completion of Tour of Duty
14. General. Postings, in terms of community and location, are driven by a number of factors, 
but the primary factor is service needs. The Cmdt, in conjunction with the Career Manager, will 
weigh all factors including the member’s overall performance, skills, aptitudes, and personal 

12
desires to make posting decisions that achieve the best overall balance of the pertinent factors. 
This applies whether the QFI is just completing their first flying tour or is a multi-tour pilot. For 
first-tour pilots, the Cmdt will strive to get them operational flying experience in the community 
of their choice as long as their performance, skills, and aptitudes will lead to a high probability of 
success. For multi-tour pilots, while there may be some pressure from the previous community to 
bring the pilot back, there is no policy guaranteeing this. To ensure the highest probability of 
obtaining the posting of their choice, QFIs are advised to clearly communicate their desires, 
demonstrate keen motivation, and attitude, and exhibit impeccable performance during their QFI 
tour. 
15. Flying vs. Non-Flying Follow-on Posting. Members who have completed three 
consecutive flying postings can anticipate being posted to a non-flying position. Members who 
arrived at 2 CFFTS from a non-flying position will normally be given preference ahead of those 
who arrived from a flying position, for a follow-on flying posting.
16. Fighters. Members who wish to proceed to fighters must have demonstrated the aptitude 
to successfully complete ENJJPT IFF and the 410 OTU, particularly with respect to learning 
curve and situational awareness. Therefore, potential applicants for fighter training must have 
received the minimum marks in Ph II or its previous equivalent course(s) that are required to 
qualify for Ph III fast jet. Applicants must have also displayed above average aptitude on any 
other flying courses such as Ph III ME or RW, the ME or RW OTUs and on the FIC. Applicants 
must submit a package to the Cmdt through their respective Flt Comds and the CFI detailing 
previous flying training history, military flying experience and providing reasons why they are 
motivated to take on the challenge of fighter training. The Cmdt will discuss each application 
with the Career Manager and the 410 Sqn CO. Because of the cost of the required training and 
the demonstrated success rate of former 2 CFFTS QFIs, only applicants who have exhibited a 
steep learning curve, consistently above average situational awareness, and excellent motivation 
shall be considered.
17. ME Conversion. Slots to convert pilots between communities are extremely limited to the 
point where several years may pass without such an opportunity being available to 2 CFFTS 
QFIs. The priority for conversion training is as follows:
a. First Assignment QFIs shall be given priority for conversion training that will 
permit them to join their first operational unit, then
b. pilots who have completed an operational tour but, because of a permanent 
aircrew medical category, cannot return to the type of aircraft previously flown, 
e.g. fast jet pilots who can no longer fly an ejection seat equipped aircraft or RW 
pilots who have a restricted category due to back problems, then
c. members who wish to convert because of personal preferences. Although the use 
of available slots will be limited by timing, priority shall be based upon 
performance displayed at 2 CFFTS, i.e. the better the performer, the better the 
chances of receiving extremely limited conversion slots.

13
Employment While at 2 CFFTS
18. NFTC Students. Phase II and III students will only carry out routine flight duties while on 
course. Between courses or upon course completion they shall be employed as required, at the 
discretion of the Cmdt.
19. FIC Members. Regardless of rank, FIC members shall normally be required to fill school 
positions and carry out special taskings. These may include running the Ops Desk or conducting 
Summary Investigations (CF members only), but will depend upon the individual student’s 
progress, FIS Flt Comd’s recommendation and the Cmdt’s approval.
20. Cease-trained FIC Students. As winged pilots, cease-trained FIC students will normally 
be employed at 2 CFFTS while they await assignment instructions.
Courses and Category Upgrades
21. Instrument Check Pilot (ICP). The ICP course shall normally be limited to pilots who 
have at least two years remaining at 2 CFFTS and who have been identified as individuals who 
shall likely fill supervisory, or standards position in the school. ICP Refresher. ICPs who have 
not flown actively as an ICP for an extended period shall be loaded on the first available ICP 
refresher course. This may occur during the FIC.
22. Career Courses. Courses shall be loaded based upon the requirements of the CF, the 
applicant’s displayed / assessed performance and 2 CFFTS staffing requirements. The limiting 
factor for course loading will normally be course availability.
23. Instructional Category Upgrades. Upgrade opportunity shall be limited solely by the 
QFI’s displayed aptitude and performance; 2 CFFTS shall not limit upgrade work-ups or testing 
based upon the amount of time remaining in a member’s tour of duty, nationality, etc. For 
example, an instructor has been assessed as ready to move from a senior B category to an A but 
only has two months remaining before their COS date. If 2 CFFTS has the resources available, it 
will schedule the QFI for the required workups and check ride.
Leave
Ref: A-PP-005-LVE/AG-001 Canadian Armed Forces Leave Policy Manual Amended 1 Apr 25.
24. 2 CFFTS leave will be conducted IAW Ref. If the CAF Leave Policy is amended and 
contradicts these local orders, the former will apply. All members are required to review ref, 
particularly item 2.1.04, for further clarification.
25. When leave passes are required, they will be processed in Guardian through the Wing OR 
via Monitor Mass. No paper copies of leave pass will be processed.
26. For Annual Leave, the applicant should fill out annual leave passes at least two weeks 
prior to commencement of leave. 

14
27. Leave passes will not be required for staff, FIS Course members and those on the 
ATL/BTL if all the conditions found in section 2.1.04 of ref are satisfied. However, staff, FIS 
course members and ATL/BTL members should advise their supervisors of whereabouts and 
contact information when out of the local area.
28. Annual leave for students will not normally be granted while in ground school. Once 
ground school is finished, students may, if so recommended by the Flight Commander, be 
granted annual leave so as to lengthen a weekend. NOTE: Students shall keep at least 3 or 4 days 
annual leave for Christmas IAW the current 2 CFFTS Annual Leave Policy.
29. CDs can authorize student weekend travel beyond a 250 km radius within the provincial 
borders without a leave pass. Student pilots on course travelling outside of working hours 
beyond the Saskatchewan border require a leave pass signed by the Flt Comd. In any case, 
student pilots on course should advise their supervisor of whereabouts and contact information 
when out of the local area. Requests for student weekend leave which does not include annual, 
short or special leave shall be signed by the CD, and Flt Comd if applicable, and the 2 CFFTS 
OR will stamp the leave pass for the student who should make a photocopy for their CD and 
respective flight.
30. Approval authority for leave passes shall be as follows:

NOTE:
1. Recommending authority is to ensure that, where applicable, the Flt Comd, and CD 
are informed, Ie., Compassionate Leave granted by the Cmdt for a student.
2. As per para 3 above.
31. Annual. All staff members of 2 CFFTS shall use all annual leave; exceptions must be 
approved by the Cmdt. Because the flying program is based upon QFI productivity per flying 
day, whenever 2 CFFTS stands down for leave such as at Christmas, all staff members shall take 
leave – no member shall be permitted to avoid taking leave when the school is closed.
32. Christmas. Dates for Christmas leave will be established by 15 Wing as early as possible 
in the FY.
33. Summer. Reasonable effort will be made to ensure QFIs can take two weeks of 
consecutive leave during the summer to coincide with the elementary / secondary school twomonth break. Leave greater than two weeks must be approved by the Flt Comd.
34. Flt Staffing. Flt Comds shall ensure that they retain sufficient personnel to meet the 
demands of a full flying program. Therefore, summer leave shall be staggered to ensure adequate 
staffing. Flt Comds may “borrow” personnel from other flights. Flt Comds shall also ensure that 
subordinates manage leave in such a manner that the Flt is adequately staffed during Feb and 
Mar to meet the demands of a full flying program.
35. Readiness to Fly upon Return. All QFIs and students returning from leave shall arrange 
travel to ensure that they can fly upon returning to duty, i.e. personnel travelling across numerous 
time zones must allow adequate time to readjust circadian rhythms to be prepared to fly a 
morning mission on the first day back at work. Further, all pilots shall ensure no flying 
qualifications will lapse while on leave. Supervisors may elect to rescind approved leave in such 
cases.
36. Leave Passes normally expire at 2359 hours on the last day of leave (as indicated on 
leave pass) and students shall arrange to be in the local area at that time.
37. 2 CFFTS leave policy for members of courses which finish Phase 2 Training early 
provides the following directives:

16
a. annual leave may be granted; however, students will be required to return for duty 
one week prior to Graduation day;
b. leave passes are to be approved by the 2 CFFTS Adjt; and
c. leave passes will not be signed by the Adjt until the student has returned their 
training equipment (Laptops, software, etc) and publications to the GBTS unless 
they are proceeding onto jet training.
Non-Working Days (NWDs) / Compensated Time Off (CTO)
Refs: A. RCAF FOM
B. Canadian Air Division Orders (CADO)
38. 2 CFFTS baseline-working hours are nine hours per day, five days per week. It is 
recognized that at times the average QFI works more than this number. Although members will 
not normally be compensated for working extended hours, 2 CFFTS will compensate members 
for days worked IAW Refs. The intent of non-working days (NWD/CTO) is to ensure aircrew 
receive adequate time off when required to work in excess of a five-day work week. As such, 
NWD/CTO are not to be accumulated and should be taken within a reasonable timeframe from 
when they were earned. It is important to note that the management of NWD/CTO is delegated 
to the Flt Comd level and they shall ensure adequate staffing levels exist to execute a full flying 
program. As such, supervisors may have to limit or deny NWD/CTO during periods of reduced 
staffing.
39. Weekend Flying. Staff members that weekend fly student pilot (SP)/course member 
(CM) sorties should be compensated for the weekend days worked. 
a. staff members are entitled to a full NWD/CTO if they conduct two or more 
SP/CM missions or staff check rides that day. Additionally, they would be granted 
a full NWD/CTO if all the following occurred:
(1) the staff member was scheduled to fly, but did not fly or only flew one 
SP/CM mission or staff check ride due to bad weather or operational 
reasons; and,
(2) the staff member was required to stay at work for a minimum of 4h prior 
to being released from duty.
b. Staff members are entitled to a half NWD/CTO if they conduct one SP/CM 
mission or staff check ride (i.e. flying during the XCTRY recovery window or 
only planning to fly one SP/CM sortie during weekend flying operations). 
Additionally, they would be granted a half NWD/CTO if all the following 
occurred:

17
(1) the staff member was scheduled to fly, but did not fly due to bad weather 
or operational reasons; and,
(2) the staff member was required to stay at work for a minimum of 2h prior to 
being released from duty.
40. Quarterly Cross-Countries. This will be conducted IAW RCAF FOM. However, the 2 
CFFTS allowance and Cmdt’s intent is for QFIs to conduct one cross-country each quarter. QFIs 
are entitled to one full NWD/CTO following their required quarterly cross-countries. 
NWD/CTOs are at the discretion of supervisors for any cross-countries beyond the minimum 
required.
41. SP/CM Cross-Countries. Staff members are entitled to one full NWD/CTO per 
weekend/statutory holiday day worked. 
42. Airshows. These events are taskings and as such staff members will be compensated 
accordingly. Flt Comds are to consider the specifics of the event on a case-by-case basis to 
determine whether one or two NWD/CTO will be awarded.
43. Duty Travel on Weekends. Staff members who travel on weekends or holidays for CF 
taskings shall be compensated for the days travelled.
44. Pre-loading NWD/CTO. The option of granting NWDs prior to them being earned is an 
option that can be exercised at the Flt Comd level. The intent of this option is to permit greater 
staffing flexibility at the flight level while balancing the interests of both 2 CFFTS and the staff 
member.
45. Unless otherwise approved by the Cmdt, NWD/CTO shall be used in the geographical 
area. Exceptions to this rule shall be supported by the respective Flt Comd and forwarded to the 
Cmdt for approval of a Short day of leave in lieu of the NWD/CTO. Ensure the remarks column 
of the leave pass clearly explains the reason for the request. SPs/CMs will not normally receive 
NWDs during training as compensation for weekend/statutory holiday days worked; however, if 
annual leave will be completely used (not applicable for CMs), and taking NWD/CTO will not 
interfere with training progression, the Cmdt may approve their allocation.
Graduation Parades and Awards Ceremonies
46. Wings Graduations. 2 CFFTS will provide maximum attendance at graduation parades. 
Anyone not flying shall attend the graduation ceremony in the directed order of dress.
Supervisors shall ensure that, if possible, QFIs will arrange briefings and debriefings so that they 
do not conflict with graduation ceremonies.
47. Notification. The SCWO shall notify, via e-mail, all staff and students of Ph III 
graduations details and timings. The 2 CFFTS Mess Entertainment Rep shall ensure Mess 
coordination is accomplished and that the event is adequately advertised, and the Cmdt Secretary 
shall notify WComd EA and 2 CFFTS HCol of Ph II graduation details and timings.

18
48. Phase II Graduation. Phase II graduations/course selections should occur at the Officers’ 
Mess as a portion of a “Special” TGIF. The graduating Ph II CD shall make all arrangements for 
the graduation, including coordinating a suitable date with the Cmdt, acquiring any presentation 
plaques as required. 2 CFFTS staff members who are not directly involved in flying operations 
are strongly encouraged to attend. All students not directly involved in flying operations should
attend.
49. Awards Ceremonies. Periodically, Cmdt 2 CFFTS will present awards to school
members. These ceremonies will make maximum use of bad weather days or other interruptions 
of the flying program to minimize the impact upon sortie generation. Cmdt Administrative 
Assistant shall inform all possible award recipients at the beginning of a vulnerability period to 
permit them to ensure dress uniforms and accoutrements are kept at the workplace.
Sports / Participation in Team Sports / Sporting Events
Refs: A. QR&O sect 4.61(1) Recreation Programs
B. CANFORGEN (as applicable)
50. 2 CFFTS members are expected to maintain a high level of physical fitness and 
participation in team sports is recognized as an accepted part of an overall fitness program, in 
accordance with Refs. 
51. 2 CFFTS members may train or participate in sporting events during the day, but only if 
there is no impact upon sortie generation and only if all an individual’s work-related 
commitments are satisfied. Members may not participate in any activity that will negatively 
impact 2 CFFTS ability to generate sorties unless recommended by a Flt Comd and approved by 
the Cmdt. Flt Comds shall ensure they have sufficient staff and students to run a full flying 
program before recommending a member for participation in any sporting activity, training camp 
or event.
52. Students shall not participate in any event or training if doing so will likely result in the 
requirement to conduct a review mission. Students shall not participate in any event or training if 
they are “sitting on” a test failure or extra dual mission; in cases where students in this situation 
are suspended training, the Cmdt may approve participation.
2 CFFTS Personal Relationship Policy
53. The 2 CFFTS personal relationship policy is attached at Annex F. All personnel – both 
staff and students - shall read and sign the policy indicating that they both understand it and 
agree to abide by it.
Alcoholic Beverages
54. The consumption of alcoholic beverages within BLDG 160 and Hangar line is prohibited.

19
Non-Sanctioned Fund Raising / Sales / Personal Businesses
55. Raffle Tickets / Fund-raising sales. For fund-raising events for clubs, sports teams, etc., 
members may sell raffle tickets in the workplace, but only in a non-intrusive manner and in a 
way that there can be no perception there is any expectation placed upon subordinates. Staff 
members selling raffle tickets or fund-raising items such as chocolate bars shall only do so in 
staff-only areas; staff members shall not conduct fund-raising in student areas. Likewise, staff 
members shall not actively sell tickets or fund-raising items directly to students.
56. Personal Sales. Staff members selling items or services shall not advertise in student 
areas or school common areas. However, they may post advertisements in staff-only areas if 
there is an appropriate information board.
57. Personal Businesses. 2 CFFTS staff members shall not engage in private business 
activity, including enterprises such as “direct” or “layered” marketing, while on duty or while 
within BLDG 160 / flight-line. 
58. Protection of Personal Information. 2 CFFTS personnel shall not provide information 
about any CAF member to any business or agent without the permission of the individual 
concerned. Example: No real estate agent, insurance agent, investment councillor, car dealership, 
etc., should be informed of a member’s posting or financial status without their permission.
Meetings
59. Meetings will be held at the direction and discretion of Cmdt. The Cmdt AA will 
normally attend and take minutes where action items and OPI(s) are identified, for all meetings.
60. Commanders Update Briefing (CUB). The Cmdt shall chair CUB meetings at least 
monthly however, they may be called at any time at the discretion of the Cmdt. The following 
personnel or their delegated representatives shall attend:
Cmdt OpsO A Flt Comd FIS Flt Comd
Cmdt Secretary Adjt B Flt Comd CAE Rep
CFI UFSO C Flt Comd SCWO
CStdsO AdminO D Flt Comd SNRs (As req)
61. Course Aircraft Selection. The Cmdt shall chair course aircraft selection meetings, 
normally to be held for Ph II courses within the last two months of training and for Ph III jet 
courses within the last month of training. The CD for the course undergoing selection will act as 
the secretary for the meeting. The CD shall prepare a presentation and information packages for 
the selection process in which the course members are described, their performance is compared 
and desires are explained. The CD shall prepare a “suggested” selection plot using the best 
available information, making educated extrapolations to predict final course standings, and 
taking student first, second and third choices into consideration. Students selected for Ph III jets 
will normally be selected from the first third of the course and in no case shall be predicted to 
possess marks below the average necessary to pass Ph IV and the fighter OTU. Students selected 

20
for Ph III ME and RW shall be drawn as equitably as possible from each representational third of 
the final course standings. 
62. Course selections shall be treated as confidential until the CD has informed their course 
of the outcome of the meeting. Course selection meeting deliberations shall be treated as 
confidential indefinitely. The following personnel or their delegated representatives shall attend 
the aircraft selection meeting:
Cmdt OpsO A Flt Comd
CD (Current and next Serial) Adjt B Flt Comd
CFI UFSO C Flt Comd
CStdsO AdminO D Flt Comd
NOTE: The Cmdt may authorize observers who have a valid reason for monitoring the selection 
process. Observers will normally not be permitted to monitor more than one selection meeting. 
An example of a valid observer would be an AJAG, the CAS Grievance Coordinator or the Wg 
Surgeon. The Chair shall stress the requirement for confidentiality to all observers. 
63. Cmdt with Senior (Snr) Students. The Cmdt may chair a meeting with the Sr Students 
once per quarter to provide them a forum to raise concerns put forward by the student body that 
are not addressed elsewhere. The purpose of the meeting is to directly provide the Cmdt insight 
into student issues and to provide direct response to the students. The meeting also augments the 
course critique system by permitting students to raise issues that may impact course progress at a 
point that may allow them to be addressed before the course concludes. The following personnel 
or their delegated representatives shall attend:
CMDT CFI
Cmdt Secretary* SCWO
A Flt Snr SP B Flt Snr SP
C Flt Snr SP D Flt Snr SP
64. Cmdt with Female Students / Female Staff Members. The Cmdt should endeavor to have 
a yearly meeting with all female staff and students. The purpose of the meeting is to permit 
female staff and students to raise issues that are not addressed in any other venue. This meeting 
can also be initiated at the request of a female 2CFFTS member by staffing an email or memo to 
the Cmdt.
FORCE Test
65. Each member of 2 CFFTS shall complete the FORCE TEST annually IAW DAOD 5023-
2 – Physical Fitness Program, or any applicable CANFORGEN.
Women in Aviation
66. 2 CFFTS recognizes the challenges faced by women in the CAF, particularly in 
operational roles. Therefore, the school fully supports participation of its female members in the 

21
Defence Women Advisory Organization and encourages its female members to become involved 
in activities that permit them to share their experiences with their peers and promote careers in 
military aviation to potential recruits. 2 CFFTS will normally send one or more female QFI(s) to 
the annual Women in Aviation International conference, and to the Canadian Women in Aviation 
Conference, which is held every two years. The Cmdt shall approve attendance on a per case 
basis. The female QFIs who participate in these activities shall provide a presentation to all 2 
CFFTS female QFIs and female student pilots, summarizing the information learned at the 
conference.
Unfit for Duty / Flying Duties
67. 2 CFFTS staff and students shall present themselves to the 23 Health Services sick parade 
(Medical clinic or flight line) when they are ill. Staff and students are not allowed to un-ground 
themselves from flying. Only a Flight Surgeon may un-ground aircrew. 
Performance and Competency Evaluation (PAcE) Procedures 
Refs: A. PAcE User Guide
B. DLN PAcE training
68. 2 CFFTS Staff as per Refs shall, at a minimum complete the following in the PAcE 
system though Monitor Mass:
a. Job Description
b. Member Aspiration Profile
c. Quarterly Feedback Notes

22
CHAPTER 7 – TERMS OF REFERENCE
Commandant (Cmdt)
Appointed by: Posting
Responsible to: Wing Commander
Responsible for: 2 CFFTS Personnel
Responsibilities:
1. Handle the overall organisation, administration, and management of 2 CFFTS to ensure 
the safe and efficient operation of the NATO Flying Training in Canada (NFTC) program at 15 
Wing Moose Jaw in accordance with 2 Canadian Air Division training policies and directives.
2. Maintain personal pilot proficiency on the CT-156, and hold a current instructor category.
3. Act as Wing Commander, on direction, during Wing Commander's absence.
4. Perform special assignments as detailed by Wing Commander.

23
School Chief Warrant Officer (SCWO)
Appointed by: Posting
Responsible to: 2 CFFTS Commandant (Cmdt)
Responsible for: 2 CFFTS Personnel
Responsibilities:
Serves as the senior Non-Commissioned Member (NCM) advisor to the Cmdt. The SCWO is 
entrusted with overseeing all matters related to dress, deportment, discipline, conduct, and the 
overall quality of life for all 2 CFFTS members. Their role is to foster an environment of 
excellence, professionalism, teamwork, and the war-fighting spirit at all times.
1. Attend and accompany the Cmdt to 2 CFFTS and Wing-level meetings/functions as 
required;
2. Assess and monitor the overall morale and well-being of the unit, taking proactive steps 
to cultivate a positive, supportive atmosphere;
3. Oversee matters impacting the quality of life, providing the Cmdt with informed 
recommendations and strategic guidance for continuous improvement;
4. Monitor staff/student dress, deportment, and conduct, providing the Cmdt with timely 
advice and recommendations to uphold standards of professionalism;
5. Manage and/or oversee disciplinary matters, providing the Cmdt with informed 
recommendations on appropriate actions to ensure fairness and consistency:
6. Oversee and offer guidance on awards presentations, parades, ceremonies, and other 
significant events, ensuring they are conducted with excellence and respect for tradition;
7. Mentor staff/students on a wide range of topics and responsibilities as required, provide 
guidance and support tailored to their individual needs; 
8. Serve as the primary point of contact with the 2 CFFTS Honorary Colonel (HCol), 
facilitating communication and fostering a strong working relationship; 
9. Liaise with the WCWO and other Unit CWOs/SWOs as needed to address and resolve 
issues impacting staff and students, ensuring timely and effective solutions;
10. Act as Wing Chief Warrant Officer (WCWO), on direction, during WCWO's absence; 
and

24
11. Other tasks as directed by Cmdt.

25
Commandant's Administrative Assistant
Appointed by: Civilian Personnel Officer
Responsible to: Commandant
Responsibilities:
1. Performs secretarial services for Cmdt, SCWO, DCO, CFI, CStdsO, FIS Flt Comd and 
Ops O of an organization that conducts pilot training under Phase II and Phase III of NFTC. 
Manages the smooth flow of administrative details for the Flying Training School.
a. types reports, letters, and memoranda from notes or from hand-written drafts and 
transcribes from voice recordings. Processes Progress Review Boards (PRBs);
b. composes, types and signs routine correspondence, and drafts other 
correspondence (civilian) for Cmdt's signature (PRBs, minutes and memos);
c. receives, screens and routes incoming mail ensuring reference material is 
attached, proper format is used, as well as grammatical and typographical 
accuracy, and security classification if applicable and takes followup- action as 
necessary;
d. reads and reviews correspondence and reports requiring Cmdt's signature, ensures 
that responses are in agreement with the inquiries and that grammar, format and 
style are consistent with established standards;
e. researches, summarises, annotates and flags written material and circulation 
correspondence of interest to the Cmdt, SCWO, DCO, CFI, and Flt Comds;
f. takes minutes of meetings and arranges for distribution of copies as necessary;
g. receives and screens telephone calls and visitors in terms of importance and 
urgency of calls, and answers general inquiries. Relays messages to staff officers. 
In absence of supervisor, directs emergency calls to appropriate senior officers. 
Has direct contact with other Wing, Squadron Commanders, Command and 
NDHQ staff officers as well as civilian agencies;
h. acts as receptionist to Cmdt. Co-ordinates appointments and interviews, maintains 
appointment register. Hosts visitors to the Cmdt's office, co-ordinating lunch 
and/or coffee as required; and
i. amends, updates and controls all publications, orders, regulations, manuals and 
periodicals held in Cmdt's office.

26
2. Provides direct assistance to the Cmdt, SCWO, DCO and CFI in the performance of their 
duties:
a. assembles and compiles background data, supporting documentation and 
reference material in response to requests or for meetings and conferences;
b. maintains thorough knowledge of regulations, orders, procedures and guidelines 
relevant to flying training;
c. assembles and integrates responses from Flt Comds on common inquiries or 
obtains necessary information from other sources on Base;
d. co-ordinates Branch administrative activities as required by Cmdt and prepares 
consolidated flying school cyclical reports and returns; and
e. establishes schedules of appointments with Flt Comds, Wing staff and visitors 
from NDHQ and Air Command Headquarters.
3. Is familiar with the operation and use of various office machines.
4. Performs related duties:
a. maintains subject matter files, makes transportation and accommodation 
reservations as required, completes forms, requisitions supplies, maintains and 
updates personnel data, registers correspondence and performs other clerical 
duties.

27
Deputy Commanding Officer (DCO)
Appointed by: Commandant
Responsible to: Commandant
Responsible for: A, B, C, D and FIS Flights 
Responsibilities:
1. Act as Cmdt 2 CFFTS on direction, during Cmdt's absence.
2. Supervision of the flying program to ensure efficient safe operation of the Flights in 
accordance with current directives.
3. Liaise with Flt Comds with respect to staff, CM and student career administration.
4. Maintain awareness of student and staff progression; coordinate with CFI to ensure unit 
staffing and experience meet instructor cadre requirements.
5. Liaise with Career Managers to coordinate incoming and outgoing staff.
6. Maintain personal pilot proficiency on the CT-156 and hold a current instructor rating.
7. Review and revise 2 CFFTS Orders as required. (Producing and distributing to be 
completed by BI CDM).
8. Perform various projects as assigned by the Cmdt.

28
Chief Flying Instructor (CFI) 
Appointed by: Commandant
Responsible to: Commandant
Responsible for: Staff and Student Training 
Responsibilities:
1. Liaise with Flt Comds with respect to staff, CM and student flying administration to 
ensure the smooth and efficient flow of student pilots through the course.
2. Coordinate staff training plan to ensure the unit maintains an effective instructor cadre.
3. Monitor student progress and take appropriate action when progress is unsatisfactory.
4. Monitor instructor proficiency and category upgrades and take appropriate action when 
progress is unsatisfactory.
5. Monitor and edit all student PRBs and CF377s.
6. Maintain personal pilot proficiency on the CT-156 and hold a current instructor rating.
7. Perform various projects as assigned by the Cmdt.

29
Flying Instructor School Flt Comd (FIS Flt Comd)
Appointed by: Commandant
Responsible to: Chief Flying Instructor
Responsible for: Flying Instructor School
Responsibilities:
1. Act as Cmdt 2 CFFTS on direction, during Cmdt's absence.
2. Oversee the administration, supervision and efficient operation of the FIS in accordance 
with 2 CFFTS policy and 1 CAD Orders.
3. Maintain continuous liaison with AOT SET to ensure standardisation of instruction 
throughout 2 CFFTS. Develop, evaluate and implement policy and program changes in 
conjunction with AOT SET.
4. Liaise with the CFI, CStdsO, and Flt Comds to ensure standardisation and continuity of 
training as per ITP/FIG.
5. Monitor, record and report to the Cmdt through the CFI on the efficiency, effectiveness, 
and standardisation of aircrew and instructor training.
6. Monitor publication amendments to ensure staff and students are kept abreast of all 
changes.
7. Prepare PARs for officers employed in the FIS.
8. Maintain personal flying proficiency on the CT-156, and an instructor category.
9. Conduct Flying Instructor Courses to produce QFIs for 2 CFFTS.
10. Perform special tasks or studies as directed by CFI, Cmdt or DCO.

30
Chief Standards Officer (CStdsO)
Appointed by: Commandant
Responsible to: Commandant
Responsible for: Harvard II Standards
Responsibilities:
1. Act as Cmdt 2 CFFTS on direction, during absence of Cmdt 2 CFFTS.
2. Maintain overall responsibility for flying training design and quality control at 2 CFFTS.
3. Maintain liaison with AOT SET to ensure standardisation of instruction and testing 
throughout 2 CFFTS. Develop, evaluate, and implement policy and program changes in 
conjunction with AOT SET.
4. Liaise with the CFI and Flt Comds to ensure standardisation and continuity of the training 
plans.
5. Monitor, record and report to the Cmdt on the efficiency, effectiveness and 
standardisation of aircrew and instructor training.
6. Monitor GBTS to ensure the efficiency, effectiveness and standardisation of FTD and 
academic instruction.
7. Liaise with FTD and Academic supervisors on day-to-day quality assurance issues.
8. Monitor publication amendments to ensure staff and students are kept abreast of all 
changes.
9. Prepare PARs for officers employed in the Standards organisation.
10. Recommend awards and raise/renew instructor categories.
11. Test students in accordance with the course ITPs and maintain testing records.
12. Conduct and report to the Cmdt on the annual/semi-annual internal audit.
13. Prepare and revise applicable Standards SOPs and Instructional Category Upgrade 
Program.
14. Maintain flying proficiency on the CT-156, and an instructor category.

31
15. Assume the duties of Supervisor of Flying (SOF) when required as per 2 CFFTS Orders.
16. Perform special tasks or studies as directed by Cmdt.

Flight Commander (Flt Comd)
Appointed by: Commandant
Responsible to: Chief Flying Instructor (CFI)
Responsible for: All personnel assigned to the Flight
Responsibilities:
1. Handle the administration, supervision, and efficient, safe operation of the Flight in 
accordance with current directives.
2. Ensure the optimum utilisation of students, instructors and flying time assigned to the 
Flight.
3. Ensure a high calibre of standardised instruction is given by the Flight, advising Course 
Directors of unsatisfactory student progress.
4. Liaise with the CFI, FIS Flt Comd and GBTS FTD scheduler to ensure effective training 
continuity.
5. Liaise with Course Directors and the officer incharge- of Officer Development to ensure 
flight line support of the OD program.
6. Actively monitor student progress and act as necessary.
7. Continue training of staff as instructors, pilots and officers.
8. Prepare PAcE items and PARs on staff.
9. Sign log books for flight staff and students.
10. Ensure the proficiency and standardisation of assigned QFIs, including the induction of 
new staff (e.g. arrival briefing).
11. Assume the duties as Supervisor of Flying (SOF) when required as per 2 CFFTS Orders.
12. Participate on parades as required.
13. Maintain personal flying proficiency on the CT-156 and an instructor category.

procedures.
6. Liaise with CAE with regard to aircraft resources to meet the 2 CFFTS flying program.
7. Compile statistical reports and returns as required by the Cmdt.
8. Liaise with Air Force Training at 2 Cdn Air Div to coordinate course loadings at 2 
CFFTS, 3 CFFTS, 419 Sqn and OTUs.
9. Co-ordinate/develop the 2 CFFTS Bad Weather program 
10. Maintain the Aircrew Information File (AIF).
11. Recommend for approval, cross-country missions IAW current directives.
12. Act as Supervisor of Flying (SOF) or co-ordinate appointing a designated supervisor 
while 2 CFFTS is conducting programmed night flying.
13. Maintain personal flying proficiency on the CT-156, and an instructor category.

35
Supervisor of Flying (SOF)
Appointed by: Commandant 
Responsible to: Commandant
Responsibilities:
1. Act as the Cmdt’s representative and maintain overall OPCON of 2 CFFTS flying 
operations when the Cmdt, CFI, DCO and Ops O are not present on the Wing or during any 
periods, as directed by the Cmdt. 
2. Must be physically present on the Wing (not flying), and in possession of the SOF cell 
phone at all times during the SOF shift.
3. Must have detailed knowledge of the 2CFFTS Flying Program matrix, and provide 
assistance to the Duty Ops O as follows:
a. provide guidance on significant program changes;
b. authorize weather check as required;
c. maintain good awareness on actual and forecast weather; and
d. authorize a “cease flying” program when actual and forecast weather indicates no 
possibility for flying ops.
4. Must have a working knowledge of 15 Wg and 2 CFFTS ERPs (including Chap 5, Annex 
T - Cmdt 2CFFTS Checklist) and SOF checklist (see para 6).
5. In the event of an incident or accident where the CP is activated, proceed to the
Command Post (BLDG 160) and call the Duty Ops Desk (Hangar 4) and assist the Duty Ops O. 
6. Assistance to the Duty Ops O during an Emergency Response should include:
a. general oversight and coordination with the Duty Ops O, as per the 2CFFTS 
AERP;
b. coordinate with the Duty Ops O to determine Search High/Search Low aircraft 
with tower, based on proximity, fuel on board and experience of aircrew;
c. coordinate and determine search aircraft replacement aircrew, considering 
previous SAR pilots and experience;
d. advise ATC (Tower) not to launch any aircraft UFN;

36
e. determine if flying operations shall cease and course of action wrt 
recalls/diversions as required, depending on the nature of the events, to maintain 
the highest level of flight safety able;
f. obtain tail number and names of personnel involved;
g. establish communication with Cmdt, DCO, CFI and Ops O;
h. notify applicable Flt Comd unless otherwise done by Cmdt; and
i. gather all pertinent information to assist the Flight Safety team with their 
investigation.
7. If the Ops O or D/Ops O is present, they would normally report to the Command Post to 
relieve the SOF. In this case, the SOF should report to the Duty Ops Desk until the released by 
Ops O, D/Ops O or Cmdt.

37
Statistics Management Officer
Appointed by: Commandant
Responsible to: Ops O
Responsibilities:
1. Develop and manage Flight Pro templates in conjunction with IP Centric scheduling. 
Ensure all items in flight pro are loaded at least 1 month in advance, including all required Flt 
Pro tracking elements such as sticky notes.
2. Maintain the School Performance Statistics:
a. maintain the School daily stats database and reports;
b. update the database with syllabus changes;
c. provide weekly summaries of the School’s progression;
3. Provide assistance to the Cmdt, DCO, CFI and Ops O with analysis and presentation of 
data.
4. Ensure that the Flt Comds have access to the required data/info to manage their flights.
5. Complete other duties and tasks as assigned by the Cmdt, DCO, OpsO, D/Ops O.

38
Unit Flight Safety Officer (UFSO)
Appointed by: Commandant
Responsible to: Commandant
Responsibilities:
1. Advise the Cmdt on all pertinent Flight Safety matters.
2. Co-ordinate and monitor, through Flight FSOs, Wing FSO and CAE safety 
representatives, the 2 CFFTS Flight Safety Program.
3. Ensure all unit personnel are aware of and comply with the Wing Flight Safety Program 
and pertinent requirements of B-GA-135.
4. Monitor all flying and ground operations, including support personnel activities, and 
report all unsafe or hazardous activities, actual or potential, via the safety reporting system.
5. Track statistics and provide analysis on local and external Flight Safety activities/trends.
6. Organise meetings IAW B-GA-135 Chap 3, for Flight FSOs, not less than once per 
quarter, to discuss policy and procedures.
7. Be prepared to brief significant Flight Safety matters during mass QB or bad weather 
briefings.
8. Attend Flight Safety Meetings as directed.
9. Attend quarterback meetings.
10. Be prepared to perform the duties of and/or provide assistance to the WFSO when 
required.
11. Respond as required by the Wing Emergency Response Plan (not eligible to carry out 
D/Ops O position).
12. Maintain personal flying proficiency on the CT-156, and hold a current instructor rating.

39
Adjutant

Responsible for: OJT, CT and VW Student Pilots
Responsibilities:
1. Standardize student administration and discipline amongst flights in conjunction with 
CFI, Flt Comds and CDs;

3. Coordinate with 2 CAD AOT SET and 2 CFFTS Ops provide loading data for Phase 3

5. Coordinate completion of Crse Reports (CF377) with applicable CD;
6. Monitor PD&C in conjunction with CDs;
7. Maintain CD Handbook and Student Standing Orders;
8. Coordinate administration of PAR exemptions for student pilots not on course; and
9. Perform various projects as assigned by Cmdt.

40
Administration Officer (Admin O)
Appointed by: Posting
Responsible to: Commandant
Responsibilities:
1. Supervision of 2 CFFTS Administration:
a. ensuring standardization of 2 CFFTS personnel and administrative services;
b. supervising maintenance of 2 CFFTS administration and personnel records;
c. monitoring 2 CFFTS clerical services; and
d. distribution of administrative workload as the situation dictates.
2. Provision of administrative assistance to 2 CFFTS personnel:
a. providing administrative assistance, advising and research for the 2 CFFTS Cmdt, 
staff and students;
b. originating and drafting administrative correspondence for the Cmdt's signature;

Qualified Flight Instructor Mentor (QFIM)
Appointed by: Flt Comd
Responsible to: Flt Comd
Responsible for: Development of C CAT Instructor.
_______________________________________________________________
INTENT: Mentoring a junior QFI is an important part in senior QFI development as well as 
officer development. As QFIs graduate FIS, they tend to have questions regarding orders, 
proficiency, instruction, and Flight operations. An appointed senior QFI, as the primary contact,
enables a venue in which the C CAT can ask questions and prosper as a QFI while at 2 CFFTS. 
QFI Mentors will hold an instructional category of B or higher.
RESPONSIBILITIES:
1. Conduct an initial interview. Explain the mentoring plan with the C Cat QFI to include 
planned meetings on a weekly basis minimum. Explain the PH II/III syllabus in general and as it 
applies to their primary role.
2. Monitor and assist in writing Prog cards in FlightPro to meet the 2 CFFTS Orders Annex 
V required standard.
3. Ensure a high degree of knowledge of Orders and Flight SOPs.
4. Ensure MUT/C Cat trg flights are scheduled IAW 2 CFFTS C Cat program for the C Cat 
QFI to ensure the C Cat achieves the required trg prior to their upgrade.
5. Explain the details/requirements for SUP Checks and Check Rides. Ensure the C Cat QFI 
coordinates with the Trg O and Scheduler to proactively plan both their upgrade to B Cat and 
conduct IRT/APC as required. Detail and discuss the information contained in the “C” Category 
Information Book (Annex G).
6. Monitor electronic Prog Books of the assigned C Cat QFI’s students. Ensure they have 
reviewed them accordingly and made the appropriate weekly sign-offs. This is to include, but not 
limited to, ensuring that Pre-Solo Emergencies and Sequences are completed and that they are 
progressing through the TP at an appropriate pace so as to meet the next block of trg.
7. Assimilate the junior QFI into the Flight esprit de corps by briefing them on existing 
traditions and social etiquette.

61
BOOK 2 – FLYING ORDERS
CHAPTER 1 – PRE-FLIGHT REQUIREMENTS
Flying Clothing Insignia, Crests, Shirts
Refs: A. RCAF FOM
B. Canadian Air Division Orders (CADO) 
1. All 2 CFFTS personnel involved in flying operations will wear flying clothing and 
crests/patches IAW Ref. Exchange Officers and foreign military pilots on 2 CFFTS staff may 
wear specialist crests recognised by their Service.
Aviation Life Support Equipment (ALSE)
2. Helmet and Mask. ALSE and Flying Clothing shall be stowed in the appropriate areas 
(equipment room) and maintained in accordance with the CMAT ALSE section instructions and 
applicable orders in the RCAF FOM. 
NOTE: ALSE personnel have noted undue wear on mask fittings due to the mask being 
left hanging from one side of the helmet when stored in the ALSE lockers. Therefore, all 
aircrew are to fasten the mask with both bayonet fittings prior to placing the helmet in 
their lockers between flights. All ALSE equipment will be inspected prior to each flight 
in accordance with appropriate inspection procedures.
Harvard II Anti-G Suit
3. All Harvard II aircrew and passengers equipped with an anti-g suit shall wear the 
equipment IAW these orders while conducting flying operations. Aircrew are to review the antig suit PowerPoint located at \\Hercules\SCommon\Standards\G SUIT. Aircrew are required to 
complete an egress trainer with the anti-g suit. All student solo missions require both g-suit and 
AGSM brief by CFEME to fly. If asymmetric inflation is experienced airborne, g-suit is U/S. 
No high g maneuvers are authorized, and g-suit must be inspected and refit by ALSE Tech. If 
refit does not rectify issue, g-suit shall be replaced.
4. Aircrew who are not equipped with the anti-g suit may be authorized to take part in flight 
operations with the following provisions:
a. Harvard II Phase II & III Students:
(1) Dual Missions - The absence of anti-g suit is accounted for as part of the 
brief and included in the MALA;
(2) Solo CH8A and NA SB - May proceed solo but must have AGSM brief as 
a minimum; and

62
(3) All other Solo Missions - Super Solo ONLY (unless centrifuge training or 
AGSM brief was completed and equipped with g-suit).
b. Harvard II FIS Course Members and QFI:
(1) Dual Missions - The absence of anti-g suit is accounted for as part of the 
brief and included in the MALA; and
(2) Solo Missions - No restrictions if personnel completed centrifuge training. 
Those without centrifuge training will be limited to missions with no 
intent to exceed 3 "G".
5. Extreme Cold Weather Restrictions (Less than -35C):
a. ACE Cold Weather Overalls are required by FOM Annex 4.2.2.4.A (Lined Bib 
Pants) and are not authorized for use with the anti-g suit. 2 CFFTS flying 
operations are authorized without the anti-g suit by the exceptions outlined below:
(1) When forecast temperatures are colder than -35C within the following 
24hr period, operations shall be limited to flying missions with no intent to 
exceed 3"G"; and
(2) Winter Cross-Country missions with no intent to exceed 3"G" may 
substitute anti-g suits for ACE Cold Weather Overalls.
Flying Clothing/ALSE Reporting of Loss or Theft
6. In the event that aircrew equipment is lost or stolen from locations away from 15 Wing 
Moose Jaw, aircrew shall: (1) immediately contact Airport Security and local police giving a 
description of the equipment and the approximate time of theft; (2) notify the nearest Military 
Police Unit; and (3) upon return to Moose Jaw, carry out actions specified in para, below.
7. In the event of loss or theft of flying clothing/equipment at 15 Wing Moose Jaw, aircrew 
shall: (1) immediately report loss/theft directly to the Military Police giving full particulars –
students are also to verbally report loss/theft to the Course Director who will report the loss to 
the applicable CMAT section and assist wherever possible in recovering missing items; and (2) if 
the items have not been recovered following a two week period, a memorandum detailing the 
circumstances of the loss/theft and action taken is to be forwarded through the applicable 
supervisor to the Cmdt.
2 CFFTS Fatigue Risk Management System (FRMS)
Ref: A. FOM 2.1.3 - Pt 3 - Fatigue Risk Management System (FRMS)
8. IAW above Ref, the 2 CFFTS FRMS will incorporate the following:

63
 
a. Education: FRMS training will be the responsibility of the HPMA Coordinators 
and conducted in conjunction with HPMA training.
b. Scheduling: Unless authorized by the Flight Comd, the following best practices 
shall be adopted: (1) no scheduling from last wave the previous day to first wave 
the following day; and (2) no scheduling first wave and last wave in the same day.
c. Sleep Quality: Monitored by individual aircrew.
d. Workplace/Mission Design: No considerations.
e. Alertness Maintenance: In conjunction with the Mission Acceptance Launch 
Authority (MALA), all aircrew shall complete a Fatigue Assessment Report 
(FAR) at the commencement of the Pre-flight Briefing.
 
(1) If the fatigue risk is low, no action required.
(2) If the fatigue risk is moderate:
 
(a) The aircrew shall attempt to mitigate through fatigue risk 
countermeasures to a low fatigue risk, report "On Schedule - FAR" 
to the Scheduler and continue the mission.
(b) If unable to mitigate to a low fatigue risk, the MALA approval 
authority is Aircraft Commander (AC) and the AC is willing to 
accept the additional risk, report "On Schedule - FAR" to the 
Scheduler and continue the mission.
(c) If the MALA approval authority is AC and the AC is unwilling to 
accept the additional FAR risk, report "Change - FAR" to the 
Scheduler and do not fly. The Scheduler will attempt to mitigate.
 
(3) If the fatigue risk is high, the aircrew shall not fly. Report "Change - FAR" 
to the Scheduler. The Scheduler will attempt to mitigate.
 
f. Reporting and Feedback: To ensure FAR data is captured, Schedulers are to 
assign FAR codes in FlightPro as follows:
 
(1) When so informed, apply the "On Schedule - FAR" code to the mission 
strip.
(2) If the mitigation required assigning different personnel or changing the 
mission, apply the "Change - FAR" code to the mission strip.

64
(3) If unable to mitigate and the mission cannot be flown, apply the "Cancel -
FAR" code to the mission strip.
9. For the FRMS culture to flourish, it is IMPERATIVE that aircrew report responsibly to 
ensure accurate data is provided on aircrew fatigue levels. Long working hours, and poor sleep 
quality/quantity must be accurately quantified if substantive changes to working conditions are to 
be developed. There shall be no stigma attached to modifying the schedule to accommodate high 
fatigue levels.
Publications Required Reading
10. In addition to the requirements of the Wing Flying Orders, aircrew will read applicable 
Pubs IAW any applicable higher-level flying orders, such as the RCAF FOM.
Aircrew Information File (AIF) / Ops Notes / Standards Bulletins
11. All aircrew shall ensure that the latest AIF, OPS Notes and Standards Bulletins have been 
read and acknowledged in FlightPro prior to flying.
Checklist and Maps - Local Area
12. All pilots shall have an appropriate checklist readily available when operating aircraft.
13. Each pilot and student shall carry a local area map showing all the restricted areas as 
depicted in 15 Wing Flying Orders.
Pre-Flight Weight and Balance Requirements
14. Aircrew shall ensure prior to any flying mission that weight and balance requirements are 
met as per the applicable AOI/flight manual and RCAF FOM. This will allow crews to confirm that 
the boarding weights of the crews do not exceed the ejection seat limits, and that the aircraft is 
within weight and balance limits.
15. All Harvard crews will use the scale located in Harvard Ops to determine their boarding 
weight. The boarding weight includes all ALSE equipment (harness, leg lines, helmet, LPSV, Gsuit), flying clothing for the conditions of the day, and all miscellaneous items on their person 
while in the cockpit (kneeboard, pubs, checklists etc.). In addition, any baggage that will be 
stored in the luggage compartment in the Harvard will also be weighed. The aircraft captain will 
ensure that the weight and balance calculations are completed using Weight and Balance 
function found in ForeFlight for the aircraft they are assigned. The aircraft captain will verify 
that the aircraft weight and balance condition is safe for flight and will save the information prior 
to completing aircraft acceptance. Review Ops Note 2502 for instructions.
CT156 Refueling

65
16. Standard practice at 15 Wing will be to fuel CT156 aircraft to 1000 lbs. Crews shall plan 
missions for 1000 lbs of fuel. After each sortie, crew will confirm the pounds of fuel remaining 
with ground crew so that an appropriate amount of fuel is added.
17. CT156 crews at 15 Wing may request up to 1100 lbs of fuel if required by their missions, 
provided they confirm weight and balance limits are not exceeded. To request, crews shall 
contact the dispatch desk as early as practicable (day prior for 1st wave), after which:
a. The applicable missions on FlightPro shall be 'Sticky Noted' indicating the 1100 
lb fuel requirement; 
b. The dispatch desk will ensure aircraft fueled to 1100 lbs are assigned 
appropriately; and
c. Missions canceled with aircraft fueled to 1100 lbs will not be de-fueled. These 
aircraft will be assigned in future waves and crews notified to confirm weight and 
balance (refer to para b).
18. When away from 15 Wing Moose Jaw, crews can expect approximately 1100 lbs of fuel 
from single point pressure refueling and 1200 lbs from gravity refueling. In either case, prior to 
fueling crews must calculate weight and balance and, if weight is fuel limited, communicate to 
the FBO the appropriate amount of fuel to add in litres (Canada) or gallons (USA) to stay within 
limits.
Photography on the Flight line
19. Before taking photographs on the flight-line or in hangars, personnel must:
a. obtain permission from CMAT Air Maintenance Officer and 2 CFFTS Ops O;
b. advise Tower if proceeding onto the flight-line; and,
c. advise MPs if Tower is closed.
Cameras and Personal Electronic Devices (PED)
20. The use of music devices in 2 CFFTS aircraft is prohibited.
21. The use of a camera in 2 CFFTS aircraft is prohibited during solo missions. As per the 
Canadian Forces Flying Orders (B-GA-100-001/AA-00) and 15 Wing Flying Orders, the Aircraft 
Captain can authorize the use of a camera during dual missions. However, the camera shall be
independently secured for take-off and landing.
22. Video recorders or cameras shall not be attached to personal ALSE. Care should be taken 
as to the location of any video or camera devices particularly near the canopy det chord.

66
FOD in the Cockpit
23. Only issued yellow pens are permitted on board all 2 CFFTS aircraft.
24. All aircrew shall do a personal inventory check prior to entering and after exiting the 
cockpit to minimize FOD occurrences.
25. With the implementation of issued pubs bags, water bottles and iPads all aircrew are 
cautioned to be aware of the accidental actuation of aircraft switches or jamming of 
seats/canopy. Crews are to be aware of the stowage of personal inventory during pre-flight, 
flight and post flight activities.
Hearing Protection
26. While on the flight line, all personnel shall wear hearing protection while aircraft are 
running. 
CHAPTER 2 – SCHEDULING AND ACCOUNTABILITY
Schedule Approval, Flight Authorization and Accountability 
1. Only those Flights and tasks required in support of 2CFFTS training mission will be 
approved. Once approved, a Designated Authorization Officer will authorise specific missions. 
The aircraft captain will then acknowledge accountability in carrying out the mission as 
authorised. All 2 CFFTS Staff shall read and sign the Assignment and Acknowledgement of 
Accountability form (Annex E), before operating aircraft at the unit.
2. Authorization of Daily Schedule. The daily schedule will be prepared in the FlightPro, by 
a scheduler and approved by the applicable Flt Comd or designate. When required, Flight Ops O
may make short notice changes to Flight schedules to ensure an efficient operation. Final daily 
schedule should be ready by 1500 and will be reviewed by the Cmdt. 
3. Authorization and Approved Missions. All student training, staff continuation training 
(SCT), post wings training and support missions are approved subject to the Flights being 
conducted IAW these orders, all other applicable orders, with due regard for Flight safety, and 
adhering to the flying schedule as depicted in FlightPro. The table below delineates the level of 
authorization designated by the Cmdt for each mission type.

4. Cross Country Flights. The Section/Flt Comd, 2 CFFTS Admin O, and the 2 CFFTS Ops 
O or delegate is the recommendation authority, and the Cmdt or designate is the approval 
authority for all cross-country flight requests. Deviations from the plan based on weather may be 
conducted without prior approval; however, they should inform the chain of command at the 
earliest convenience. All deviations other than weather must get at a minimum, verbal 
authorization Ops O or Duty Ops O or SOF.
5. Out-and-Backs.
a. Phase 2/3/FIS CM: Same day and RON Out-and-Back missions (CYXE, CYQR)
are recommended by Flt Comd and approved by Cmdt. Due to volume of 
submissions, approval of syllabus away missions is delegated to Ops O & D/Ops
