deviation should not be authorized; and 
(3) Potential delays in completing the corresponding airborne missions. FTD 
“expiry” rules IAW the ITP still apply. If the corresponding airborne 
mission(s) will not likely be completed before the FTD expires, the FTD 
should not normally be completed. 
c. If the out-of-sequence FTD is “Unsatisfactory”, the failed sequence(s) shall be 
debriefed accordingly and the progress card shall be assigned an “Unsatisfactory” 
overall score. The FTD instructor will then initiate an “Unsatisfactory Out-ofSequence FTD” workflow. Instead of commencing remedial training, however, 
the airborne sortie that was skipped shall be flown, and provided the outcome is 
satisfactory, the “Unsatisfactory” out-of-sequence FTD is to be re-flown and 
graded normally.
35. If an FTD is rated "Unsatisfactory" the following points shall apply:
a. a progress card shall be completed;
b. the student shall be debriefed by the FTD instructor;
c. the student’s Flt Comd shall be advised of the unsatisfactory performance by the 
GBTS supervisor;
d. the student shall enter the "Unsatisfactory" mission rating in the prog book;
e. the mission will be repeated a maximum of two times in the FTD, normally with 
the student’s QFI instructing, prior to flying the corresponding trip in the aircraft;
f. if any additional FTD mission results in another “Unsatisfactory” rating, the Cmdt 
or CFI is to be advised and a Senior Instructor is to fly the corresponding trip in 
the aircraft;
g. if the airborne trip is rated as “Marginal” or higher, the student will continue with 
the syllabus; and,
h. if the airborne trip results in an “Unsatisfactory” assessment, an Independent 
Review will be ordered by the Cmdt or CFI.

144
36. To accommodate staff and student FTD requirements, it has become necessary to 
establish rules governing student practice in the FTD. Accordingly, the following rules will 
apply to practice FTD sessions:
a. all sessions are limited to published hours of operation and will be scheduled by 
the schedulers, who shall ensure an equitable distribution;
b. student “solo” practice FTD missions are not permitted until CH6S and FTD 
IF10S (Block 1 FTDs Ph II) have been completed.. Prior to this point, and only 
after successfully completing CPT 3, students may use their respective FTD as a 
Cockpit Procedures Trainer only. Thereafter, student practice FTD missions are 
normally limited to a maximum of one per seven calendar day period... After 
completing IF12, students are allowed one additional practice FTD per week until 
the BIT is complete. These limitations can be lifted by the Cmdt on a case-bycase basis;
c. unauthorized civilians are not to be brought into the FTD;
d. students are to wear appropriate flying clothing,ALSE, and strap in completely;
e. Students are to use the FTD for training related to the course, only practising start 
and ground ops and sequences which have been previously taught and assessed on 
syllabus flying missions with a QFI;
f. practice FTDs are usually limited to one 1.2 hour session per person; 
g. normal restrictions to flying after an FTD apply to all FTD missions, including 
practice FTDs;
h. in the interest of student development, practice FTDs while a student is 
undergoing remedial training shall only be permitted with the approval of the 
Flight Commander or designate on a case-by-case basis. If authorization to 
conduct a practice FTD is granted, the student shall not practice the unsatisfactory 
sequence(s) for which they are receiving remedial training until the unsatisfactory 
sequence(s) have been upgraded in either the FTD or during airborne training.
i. students must comply with any other restriction that may be imposed by Student 
Standing Orders; and 
j. failure to comply with 2 CFFTS FTD policy will result in disciplinary actions and 
loss of privileges;
37. Egress training is not to be conducted in the FTD for NFTC aircraft (CT156). 
Instructor Continuity

145
38. All QFIs are monitored through Sup Checks and Category Check Rides, at an interval 
respective of their category, to ensure the standardized application of the instruction model as set 
forth in the Flight Instructor Handbook (FIH) and as such, variance within instruction is limited. 
Therefore, instructor continuity will be subordinate to flying continuity (Normal Progression) 
and will only be prioritized under the following circumstances:
39. Pre-Solo: Students will be designated a primary and secondary instructor to complete 
CH1 through CH7 and IF1 through IF7. In cases where the primary or secondary instructor is not 
available, the Flt Comd, D/Flt Comd or a Senior QFI specifically designated by the Flt Comd 
may fly the mission.
40. Students Experiencing Difficulties: When students experiencing difficulties have been 
identified through a formal process such as an IR/PRB, or when the Flt Comd deems necessary, 
their instructor continuity will be managed through a Instructor Continuity Assistance Program 
(ICAP) until such time that they can return to normal progression.
41. The ICAP will allow supervisors to provide mentorship and appropriate supervision of 
the progression of student training for those experiencing difficulties. The intent of the program 
will be to provide assistance for the duration required to a defined end state within one phase of 
training (e.g. within one CH training block or completion of IIT). Training in other phases the 
student is currently enrolled in will utilize normal progression. ICAP in more than one phase 
must be approved by the Cmdt/CFI. Three QFIs will be designated with one requested by the 
student and two recommended by the Flt Comd ensuring a collaborative approach. This process 
will be documented by the relevant form located in Annex G and a copy will be attached to the 
student’s training file. Upon the successful completion of the prescribed period, students will 
return to normal progression. Extension of an ICAP is permitted but must be documented using 
an additional Annex G stating the revised end state. While on an ICAP, student progress cards 
will state flight was conducted under ICAP.
42. The flights shall schedule at least a B Cat instructor to fly the last dual trip before any test 
or confirmation ride and therefore deviation from the ICAP is authorized in the performance of 
these sorties as required.
Student Testing
43. GBTS instructors will normally conduct all student Emergency FTD (EFTD) missions; 
however, at least a B Cat QFI may be used if GBTS instructors are unavailable. A subsequent 
remedial EFTD mission will be conducted with a senior GBTS Instructor and monitored by a 
Standards Officer.
44. Both Open and Closed book exams are required to be completed by Student Pilots prior 
to flying their FCHT and FIT tests as follows: 
a. The Open Book AOI exams will be written by Student Pilots and graded by 
Standards. The Closed Book AOIs (Emergency Operation Procedures) will be 

146
arranged by Flt Comd or delegate, and administered and graded by Standards at a 
mutually agreeable time.
b. Both Open Book (Instrument and Met) and closed book IRT exams will be 
administered, invigilated and corrected by the Flight ICP or delegated ICP. A 
Standards ICP may assist in this role if there is no Flight ICP or delegate 
available. Once corrected, the Flt Comd or delegate will return all three exam 
cover sheets to STDS for input into the SP’s training file.
45. Student Flight Tests. All student tests shall be co-ordinated through Standards. Tests shall 
normally be flown with a Standards Officer or a designated Standards affiliate.
46. Flight Test Briefings. Flts should ensure that the student is ready for the Standards 
Officer’s pre-flight briefing at least 60 minutes prior to the scheduled take-off time.
47. Flight Test Debriefings. The student’s primary instructor should normally attend the 
Standards Officer’s test debriefings. Additional personnel may attend as required.
48. Improperly Taught Sequences. If at anytime during a test or check ride the Testing 
Officer determines that the student’s poor performance on any of the elements being evaluated 
resulted from improper teaching, the following procedure shall be followed:
a. if the errors are minor in nature and can be corrected by briefing the student and 
instructor, the testing officer shall brief the student and the instructor on the 
correct procedure and allocate the required grade for that sequence.
b. if the errors are sufficiently serious to require further dual missions to overcome 
the incorrect instruction, the testing officer shall inform the CStdsO on the 
particulars of the situation and in turn, the CStdsO will recommend a re-test as the
course of action. These recommendations shall be forwarded to the Cmdt for 
approval.
49. The Cmdt and CFI are to be informed of all cases of incorrect instruction requiring action 
and of the corrective action taken.
50. All unsatisfactory tests shall first be debriefed with the Flt Comd or their designate. Any 
disagreement with the final assessment will be referred to the CStdsO.
51. If a discrepancy arises at any time during a test debrief, the debrief will be immediately 
discontinued and the student dismissed until the discrepancy is resolved.
Supervised Solo
52. During periods of prolonged bad weather, it may be necessary to authorize instructors to 
fly with students on syllabus solo missions to ensure continuity and achievement of course
graduation dates. The following conditions shall be observed when authorizing supervised solo:

147
a. only the Flt Comd, CFI or Cmdt may authorize a supervised solo;
b. a supervised solo will be flown after a gearup approach if the next syllabus trip is 
a solo or if the student's scheduled third event of the day must be a solo;
c. Instruction shall not to be given during a supervised solo. The aircraft captain 
should remain silent for the duration of the flight with the exception of responding 
to checks. However, the QFI remains the aircraft captain, and is expected to 
exercise the usual judgement with regard to safety of the aircraft and crew. If it is 
deemed necessary, the QFI should not hesitate to verbally guide the student or 
take control as required to rectify the situation. Possible significant events that 
might require QFI intervention include traffic conflicts, near ancillary overspeeds,
or actual emergencies. After such an event, the QFI should take a moment to 
clarify what has just happened, but this is not an opportunity for instruction. If the 
QFI is required to intervene during a supervised solo, a comment will be made in 
the student's progress card for the mission in question, however, grades are not to 
be assigned for individual items on supervised solo progress cards. The sequence 
involved shall be performed and assessed on the following dual mission prior to 
the student's next solo mission. A supervised solo mission shall not be stopped 
early due to poor student performance unless the QFI deems that there is a serious 
risk of repeated unsafe incidents.
d. When logging a supervised solo, the time is to be entered as solo effective time in 
flight pro, however the student will enter it as dual time in their logbook. The QFI 
will enter the time as first pilot, instructional.
Review Missions 
Ref: 1 Can Air Div Orders 5-216 and 5-217
53. General. For the purposes of this order, a review mission shall be defined as a mission 
given to a student/FIS CM to reduce skill fade and bring the student’s proficiency up to an 
appropriate proficiency level (minimum level required) after an extended break in flying training 
of up to and including 45 days. Ideally the student/CM will achieve their previously attained 
levels however this is not a requirement of the review mission. A review missions should focus 
on the primary phase of flights however may be composite in nature at the discretion of the Flt 
Comd. A maximum of one review FTD mission may be conducted prior to any airborne review 
mission at the discretion of the Flt Comd. Additional FTD review missions require Cmdt or CFI 
approval. Progress cards with sequences flown and levels obtained are to be completed in 
FlightPro for all review sorties. The overall grade assigned will be “NG”, but a statement as to 
whether the sortie was successful or not will be included in the Overall Comments section. The 
Flt Comd will annotate the appropriate workflow in FlightPro indicating the reason the review 
was authorized.

148
54. Guidelines. Prior to recommending and/or authorizing review missions, Flt Comds will 
conduct a thorough review of all other factors including student ability, course time available, 
syllabus position, and continuity in phase. Any abnormalities in these areas will require 
consultation with the CFI or Cmdt. If a student/CM is entitled to a non-course time review IAW 
the below criteria, the Flt Comd will authorize one non-course time review. Unless 
circumstances exist that potentially warrant additional reviews, the student/CM will proceed to 
the next syllabus mission regardless of outcome of the review sortie. 
55. The Flt Comd has the authority to authorise one Review Mission IAW the criteria stated 
below. Any additional requirements for Review Missions will be requested and justified to the 
CFI/Cmdt via a FlightPro workflow. If further Review Missions are required, the CFI/Cmdt may 
authorize one additional Review Mission. To ensure consistency within the School, the following 
guidelines shall be followed:
a. Seven (7) Calendar Day Layoff Prior to a Test (within the flying phase). The Flt 
Comd may authorize a non-course time review mission on the eighth day. This 
review is intended to ensure that all students have the same opportunity to retain 
currency in phase, immediately prior to Phase II and Phase III flying tests;
b. Ten (10) Calendar Day Layoff. The Flt Comd may authorise a Review Mission if 
a student has not conducted training (flying or FTD) in 10 calendar days (21 for 
CMs). The Review Mission may be conducted in either the FTD or aircraft as 
appropriate to the student’s situation. The Cmdt may authorize additional noncourse time review missions;
c. Twenty (20) Calendar Day Training Layoff in any One Flying Phase. The Flt 
Comd may authorise a Review Mission if a student has not conducted training 
(flying or FTD) in 20 Calendar days in a phase. Extended layoff from flying or 
FTD training in a given phase of training is defined as greater than 20 days for 
students and greater than 30 days for CMs. The Review Mission may be 
conducted in either the FTD or aircraft as appropriate to the student’s situation. 
The Cmdt may authorize additional non-course time review missions; and,
d. Christmas Leave Period. On return from the annual Christmas leave period, 
students may fly up to two Review Missions. The first mission should concentrate 
on the primary phase of training; however, both Review Missions may be 
composite in nature at the discretion of the Flt Comd. If the student is 
demonstrating the required performance after the first Review Mission, they 
should proceed with the next syllabus trip. The FTD may be used as one of the 
Review Missions if the Flt Comd feels it is appropriate.
e. In order to preserve the integrity and validation of the training plan, flights shall 
not deliberately avoid scheduling a student or CM from continued training in 
order to entitle them to a review mission. Students and CMs are expected to retain 
previously taught material within the boundaries of these time frames.

149
CHAPTER 5 - FLYING INSTRUCTOR COURSE & CONVERSION COURSE 
TRAINING
Course Reports
Ref: Flying Instructors Course & Hawk/Harvard Aircrew TS, Chapter 3
1. The FIS Flt Comd shall ensure a course report (form CF377) is completed for each 
course member upon completion of training on the FIC or Conversion course. The Cmdt will 
sign the course report, which will be forwarded through the CFI for signature. Course reports are 
to be distributed to addressees as expeditiously as possible after the course completion date. 
FIC/Conversion Course Commencement
2. FIS shall notify appropriate CMAT agencies at least two weeks prior to commencement 
of formal FIC and Conversion courses. Specifically, FIS shall forward pertinent course member 
details, specific aircraft/course details, publication and computer support requirements, ALSE 
requirements, and any additional administrative requirements.
Post Wings Graduate (Course Member (CM)) Crew Duty Day
3. A Course Member’s (CM) crew duty shall not exceed 12 consecutive hours
4. If a CM experiences simulator sickness or disorientation in the FTD, they will be 
restricted from flying for a period of 12 hours after using the FTD, including practise FTDs.
5. CMs may be given up to three missions per flying training day during the conversion 
course or during Phases I & II of the FIC course CMs shall normally be scheduled for one 
mission per day when on Phase III of the FIC, a second mission may be scheduled at the 
discretion of the FIS Flt Comd.
Clearhood Phase
6. Prior to flying the Captaincy check, CMs must have completed the Emergency FTD, the 
open book AOI exam, and have written the applicable aircraft Red Page Emergencies.
EFTD Phase
7. FIS CMs may be authorized to conduct additional EFTDs if experiencing significant 
delays. At no time will a FIS CM exceed 90 days without an EFTD. If the amount of EFTDs 
required exceeds that of those currently in the ITP/FlightPro, EFTDRE’s are to be created on the 
final existing EFTD in the syllabus. 
FIS Staff

150
8. FIS staff members shall be selected from 2 CFFTS or have previous 2 CFFTS 
experience. QFIs selected to be FIS staff members will normally be assigned to FIS for a 
minimum of two years, regardless of current time at 2 CFFTS to date. Ph I FIC will be conducted 
by B Sup or higher or B Cat QFIs approved by the Cmdt.
Academic Assessment
9. When a course member demonstrates unsatisfactory academic progress, the D/Flt Comd 
shall advise the FIS Flt Comd and CFI who will convene a PRB IAW Flying Instructors Course 
and Conversion Course TS, Chap 3. The PRB shall make its recommendations to the Cmdt. 
Extra Dual
10. Remedial training will be conducted IAW the ITP. NOTE: Due to the unique nature of 
FIC training, the duration and conduct of the ED (i.e. focussing solely on problem areas and/or 
completing other review items) is at the discretion of the FIS Flt Comd. The CM may continue 
flying in other phases prior to meeting the required proficiency level on an ED with the FIS Flt 
Comd’s approval.
11. If the CM fails to demonstrate the required levels of performance at the completion of the 
EDs, the FIS Flt Comd in consultation with the CFI shall determine the course of action. The 
possible outcomes are authorization to fly one more ED (if there are extenuating circumstances 
which warrant further training and course time is available); or convene a PRB. Should the CM 
fail to correct the deficiencies by the end of the additional ED, a PRB shall be convened.
Course Member Flying Training – Unsatisfactory Progress
Ref: Flying Instructors Course & Harvard Aircrew ITP, Chapter 3
12. Any unsatisfactory progress must be entered properly in the CM’s prog book and 
FlightPro course comments. The respective D/Flt Comd shall inform the FIS Flt Comd if a CM 
displays unsatisfactory flying progress.
13. The FIS Flt Comd has been delegated the authority to task an instructor to conduct an 
independent review. Based on the results of this review, the FIS Flt Comd shall either return the 
candidate to course with remedial training allocated, or convene a PRB on behalf of the Cmdt.
Initial Instructional Rating – Form T-21 Guidelines
14. The FIS Flt Comd shall sign paragraph 4.A. of the form T-21 for all initial category 
attempts. The initial category will normally be a C Category; however, based upon previous 
instructional experience, the FIC CM may be recommended for a higher category. Note: The FIS 
Flt Comd has been delegated with the authority to sign on behalf of the Cmdt under paragraph 
4.C. of the form T-21 on all initial category rides.

151
15. In all instances, the front page of the form T-21 will be filled out and signed in its entirety 
prior to the CM attempting the Category Ride. The completed front page shall be kept on file at 
FIS until the ride is complete.
16. The T-21 administration shall be completed in MESH. Prior to the CAT ride, the 
workflow should be directed to the CStdsO who will then forward to the appropriate AOT SET 
pilot. Upon completion of the ride, SSO AOT signature will be obtained using MESH and 
returned to the CStdsO for inclusion in the member’s aircrew file. 

152
CHAPTER 6 - STAFF REQUIREMENTS, UNIT CHECK OUT, AND UPGRADES
Introduction
1. IAW RCAF FOM, AOT SET will conduct all initial Instructor Category Rides and all A 
Category Upgrades, as well as all APC and Category Rides on FIS and Standards pilots. 
Standards will co-ordinate all other tests and proficiency rides as required by the RCAF FOM. 
This section establishes common standards and procedures for the conduct of all staff rides. 
Regulations governing the categorization and assessment of flying instructors are contained in 
the RCAF FOM. While scheduling is coordinated through Standards, each pilot shall monitor 
their own expiry date to ensure qualifications do not expire. Standards Officers and/or delegates 
should complete staff write-ups within 24 hrs from completion of the mission.
Progress Card Administration
2. Annex V adapts the guidance and procedures for progress card administration previously 
described in MJSPC-1 (28 Nov 12) and replaces that document. Further amplification and 
direction for tests and SCT is contained in the Standards SOPs. 
Category Tests
3. Category rides done by AOT SET require prior coordination. Category rides done by 
Standards are normally scheduled one day in advance of the daily flying schedule but can be 
scheduled further in advance wherever practical. Before attempting the category upgrade or 
renewal, the candidate must ensure that the T-21 Form is completed and submitted to the CStdsO 
through MESH, and that the Category exam is complete. This process can be found at Annex P.
Annual Proficiency Check
4. Annual Proficiency Check currency dates are based on the FIC Solo Check (ensure that 
the Staff Ride workflow is utilized so that the Aircrew file will be updated); or the FCHT (First 
Assignment instructors).
5. Pilots are required to complete the AOI Open and Closed Book exams prior to renewing 
their APC. IAW RCAF FOM these may be completed up to four months before the APC expiry 
date, but no later than one day prior to the scheduled APC flight.
6. In order to maximize training events, Flights shall strive to use the dedicated Formation 
Lead as an opportunity to conduct staff training (i.e. B Cat Trg, Sup Check, etc.) when 
appropriate.
Currency Extensions
7. Currency extensions (prior to expiry) are preferrable to currency waivers (after expiry). 
The CStdsO is to submit requests for extensions to the Cmdt (info CFI) seven days prior to 
qualification expiring. Flt Comds are to inform the CStdsO eight days prior to member's 

153
qualification expiring. The Standards Trg O is available to assist members in preparing extension 
requests.
Instrument Rating Ticket
8. Annual IRT check rides are flown IAW RCAF FOM. ICPs shall administer the CF456 
and CF457 using MESH, as described in Annex P. 
Supervisory Training Flights
9. Supervisory Trainer flights (Sup Checks) shall normally be scheduled one day in advance 
on the daily flying schedule. Ideally, QFIs should endeavour to obtain a scenario for their 
Supervisory check flight up to one week in advance but no later than one day prior to being 
scheduled. Sup trainers are to be carried out as follows:
a. B Cat – within 30-days of every three months of your category anniversary date 
(i.e., if your CAT was flown on 19 Apr, you would fly your Sup Trg within 30-
days of 19 July, 19 Oct, 19 Jan); and,
b. C Cat – every 42 days.
10. Sup trainers will be carried out by designated supervisors, Standards Officers, FIS 
instructors, or other QFIs assigned by the CStdsO. C Category instructors shall conduct sup 
trainers with a Standards Officer or FIS instructor. B Category instructors may alternate sup 
checks between their Flight/affiliated Flight and Standards/FIS. The Flt Comd, D/Flt Comd, or 
Trg O will normally conduct sup trainers within their own flights. Flt Comds who wish to 
conduct a sup trainer normally assigned to Standards/FIS (e.g., C Category sup trainers) may 
coordinate with the CStdsO to do so. Operations staff may complete their “in-Flight” sup trainers 
with their affiliated or the Ops O. The supervising QFI must have a category equal to or greater 
than the QFI under supervision unless specifically authorized by the Cmdt. Should a Flt sup 
trainer expire, this mission will be conducted by Standards. All due dates are based on the issue 
date of the category held. Expiry of the sup trainer will result in the temporary suspension of 
instructional duties until the currency is renewed. Only under exceptional circumstances will the
CStdsO authorize instructional duties to continue. The renewal of this currency shall be the QFIs 
highest priority.
11. Flt Comds, and the 2 CFFTS Ops O should have their “in-Flight” sup trainers completed 
by the CFI providing the provisions of para 2 above are met. If the conditions of para 2 are not 
met, the CFI may designate an alternate with the concurrence of the CStdsO.
Harvard Staff Critical Sequence Training (CST)
12. Critical Sequence Training (CST) aims to expose Harvard staff and course members to 
the handling characteristics of the aircraft in less common yet critical regimes of flight. This 
training aims to equip staff with the experience needed to form sound personal limits. The design 

154
and maintenance of Critical Sequence Training is the responsibility of the CStdsO, who will 
coordinate with FIS regarding any updates. Airborne CST training may be delivered by 
Standards or FIS instructors, as assigned by their supervisor.
13. The delivery of critical sequence training (CST) for Harvard staff and course members 
will proceed in the following manner: 
a. FIS Course Members will conduct an airborne CST sortie, with an emphasis on 
prevention and personal limits.
b. Harvard staff do not conduct an annual CST airborne sortie. However, annual 
exposure to high attitude, low energy handling, and oil system emergencies is 
conducted in an EFTD.
c. Supervisory Training sorties for Phase 2 QFIs should include ongoing exposure to 
max performance and advanced handling manoeuvres wherever practical.
FIS Graduate Flight Checkout
14. A formal checkout is normally not required following Flying Instructor Course (FIC) 
graduation. However, Flt Comds are to ensure that any new arrival is briefed on all operating 
procedures pertaining to daily flight operations.
15. For an extended lapse between completion of the FIC and the start of instructional duties 
at 2 CFFTS, FIC graduates will undergo refresher training as recommended by the Flt Comd and 
approved by the CFI.
Staff Emergency Training
Ref: A. RCAF FOM 3.7.2.3. 
B. FSIMS 179746
16. The practice, training or evaluation of aircraft emergencies shall not take place in CT-156 
aircraft. This directive will not prohibit the maneuvering of the aircraft in those ways an actual 
emergency would dictate and will allow for maneuvers described in the SMM, such as Forced 
Landing Patterns, and Flapless approaches, to be conducted. The Flight Training Devices shall 
be used to exploit emergency training to the highest extent possible.
17. CT-156 Harvard QFIs shall complete EFTD training quarterly. If unable to meet the 
EFTD requirement by the end of a quarter, the QFI will revert to U/T status and the CStdsO shall 
be advised. EFTD missions shall be scheduled specifically in FlightPro and shall be conducted 
by a GBTS instructor. A Flight Pro mission card shall be completed with a staff Workflow 
attached and a copy shall be kept on the individual’s aircrew file. The Overall Remarks comment 
of the EFTD mission card shall be copied and saved in the Workflow Header. The APC will not 
be renewed without an EFTD having been completed within the timeframe as detailed in the 
FOM.

155
18. Harvard EFTD training should include oil pressure emergency handling scenarios. In 
addition, a minimum of one quarterly Harvard EFTD shall include low energy/high attitude 
unusual attitude scenarios combined with CHIP Light and/or oil system malfunctions.
19. EFTD missions shall be a minimum of 1.2 in length to be considered complete and shall 
contribute to the requirement to complete a minimum of 4.8 hours of FTD training each year.
PFL Flying Requirements
20. In addition to the RCAF FOM flying requirements, all QFIs shall complete a Practice 
Forced Landing (PFL) on a regular basis dependant upon instructional category. C Category 
QFIs shall complete at a minimum one PFL every 42 days. B Category QFIs shall complete at a 
minimum one PFL every 3 months. QFI who are unable to meet this requirement will only be 
restricted from instructing the expired sequence. PFL currency will be reinstated once the 
sequence is completed with a current QFI. Currency may only be reset in the aircraft.
Staff Continuation Flying
21. QFIs are mandated to maintain flying proficiency in all phases and disciplines IAW 
RCAF FOM. To accomplish this goal, SCT guidelines directed by the Cmdt are outlined below 
and will be revised as required. Supervisors are responsible to ensure that these guidelines are 
followed. These hours include cross-country, supervisory trainer, check flights (IRT/APC/Cat 
Ride), as well as category upgrade program flying, famil flights, and weather checks.
22. Pilots qualified on both 2 CFFTS aircraft type shall meet the proficiency flying 
requirements outlines above for the primary aircraft type and 50% of the requirement for the 
secondary type.
23. Staff are required to fly a minimum of 0.6 hours local hands on in each phase per quarter, 
excluding night. This time is based upon an average two pilot sortie of 1.2 hours flying time. The 
time allocated to each phase can be conducted in one dedicated mission or may be spread out 
over the course of multiple composite missions.
24. Further to the above, staff will be required to dedicate further time to hone their skills on 
local proficiency missions. This requirement will be based upon the instructor’s category as 
follows:
a. A Category 0.6 hrs (hands on time);
b. B Category 1.2 hrs (hands on time); or
c. C Category 1.8 hrs (hands on time)
25. The remaining time within an individual’s proficiency time allocation will be used to 
maintain night and instrument approach currencies. Any further time can be allocated to IFR 

156
cross-country/out and back missions. It will be considered acceptable to allocate minimum phase 
times to cross-country/out and back missions where appropriate (i.e. IF or NAV minimums). 
Any restrictions imposed by Operations with respect to maximum number of cross-country/out 
and back flying shall still be observed.
26. The primary tool for monitoring the training directive will be FlightPro. All aircrew will 
be required to break down their mission time allocations into FlightPro when signing in at the 
Operations desk. Mission appropriate codes will be added to FlightPro that will reflect what an 
individual did during a particular mission (e.g. Prof-SCT-CH for clearhood proficiency time, 
Prof-SCT-FM for formation proficiency time, etc). Multiple codes can be assigned in any 
mission and will reflect the time that was dedicated to a given task. Flt TrgOs shall keep an 
electronic or paper record of the quarterly Mut-tracker file for up to one year.
27. CStdsO shall notify the Cmdt if a QFI fails to meet quarterly proficiency requirements. In 
the next quarter the QFI must complete sufficient local proficiency missions to account for the 
deficiency in the previous quarter. These missions will not count towards the requirements of the 
new quarter. The Cmdt may waive this requirement under extenuating circumstances.
28. Solo SCT may only be approved by the Cmdt, CFI, 2 CFFTS Ops O or Flt Cmdr. Note: 
The 2 CFFTS SCT plan has been approved by CFS on 16 May 2012, IAW 1 Cdn Air Div 5-506.
Phase 2 and Phase 3 Instructors
29. Harvard QFIs each hold either an A, B, or C Instructional Category IAW RCAF FOM. 
Furthermore, Harvard QFIs are appointed by the Cmdt to teach Phase 2 or Phase 3. These 
designations are recorded as qualifications in FlightPro. 
30. Phase 2 QFIs are responsible to deliver the Phase 2 syllabus and the Phase 3 Instrument 
Flying syllabus. Phase 3 QFIs are responsible to deliver both the Phase 2 syllabus and the Phase 
3 syllabus. Phase 3 QFIs may also teach FIS conversion and rear seat sorties.
31. All QFIs are responsible to perform syllabus sequences that they teach to a Level 4. 
Phase 2 QFIs are responsible to perform the following core sequences (beyond the Phase 2 
syllabus) to a Level 3:
a. Flapless final turn, AOA approach, AOA landing, PFLs (Area and Pattern);
b. Uncontrolled aerodrome operations;
c. Formation station keeping to 60 AOB;
d. Formation departures and arrivals, including Wing Take-Off and Wing Landing.
32. Phase 2 QFIs may qualify to practice additional Phase 3 syllabus sequences solo. To 
qualify, a Phase 3 QFI must certify the staff member as safe for solo on that sequence during a 
Supervisory Trainer, C CAT Trainer, B CAT Trainer, or during FIS syllabus training. The 

157
certification “safe to practice ___ solo” shall be indicated on the mission card in the overall 
comments and recorded in the staff member’s Category Information Book (Annex G). 
33. Phase 2 QFIs are not required to seek certification to practice additional sequences solo. 
However, once certified, they are responsible to perform these additional sequences to a Level 3 
on any future APC or Supervisory Trainer. 
34. Phase 2 QFIs may complete the ELUG program and obtain the Element Lead 
qualification provided that they can meet the proficiency and HPMA standard for that upgrade.
Phase 2 QFIs may also be appointed Senior Instructor (B+) or Supervisory Instructor (BSup) as 
required.
35. Appointments to Phase 3 QFI are granted by the Cmdt based on the staff member’s 
performance, anticipate progression, and the needs of the NFTC program. Staff should not be 
restricted from progressing in instructional category if the needs of the program do not permit 
appointment to also teach Phase 3. 
36. The upgrade process from PH2 QFI to PH3 QFI is detailed in Annex X - Phase 3 
Upgrade. Completion of this program does not reset or change the instructional category of the 
candidate.
C Category Instructors
37. Each Flt Comd is responsible for their Flight’s “C” Category Upgrade Program. It is 
administered by the Flt Trg O and will be monitored, as required, by Standards. Refer to the 
Instructor Category Information Book at Annex G for detailed information on C Category 
administration. All C Cat training missions will be scheduled on FlightPro with the associated 
progress card attached with a Flt Comd workflow. C Cat training missions shall be conducted by 
A Cat or B Cats designated by the Cmdt.
B Category Instructors
38. Flt Comds are responsible for monitoring the development of their B Cat QFIs. Training 
for B Cat QFIs will be scheduled as required to aid with progression and preparation for 
Category rides etc. All B Cat training missions will be scheduled in FlightPro with the associated 
progress card attached with a Flt Comd workflow. B Cat training mission shall be conducted by 
A Cat or B Cats Designated by the Cmdt.
Element Lead Upgrade (ELUG) Program
39. General. Flt Comds shall nominate appropriate candidates for upgrade to Element Lead at 
the 2 CFFTS supervisors’ meetings after consultation with the CStdsO (FIS Course Members 
included). Nominations are restricted to B Category QFIs or above unless previous flying 
experience demonstrates that candidates are proficient with formation leading before holding a B 
Category (FIS Course Members included). Under normal circumstances, QFIs shall begin the 

158
ELUG inside 12 months after upgrading to B CAT. The requirement expected during the ELUG 
training syllabus is included in Annex Q. All candidates shall be trained as per this syllabus. 
Remedial training shall be limited to one extra mission. Deviation from this syllabus requires 
Cmdt approval. The responsibility for managing the ELUG Upgrade Program rests with 
Standards, and is found at Annex Q. A Staff Ride workflow shall be used following all 
successful ELUG check rides so that the members Aircrew file can be updated. 
40. Designated senior QFIs holding an ELUG qualification will be selected by their Flight 
Commander to instruct work ups. Check ride will be flown with standards or designate (for FIS 
Course Members).
41. To allow for exposure in the Hvd the QFI being upgraded will sit in the front seat for the 
first and third flight and in the rear seat for the second flight and the check flight.
42. Prior to the ELUG ride, the check pilot will present a mission plan to the candidate 
outlining all pertinent details of the mission. As a minimum, this outline will include the type of 
mission profile, any simulated weather restrictions, specific departure details, 
manoeuvres/formations to be covered in the area and any specific RTB instructions.
43. Mission. The pilot supervising the upgrade will act as Mission Comd and fly in the lead 
aircraft with the QFI to be upgraded. The upgrade mission will normally be conducted as 
follows:
a. Brief. The two-plane brief is to be performed by the QFI seeking upgrade and 
should follow current 2 CFFTS SOPs. The briefing is to cover all aspects of the 
mission from sign-out to sign-in. In addition to the pre-briefed scenario, the check 
pilot will indicate to the upgrading QFI any desired manoeuvres or mission 
specifics that they wishes to assess.
b. Mission. The mission will, with the exception of emergency scenarios, be flown 
as per the briefing. The QFI seeking upgrade will, aside from the pre-planned 
mission format, be expected to maintain control of the formation in abnormal or 
emergency situations. A general outline of a normal upgrade is as follows:
(1) take-off and departure;
(2) climb to altitude for a workout in the area. All sequences of the Area 
Work (reference Annex Q) are testable.
(3) Formation Split and Rendez-Vous;
(4) Simulated approach to another destination
(5) Recovery to base, including one go-around in the pattern.

159
c. Debrief. Will be performed by the two-plane lead candidate followed by the 
Mission Comd who will comment on the overall mission effectiveness.
44. Report. The pilot supervising the upgrade shall:
a. Upon completion of the formation debrief, the Mission Comd will privately 
debrief the two-plane lead candidate as to their performance during the mission. 
The effectiveness of the mission will be based on proficiency, knowledge, HPMA
and leadership. A prog card with staff workflow will be completed for the 
candidate's training file, and an entry made in the newly upgraded lead’s logbook; 
and
b. Should the upgrade be unsuccessful, there will be a 3 month training period 
before the next check ride. If the second check ride is unsuccessful, there will be a 
Category Review chaired by the CFI. 
Section Lead Formation Upgrade (SLUG) and Certification of Leads
45. General. Flt Comds shall nominate appropriate candidates for initial selection of fourplane lead candidates at the 2 CFFTS supervisors’ meetings after consultation with the CStdsO. 
Candidates shall be selected based on Flight requirements and candidates’ previous experience 
(amount and type). Selected candidates’ names will then be forwarded to the Cmdt for approval 
prior to commencing upgrade training or certification. The four-plane training program will be 
tailored to meet individual needs. A proposed four-plane lead program along with minimum 
requirements is included in Annex R. Four-plane upgrade missions will be scheduled from 
FlightPro Hvd Staff Prog. The associated card will be completed by the supervising pilot. A 
Final Upgrade mission will be coordinated through Standards and will be carried out by a 
designated Standards or Affiliates four-plane formation check pilot. Flt Comds should ensure 
that upgrade candidates are familiar with the minimum requirements and the conduct of the 
upgrade mission.
46. The designated check pilot will carry out a thorough review of the candidate’s mission 
cards on FlightPro. Upon completion of this review, the check pilot will present a mission plan to 
the candidate outlining all pertinent details of the subsequent upgrade mission. As a minimum, 
this outline will include the type of mission profile, any simulated weather restrictions, specific 
departure details, manoeuvres/formations to be covered in the area and any specific RTB 
instructions.
47. Mission. The pilot supervising the upgrade will act as Mission Comd and fly in the lead 
aircraft with the QFI to be upgraded. The upgrade mission will normally be conducted as 
follows:
a. Brief. The four-plane brief is to be performed by the QFI seeking upgrade and 
should follow current 2 CFFTS SOPs. The briefing is to cover all aspects of the 
mission from sign-out to sign-in. In addition to the pre-briefed scenario, the check 

160
pilot will indicate to the upgrading QFI any desired manoeuvres or mission 
specifics that they wishes to assess.
b. Mission. The mission will, with the exception of emergency scenarios, be flown 
as per the briefing. The QFI seeking upgrade will, aside from the pre-planned 
mission format, be expected to maintain control of the formation in abnormal or 
emergency situations. A general outline of a normal upgrade is as follows:
(1) take-off and join-up;
(2) climb to altitude for a workout in the area. The workout should include all 
the standard formation manoeuvres;
(3) an element split to beyond visual range;
(4) rendezvous; and,
(5) recovery via a gear-up low approach followed by an overhead break and 
landing. If the conditions of the day require element recoveries, the 
requirement for a gear-up low approach and overhead circuit may be 
waived.
c. Debrief. Will be performed by the four-plane lead candidate followed by the 
Mission Comd who will comment on the overall mission effectiveness.
48. Report: Upon completion of the formation debrief, the Mission Comd will privately 
debrief the four-plane lead candidate as to their performance during the mission. The 
effectiveness of the mission will be based on proficiency, knowledge, airmanship and leadership. 
Four-plane leads will be expected to have displayed a strong ability to lead and effectively 
manage the formation throughout the full range of four-ship operations. A prog card with staff 
workflow will be completed for the candidate's training file, and an entry made in the newly 
upgraded lead's logbook.
Harvard Tactical Navigation Instructor Program
49. General. Standards is responsible for coordinating the Tactical Navigation (Tac Nav) 
training within 2CFFTS for all QFIs. All candidates considered for Tac Nav training (including 
FIS CMs) shall be nominated at the weekly supervisor’s meeting after consultation with the 
CStdsO. Candidates will receive a Tac Nav Wingman qualification following the Proficiency 
Phase and a Tac Nav Instructor qualification following successful completion of the Instructional 
Phase (Annex R).
50. The Tac Nav Wingman qualification may be considered for any FIS CM or C category 
instructor. Harvard Ph 3 graduates will be granted this qualification following the completion of 
their Tac Nav phase. The overall emphasis and intent of this qualification is to retain experience 
levels for development of Tac Nav Instructors. A Tac Nav Instructor must authorize and be 

161
present in any Tac Nav formation. A Tac Nav Wingman may be designated as the Tac Lead at 
the discretion of the Tac Nav Instructor, but as the authorizing Element Lead, the Tac Nav 
Instructor retains responsibility for the conduct of the formation. The normative progression for 
any instructor would be ELUG and/or Tac Nav Wingman as first qualification. Once both those 
qualifications have been granted, Tac Nav Instructor may be sought.
51. Prerequisites for the instructional upgrade are subject to the candidate having 
successfully completed the ELUG program, Tac Nav Phase 1, must hold a minimum of a B 
category instructor qualification; or, in exceptional circumstances where the above criteria has 
not been met, a candidate who has demonstrated the ability and/or possesses the required 
previous experience may be approved for training by the Cmdt through the CStdsO.
52. The upgrade program will occur in two phases, each of which will be coordinated 
through the CStdsO and will be carried out by designated Tac Nav Sup-on-Sup qualified QFIs. 
Check rides will be carried out by Standards or Tac Nav qualified affiliates designated by the 
CStdsO. Tac Nav training missions will be scheduled in FlightPro from the Hvd Staff Progress. 
Progress cards will be completed by the supervising QFI. All successful check rides resulting in 
a Staff Tac Nav qualification will be accompanied by a Staff Ride workflow.
53. Phases.
a. Phase 1 – Proficiency. This phase will consist of a minimum of 1 sortie for 
experienced QFIs (successfully completed Phase III or higher/equivalent) or from 
2 – 5 sorties for the inexperienced QFIs, as per Annex R. Ideally, candidates will 
be paired with another candidate and both A/C in the formation will be 
conducting upgrade training. QFIs undergoing this training will be responsible for 
all aspects of the Tac Nav mission both as lead and a wingman. The following 
general guidelines will be in effect:
(1) The QFI supervising the upgrade check ride will act as A/C Comd and 
will fly in the same aircraft as the QFI to be upgraded. The final sortie in 
PH I will be considered the proficiency check. Once successfully 
completed, candidates may be permitted to continue to Phase 2;
(2) The QFI seeking the upgrade will be responsible for the briefing, conduct 
and debrief of the upgrade mission. While leading, the QFI will be 
expected to maintain control of the formation in a variety of situations; 
and
(3) Where practical, candidates should be exposed to proficiency missions 
from both front and rear seats.
(4) Following the awarding of the Tac Nav Wingman qualification, the QFI 
must fly in a Tac Nav formation with a Tac Nav Instructor in the other 
aircraft at least once every 6 months to maintain the qualification. Should 

162
the qualification lapse, a proficiency flight shall be conducted with Tac 
Nav Instructor in the same aircraft to regain it. 
b. Phase 2 – Instructional. This phase will consist of a minimum of 1 sortie, and not 
more than 4 sorties per QFI. The sortie(s) will be focused on instruction of Tac 
Nav. The final sortie in PH 2 will be considered the Tac Nav qualification check 
and shall be flown by Standards or a Tac Nav qualified QFI designated by the 
CStdsO. The following general guidelines will be in effect:
(1) The QFI supervising the upgrade check ride will act as A/C Comd and 
will fly in the same aircraft as the QFI to be upgraded;
(2) The QFI seeking the upgrade will be responsible for monitoring, guiding 
and assessing the briefing, conduct and debrief of the simulated 
instructional upgrade mission; and
(3) Tac Nav candidates are required to fly instructional phase missions from 
the rear seat.
54. Grading. Phase 1 – Proficiency and Phase 2 – Instructional grading during this phase will 
be as per Annex R.
55. Standards will record all upgrades in the individual’s Aircrew File and in FlightPro 
following successful completion of each phase. An entry shall be made in the log book of the 
newly-upgraded QFI following successful completion of Phase I.
"A" Category Upgrade Program
56. Flt Comds shall nominate appropriate candidates for initial selection of candidates at the 
2 CFFTS supervisors’ meetings. The CFI and CStdsO will review nominations with 
consideration given to 2 CFFTS and Flt requirements, previous experience, instructional 
experience, and overall performance. The selected candidates’ names will be forwarded to the 
Cmdt for approval prior to commencing upgrade training.
57. The responsibility for managing the A Cat Upgrade Program rests with FIS. In order to 
allow the co-ordination of this program the following steps are to be followed:
a. Flt Comds are to prepare and submit selected candidates’ T-21 to CStdsO;
b. FIS will inform all selected nominees as to timings and pre-upgrade requirements. 
c. Should a candidate be unsuccessful in upgrading to an A category on their initial 
attempt, remedial training will be coordinated thru FIS and submitted to the 
CStdsO for approval prior to implementation.

163
Senior QFI (B+)
58. Senior QFIs are appointed at Cmdt discretion. Flt Comds can nominate candidates for 
Senior QFI appointment by stating the requirement at the Commanders Update Brief. If the 
Cmdt approves, the Flt Comd will attach the Senior Instructor Recommendation workflow to the 
candidate’s upcoming CAT Ride/Sup Check. Senior QFI nominees shall hold a minimum of a B 
instructional category and shall meet the following criteria:
a. have accumulated 300 hours of instructional training;
b. demonstrated a high level of instructional and flying proficiency on recent Cat 
Ride/Sup check flights with Standards Flight; and,
c. demonstrated the integrity necessary to assume a leadership position in the school.
NOTE: The Cmdt of 2 CFFTS may authorize early upgrade to those instructors who 
have clearly demonstrated they meet or exceed the instructional abilities required by a 
Senior Instructor, but who do not meet the pre-requisite instructional hour requirement.
59. A Check Ride/Sup Trainer specific to B+ (Solo Check) scenario will normally be 
conducted by Standards unless otherwise recommended by Flt Comd and CStd O. 
Supervisory B QFI (B Sup)
60. With concurrence of the CStdsO and/or CFI, Flt Comds may nominate candidates for 
Supervisory B QFI (B Sup) appointment during the weekly Commanders Update Briefing. B Sup 
QFI appointees shall meet the following minimum criteria:
a. Senior QFI appointment (B+),
b. Element Lead (ELUG) qualification,
c. Completed A Cat Ground School,
d. Completed Supervisor Instructor airborne staff training involving role-playing, 
AMOL, EDIC and staff development scenarios.
e. Demonstrated the integrity necessary to assume a leadership position in the 
school.
61. Additional notes:
a. Cmdt 2 CFFTS may authorize early upgrade to those supervisors who have 
clearly demonstrated they meet or exceed the instructional abilities required by a 
B Sup QFI.

164
b. A staff trainer specific to a B Sup scenario will be conducted by 2 CFFTS 
Standards or designate.
c. Once appointed B Sup by the Cmdt, the staff member will be authorized to 
conduct “in house” B Category trainers.
d. Flight Comds are encouraged to monitor their staff members during mutual flying 
missions.
Flying Awards – QFI
62. Flying awards shall be conducted IAW the RCAF FOM. 
63. Responsibility for the requisition and completion of scrolls for presentation are as 
follows:
a. “A” Instructional Category Scrolls. CStdsO is to ensure requisition and 
preparation of these awards;
b. 1000h and 2000h Aircraft Type Awards. 2 CFFTS OpsO is to ensure requisition 
and preparation of these awards through CAE.

165
CHAPTER 7 – REMEDIAL MEASURES AND UNSATISFACTORY COURSE 
PROGRESS
Student Counselling
1. This order applies to all students including OTs. When it becomes necessary to counsel a 
student, counselling Officers will use the appropriate forms (Annex O).
2. The overall aim of counselling is to correct the deficiency before drastic measures are 
required. To deal with matters of discipline:
a. a UASAR for minor offences (Annex M) or Statement of Formal Warning 
(Annex N) for major offences must be completed with sufficient detail so that 
failure to correct the deficiency or any recurrence of a similar nature will be easily 
identified to review a student's progress; and/or
b. normal CF disciplinary or administrative action shall be taken for repeat or 
serious offences.
3. Flt Comds or the CFI shall conduct formal counselling for flying training and flight line 
related activities. CDs, Flt Comds or the Adjt shall conduct formal counselling for all other 
offences. 
4. When completed, UASARs, Formal Warning, and Record of Counseling shall be passed 
to the CFI for administration and retention on the student's training file.
5. If a Flt Comd or PRB/IR Chairman requires the student to have an interview with the 
Wing Social Worker (WSWO) a request for the interview shall be made through the CFI. (Selfinitiated interviews by a student can be made directly with the WSWO).
Student Re-Course Procedures
6. Given the scheduling constraints of the block training program, if a student cannot 
complete all of the ground school or FTD missions within the allotted time, they may need to be 
re-coursed to a later course. There is some flexibility within the flying blocks; however, extended 
absences will result in a student re-course.
7. All recommendations for student re-coursing will be submitted to the CFI by the Flt 
Comd. The CFI will review all of the relevant factors prior to making a recommendation to the 
Cmdt.
8. Upon approval by the Cmdt, the CFI will inform the Adjt, NFTC Prog Services, Student 
Administrator, and respective Flt Comd. The Flt Comd will inform the student of the re-course
decision.

166
9. If a recommendation for re-coursing originates from Ops Flt, the Flt Comd will be 
consulted prior to making recommendations to the CFI.
10. The CFI will action any required correspondence with 2 Cdn Air Div AF Training and/or 
D Air CFG where applicable.
Suspended Training
11. Decisions to suspend students from training rest with the CFI and/or Cmdt. If a decision 
is taken to suspend a student from training, the CFI will inform the Adjt, NFTC Prog Services, 
Student Administrator, and respective Flt Comd. The CFI will further direct whether the student 
is to cease ground school training and return course materials and laptop computers. Normally 
the student shall not be permitted to fly practice FTDs. When a student is ready to resume 
training following a period exceeding 45 days, a Reintegration proposal IAW 1 Cdn Air Div 
Orders Vol 5, 5-217 must be staffed to 2 Cdn Air Div/AF AOT through 15 Wing Stds. For 
breaks in training of 45 days or less, the unit Review Mission policy applies. Once the 
reintegration plan is approved by SSO AOT, an electronic copy will be sent to the student’s 
Flight Commander and GBTS via the NFTC services training administrator. Flight Commanders 
will have students acknowledge and sign a hard copy of the reintegration plan, then archive the 
document in the student’s training file with the 2 CFFTS Adjt.
Academics Unsatisfactory Progress
Ref: ITP Phase II Chapter 3
NOTE: For this Order, the term Academic Performance includes Officer Development (OD) 
classroom training.
12. General.
a. Below Average academic performance is indicated by consistently below average 
results on examinations, Progress Tests, quizzes, or term assignments; and
b. for students demonstrating below average performance, a prompt and positive 
remedial program is necessary. Close liaison between the Academic Instructors, 
Flying Instructors, and CDs/ Flt Comds is essential to ensure effectiveness.
13. Stage I: Below average performance - First failure of a Progress Test or OD assignment.
a. Academic Instructor duties:
(1) counsel the student on their study habits and provide a remedial action 
plan, positive direction and followup to ensure the student’s performance 
improves and meets the TP Standard; and

167
(2) complete and forward to the respective Flt Comd, the Student Academic 
Counselling Report (ACR) (Annex T), specifying the remedial and followup action initiated. 
b. Flt Comd duties:
(1) review the ACR and inform the CFI;
(2) provide Flt Comd comments on the ACR form; and
(3) forward the ACR form to the appropriate CD.
c. CD duties:
(1) review the student's file for overall course performance;
(2) discuss the student's progress with the applicable academic instructor and 
advise the Adjt, and Flt Comd
(3) forward the ACR to the Adjt for inclusion on student’s training file;
(4) follow up the student's response to counselling and inform the student’s 
Flt Comd of any observations; and
14. Stage II: Continued Below Average Performance – Unsatisfactory Academic 
Performance as defined by the ITP (failure of one final examination or failure of two progress 
tests):
a. Academic Instructor duties:
(1) inform the Flt Comd; and
(2) inform the student that the situation requires an IR.
b. The IR normally recommends:
(1) Tutorial Assistance and, if required, a supplemental exam;
(2) further language training; or
(3) a Progress Review Board (PRB) be convened to assess suitability for 
further training.
15. Stage III: Academic Course Failure – As defined by the ITP Chap 3
a. In the event of academic Course Failure:

168
(1) the Academic Instructor will inform the CD and Flt Comd;
(2) the Flt Comd will advise the CFI and Adjt; and
(3) the CFI will convene a PRB.
b. The PRB shall be conducted IAW 1 Cdn Air Div Orders Vol 5-035 
b. The Cmdt shall, based on the recommendations of the PRB, determine the course 
of action. 
16. If the student is retained and fails another Progress test or exam they is normally be 
considered a course failure. However, where extenuating circumstances exist, the Cmdt may 
reconvene the PRB prior to making a final decision.
Flying Training - Unsatisfactory Progress
Ref A: ITP Part 2, Ch 3.
17. A student's unsatisfactory flying progress can manifest itself in several different ways: 
inconsistent performance, inability to meet required proficiency levels within syllabus time, test 
failures, flying violations or various combinations thereof. Course missions will be monitored 
closely to ensure that the student does not exceed the flying training allocated in the ITP. 
18. Any unsatisfactory progress must be entered properly in the student's progress record 
(FlightPro).
19. Flight supervisory personnel are to be debriefed by the testing officer immediately after a 
test failure.
20. Flt Comds are to ensure all unsatisfactory student progress is reported directly to the CFI.
Weight Restriction – Student Handling
Ref: flight manual 4-13-59 Escape Limitations
21. This order provides guidelines to assist supervisors in the handling of students who are 
unable to maintain the aircraft ejection seat weight limitations.
22. If a student is unable to maintain body weight within the required ejection seat 
limitations, the member must immediately inform the CoC CC’ing their CD who shall in turn 
inform the 2 CFFTS Adjt. Thereafter, the CFI will consult with a Flt Surgeon and the PSP staff 
to give further direction to the CD for liaison between the applicable agency(s) and the member. 
The CD will provide feedback to the CFI once an individualized plan has been developed.

169
23. Although efforts must be made to assist the student in managing any weight issues, TP 
course flying time and safety of flight cannot be compromised. The decision regarding best 
course of action in the event of failing to meet body weight restrictions is made by the Cmdt, in 
consultation with the Wg Surgeon, CFI and Flt Comd.
Airsickness - Student Handling
Ref: 1 Cdn Air Div Orders, Vol 5, 5-209
24. This order provides guidelines to assist supervisors in the handling of airsick students. 
Airsickness cases are infinitely variable and each case must be judged on its own merit.
25. When a student is airsick or suffers from nausea to the point where flying performance is 
impaired, an entry must be made in FlightPro, stating date, mission number and magnitude of 
problem. The student’s QFI will then discuss the problem with the Flt Comd . The Flt Comd may 
engage the Flt Surgeon through the CFI. Thereafter, the Flt Surgeon should be kept informed of 
the student's progress with respect to airsickness. The Flt Surgeon will make further 
recommendations to the CFI.
26. Although efforts must be made to help the student through any airsickness problem, TP
flying missions and safety of flight cannot be compromised. The decision regarding whether to 
continue the student's flying training in the face of chronic airsickness is made by the Cmdt, in 
consultation with the Wg Surgeon, CFI and Flt Comd.
Extra Dual
27. Flt Comds are the approving authority for extra dual (ED) instruction following an 
unsatisfactory student syllabus mission or first test failure with the following constraints:
a. a maximum of two consecutive extra dual missions can be authorized to correct 
the deficiency;
b. a maximum of two dual practice FTDs can be awarded by the Flt Comd, if the 
FTD is deemed useful in correcting the student’s deficiencies;
(1) additional training time will not exceed the course flying time limits as 
outlined in the ITP.
28. When EDs are granted, the student should attain the required proficiency levels before 
proceeding with work in other phases. Exceptions will be approved by the CFI. 
29. If the student fails to demonstrate the required level of performance after the completion 
of a maximum of two ED flights, the Cmdt or the CFI shall be consulted to determine which of 
the following courses of action will be taken:

170
a. authorization to fly up to two more EDs (if there are extenuating circumstances 
which warrant further training and course time is available); or
b. convene a PRB.
30. Should the student fail to correct the deficiencies by the end of additional EDs, a PRB 
shall be convened.
31. If the student has only met Unsatisfactory Course Progress, an IR is the approving 
authority for Extra Dual (ED) instruction following unsatisfactory flying progress:
a. an IR may authorise a maximum of up to two consecutive FTD ED missions and 
two ED flying missions to correct the deficiency;
b. additional ED missions will not exceed the limits as outlined in the TP; and
c. should the student fail to correct the deficiencies by the end of additional EDs, a 
PRB shall be convened.
32. Any EOB failure not meeting the criteria IAW the ITP is an Unsatisfactory mission only 
and does not require an IR. A single test failure does not require an IR therefore in both cases an 
Unsatisfactory Flight workflow will be created and will be the approving authority for ED 
instructions as stipulated in para 1a.
33. EDs will only be flown by A or B Category instructors.

171
CHAPTER 8 – IR /PRB PROCEDURES AND GUIDELINES
Ref A: 1 Cdn Air Div Orders Vol 5-035 
B. 2 Cdn Air Div A3 Trg, Progress Review Board (PRB) Aide-Memoire, 3 Dec 2021
General
1. 2 CFFTS Progress Review Procedures provide guidelines for the conduct of student 
progress reviews including the initiation and conduct for Independent Reviews (IR) and Progress 
Review Boards (PRB).
2. Progress reviews must be conducted thoroughly and fairly. A structured system of review 
has been established in Ref A to ensure that:
a. training progress is monitored closely;
b. weaknesses are detected at an early stage and that positive corrective action is 
taken before progress becomes unsatisfactory; and,
c. circumstances surrounding unsatisfactory course progress are examined to rectify 
the problem and to make recommendations for the student’s disposition.
3. The system employs a multi-tier approach consisting of three levels of review and 
reporting to address escalating levels of performance deficiency:
a. Regular Progress Review. A Regular Progress Review is used to address minor 
problems students often experience, such as failure of a syllabus mission or a 
marginal training assessment, before these failures become major problems. This 
level of review also includes the feedback the student receives through verbal 
debriefs and trip assessments on their performance for each trainer or flight, 
including assessment and feedback of their Officer Development (OD) including 
dress and deportment. Additionally, GBTS instructors, 2 CFFTS staff, QFIs , 
Course Directors (CD) and Flt Comds monitor daily student progress and may 
prescribe basic remedial instruction for minor student difficulties as soon as they 
occur;
b. Independent Review (IR). See table below.
c. Progress Review Board (PRB). See table below.
4. Unsatisfactory flying or academic progress and course failure are the primary conditions 
that will trigger a formal progress review of an IR or PRB. Flying and academic performance 
shortfalls that reflect unsatisfactory progress and course failure are contained within the ITP. 
Poor OD including professional misconduct may also trigger formal progress review. However, 
typical initiating conditions for formal progress review, the composition of reviewing teams, and 
eligibility criteria are presented in the following table:

172
Independent Review (IR) Progress Review Board (PRB)
Condition - Unsatisfactory flying progress 
(IAW ITP)
- Remedial trg is suspected as 
being the appropriate outcome.
- Course failure academic or flying 
(IAW ITP)
- Corrective actions arising from 
previous levels of review are 
unsuccessful.
- Voluntary withdrawal.
- Cmdt’s prerogative.
Composition Chair - 1 x QFI (should have 
shadowed an IR previously)
Chair - 1 x Senior QFI or as 
designated by CFI
Members – 2 x QFI 
Eligibility

- Up to two IRs can be conducted 
from within the flight as an 
internal review. If a third IR is 
required, it will be completed 
externally from the flight. An 
external IR may also be requested 
by the Flt Comd if they believe it 
is required to maintain 
transparency. 
- No interaction with subject 
student within area of difficulty.
- Members should have observed 
PRB.
