however, C-CAT may participate if 
authorized by CFI.
- Chair and members shall not have 
had direct instructor relationship with 
subject student.
- PRB proceedings may be audited by 
HHQ.
Report Format Per 1 Cdn Air Div Vol 5-035
Time Limit No longer than 1 day No longer than 3 days 
5. If a formal progress review (IR or PRB) is required, the applicable Flt Comd shall inform 
the CFI, who will initiate appropriate actions. Flt Comds will ensure that the applicable CD is 
also advised and provided with the circumstances surrounding the requirement for progress 
review. More information concerning formal progress review levels can be found later in this 
chapter.
6. 2 CFFTS Cmdt retains authority to convene a PRB if deemed appropriate in their 
judgement, even if the student has not been the subject of an IR. Cases involving professional 
misconduct are an example of such an instance.
Independent Review (IR)
7. An IR is initiated when a student meets unsatisfactory progress criteria IAW the ITP. The 
IR is meant to be a rapid process to address deficiencies in an objective manner and begin any 
remedial action as quickly as possible. 

173
8. The IR is to be conducted by a designated QFI.
9. By the end of the day of commencing, the IR the Chairperson shall, IAW Ref A, conduct 
the IR by:
a. reviewing the student’s training file and Flight Pro records;
b. interview personnel involved with the student’s trg. These may include the CD, 
Flt Comd, instructors, trg and standards personnel;
c. interview the student;
d. if required, request through the CFI for recommendations from those whom may 
have dealt with any medical or personal issues that may have negatively impacted 
the student’s performance. These could include a Flt Surgeon, Wing Padre, or 
WSWO; 
e. determine areas of weaknesses; and,
f. recommend a course of action, including milestones, that should overcome 
deficiencies and permit the student to progress at the required rate. NOTE: For an 
IR, there is no requirement to record or sign any witness statements. Audio / video 
recordings of interviews are prohibited. Input initiated by the WSWO shall only 
be made through the Wg Surgeon office.
10. Upon completion of the IR, the Chairperson shall submit the IR to the CFI for their 
approval. A report shall be completed detailing the Chairperson’s findings, conclusions and 
recommendations.
11. Duties and Responsibilities:
a. Unit Admin Support:
(1) At completion of IR, place a copy on the student’s trg file.
b. IR Chair, CFI:
(1) Upon determining the requirement for an IR, the applicable Flt Comd will 
inform the CFI and appoint a QFI for an internal review. If an external 
review is required, the CFI will coordinate with another Flt Comd to 
appoint an IR chairperson;
(2) Upon completing the IR, the Chairperson will discuss the proposed 
recommendation(s) with the Flt Comd and CFI. The IR Chairperson shall 
complete the IR Report and submit it to the CFI; and

174
(3) Upon receiving the CFI’s verbal or written authorization, the IR 
Chairperson will brief the respective Flt Comd on the IR’s findings and 
CFI’s decision.
c. CD, Flt Comd and Student:
(1) The Flt Comd or delegated officer will brief the student on the decisions 
arising from the IR as soon as possible after being briefed by the IR 
Chairperson.
Progress Review Board (PRB)
Ref A: 1 Cdn Air Div Orders 5-035
B: 2 Cdn Air Div A3 Trg, Progress Review Board (PRB) Aide-Memoire, 3 Dec 2021
12. A PRB is a formal review convened by the Cmdt when a student is in danger of failing 
the course. The PRB shall consider any previously completed level(s) of review. PRBs will be 
completed as expeditiously as possible, in most cases no longer than three days to complete, not 
including time for the student rebuttal; however, some delays such as medical disposition can 
extend this timeline. PRB Members will be removed from the flying schedule to solely focus on 
the PRB. Ref B provides guidance on how to conduct a PRB as well as providing example 
templates. 
13. A PRB may be convened by the Cmdt 2 CFFTS IAW Ref A.
14. Students may be suspended or removed from flying trg for medical (including psychosocial) reasons upon the recommendation of the Wg Surgeon. The Wg Surgeon shall advise the 
PRB, through the Cmdt or CFI, of the likely outcome of the suspension, i.e. short-term, longterm or permanent grounding. In the event of a temporary suspension, the PRB will delay its 
final report until the Wg Surgeon has restored the student’s medical category. In the event of a 
permanent loss of medical category, the student will first be referred to the CFI then to the 2 
CFFTS Adjutant (Adjt) for required administrative actions and to determine future employment 
possibilities. In this case, the CFI shall ensure that a CF 377 Course Report is completed.
15. The supervisor who considers that a PRB is warranted shall request it be convened 
through the CFI, who will make a recommendation to the Cmdt. The CFI will select PRB 
Chairpersons on a rotational basis and IAW direction in Ref A. When a student has met course 
fail criteria, the Cmdt shall be notified of the requirement for a PRB. In cases where course fail 
criteria has not been met, the decision to convene a PRB shall rest solely with the Cmdt and it 
shall be documented in a written memorandum signed by him/her.
16. A student on a flying PRB shall not be permitted to fly while the PRB is in progress. A 
student on an Academic PRB will be allowed to continue with other ground school classes, but 
will not be conduct any formal assessments (ECs or PCs). In the event that a PRB is going to be 
delayed due to medical (including psycho-social assessments) to the point where re-course is the 

175
only continue-trg option, then the student shall be suspended from trg until the PRB is completed 
and their disposition is determined.
17. In conducting the investigation, the PRB shall:
a. review all orders relevant to the conduct of a PRB. Namely, ref A and B above as 
well as the 2 CFFTS Orders;
b. review all documentation relating to the member’s course trg including unit trg 
and standards files;
c. interview the student. The interview shall be comprehensive and cover any issues 
that may have influenced trg. The student must be made aware of the opportunity 
and the necessity to state any problems or extraordinary circumstances that the 
Board should consider;
d. interview trg or standards personnel, instructors, and/or supervisors who have 
personally witnessed the member’s performance. These interviews are not to be 
conducted in the presence of the member in order to solicit candid assessments. 
Questions should focus on identifying the root cause of a student’s issues linked 
to appropriate pilot competencies (as per ref B), provide an expert factual analysis 
of students performance and any opinions should be substantiated. The student 
shall not attend the proceedings of the PRB, apart from their own interview;
e. if required, interview personnel who may have dealt with any medical or personal 
issues that may have negatively impacted the student’s performance. These could 
include a Flt Surgeon, or Wing Padres. If required, input from the WSWO can be 
acquired through the Wing Surgeon’s (Wg Surgeon) office. All requests for 
interviews outside of the school shall go through the CFI; 
f. upon completion of the Board’s deliberations, the Chairperson shall present their 
findings and recommendations to the CFI prior to completing the report. The 
Chair and Flt Comd will debrief the student on the results and the student will 
sign the report as having read. The student/Course Member will be given the 
opportunity to correct, interject, question or provide counter-arguments to all the 
PRB findings by way of formal written rebuttal. The Chair will then submit the 
completed report, including student rebuttal/responses to Cmdt for their decision.
18. Under separate cover and only if applicable/required, the PRB may be requested to 
identify lessons learned and other recommendations for follow-on action.
19. At the conclusion of the PRB, a report shall be completed detailing the Board’s findings, 
conclusions and recommendations in accordance with Ref A.
20. The student is entitled access to all of the information used by the PRB. The student will 
be permitted to review the information in a controlled environment with the aim of submitting, in 

176
writing, any questions, clarifications, and counter arguments to all the Board’s findings within 24 
hours. The student may make notes, but they shall not be permitted to photocopy the PRB report 
or any of the documentation. Should 24 hours prove to be insufficient, the student shall request 
extensions in 24-hour blocks, from the PRB Chairperson. The student’s inputs/rebuttals and the 
PRB’s response shall both be incorporated into the PRB Report. The responses to any issues 
raised by the student shall be explained to him/her by the PRB and, if required, he/she shall be 
permitted to provide counterpoints. The completed report including student rebuttal/responses 
shall be sent to the Cmdt for their decision.
21. The Cmdt will make the final decision regarding the disposition of the student. If the 
Cmdt does not take the Board’s recommendation(s) and decides on another course (or courses) 
of action, then the student will be so advised. The Cmdt will interview the student and inform 
them of the decision and permit the student to offer any other comments that they may not have 
previously brought up or that he/she does not believe were adequately addressed. The Cmdt may 
reconvene the PRB to address any “new” concerns. Otherwise the Cmdt will inform the student 
of their decision. 
22. The Cmdt’s decision regarding the student shall be documented in the Candidate 
Disposition form IAW Ref B. In those cases where the Cmdt does not have the authority to direct 
cessation or continuation of trg, the proceedings will be forwarded, with a cover letter prepared 
for the WComd’s signature, to 2 Cdn Air Div AF Trg (Trg) for a decision.
23. In all instances, a copy of the PRB report, including the Cmdt’s decision, will be sent to 2 
Cdn Air Div, AF Trg. All phases of the PRB process should be executed in a timely and 
expeditious manner.
24. Duties and responsibilities:
a. Unit Admin Support.
(1) secure conference room if required;
(2) format PRB report and prepare it for applicable signatures; and
(3) distribute, including proper archiving of finalized PRB report and other 
documentation as required.
b. Chair, Board Members, CFI, and Cmdt.
(1) The Chairperson will conduct the PRB IAW Ref A, Ref B and 2 CFFTS 
Orders. 
(2) Once the PRB is completed it shall be debriefed to the student by the PRB 
Chairperson. The Chairperson will ensure the student is granted access to 
the PRB report IAW para 20 of this chapter.

177
(3) The completed report including student statement shall be sent to the Unit 
Admin Support for formatting then presented to the Cmdt for their 
decision. 
(4) The Chairperson will ensure the student training file is returned to Unit 
Admin Support.
(5) The Cmdt or their designate shall debrief the student on their decision and 
provide the student a final opportunity to raise any issue(s) he/she may 
feel were not adequately addressed by the PRB. The Cmdt can choose to 
re-open the PRB if he/she believes it necessary to address the concerns.
(6) Upon reaching a decision, the Cmdt will advise the CFI of the final 
outcome. The CFI or designated officer will debrief the appropriate 
supervisors of the final decision and course of action.
c. CD, Flt Comd and Student.
(1) The student’s Flt Comd will advise the student of the Cmdt’s decision to 
convene a PRB.
(2) Upon completion of the PRB report, the student’s supervisor or delegated 
representative shall coordinate with the Chairperson to provide the student 
controlled access for review. 
25. If Cease Trg (CT) is directed.
a. once the Cmdt informs 2 Cdn Air Div AF Trg of the decision to cease train the 
student, the Admin O will forward “Change of Position Notices” for cease trg, 
recourse or temporary removal from course; and
b. the student’s supervisor or a delegated officer shall interview the student after the 
Cmdt’s debrief and confirmation of CT. Following the interview the student will 
be sent to the Adjt for disposition.
26. The Flt Comd shall ensure that the PSO Referral Form and course report (CF377) is 
completed and returned to the Adjt/Unit Admin Support as soon as practicable.
Action in the Event of Cease Training or Voluntary Withdrawal (VW)
27. In the event that a student is cease trained, they shall return all flying equipment issued 
by the 15 Wing clothing stores, including but not limited to the knee board, publications bag, 
modified wet-weather bibs and locally issued flight suits, within two working days. Additionally, 
any training materials belonging to CAE must be returned within two working days.

178
28. Cease-trained pre-wings students will remain under the authority of the unit and 
supervision of the Adj. The Adj will coordinate employment for the students while they await 
reassignment or release.
29. Students who Voluntarily Withdraw (VW) from Training training may remain in the unit 
at the discretion of the Cmdt. These students remain under the authority of the unit and 
supervision of the Adjt until posted or released.

179
CHAPTER 9 - GBTS QUALITY ASSURANCE
General
1. This chapter outlines the quality assurance plan for ensuring standardized Academic and 
FTD instruction within the NFTC program. CMAT is responsible for training Ground School 
and FTD instructors in both Phase II and Phase III of the NFTC program. 2 CFFTS/AOT SET is 
responsible for conducting the qualification of all initial instructors, as well as for the routine 
monitoring of the quality and standard of instruction provided on a daily basis. To this end, the 
CStdsO is responsible for GBTS monitoring and quality assurance. This monitoring will be in 
the form of regularly scheduled classroom and FTD monitoring. This chapter outlines the 
administrative details regarding Academic and FTD monitoring, as well as procedures to follow 
with regard to day-to-day quality assurance issues.
2. The central role of 2 CFFTS/AOT SET within the QA of GBTS is to ensure that new 
staff members are properly trained to provide instruction to student pilot candidates. 
Furthermore, 2 CFFTS/AOT SET is responsible to ensure that any staff members within CMAT, 
tasked with fulfilling an internal standards role, are fully capable of overseeing the routine 
functioning of GBTS. A significant proportion of the on-going QA role within GBTS may be 
conducted by internal monitoring.
Classroom Monitoring
3. The purpose of classroom monitoring is to ensure that a high standard of ground 
instruction is being provided to all NFTC student pilots, regardless of their phase of training. To 
this end, the primary focus during classroom monitoring will be on the quality of instruction 
being provided by the individual Ground School instructors. Secondary to this will be to ensure 
that the subject matter is accurate, relevant, and appropriate to the classroom environment. 
Furthermore, the quality of the instruction must be demonstrated to a high standard ensuring 
uniformity of delivery technique and utilising recognised tools of instruction.
4. The formal acceptance or qualification of a classroom instructor will initially be 
coordinated through the CStdsO and the GBTS Supervisor. After this initial 2 CFFTS/AOT SET
checkout, formal monitoring of individual instructors will normally occur on an annual basis, 
however, more frequent monitoring may be dictated by the demonstrated proficiency. Whenever 
the quality of instruction demonstrated by the instructor is such that recurring monitoring is 
scheduled annually, GBTS Standards will normally be delegated the responsibility to perform 
subsequent monitoring sessions. GBTS will keep a separate training file on each Ground School 
instructor, available to the CStdsO for auditing purposes. This file will include a copy of the 
instructor’s initial qualification record as well as the original copy of all classroom-monitoring 
forms.
5. GBTS standards personnel, who are delegated the responsibility of monitoring their staff, 
will also be observed performing this role on an annual basis. In the absence of suitable GBTS 
standards personnel, 2 CFFTS/AOT SET will conduct annual monitoring of GBTS staff. In order 
to ensure the standard of training is being met, the CStdsO may also, at any time, schedule 

180
additional classroom monitoring of GBTS staff, to be conducted by appropriate 2 CFFTS/AOT 
SET staff.
6. The applicable Standards instructor will record all details of the monitoring session on 
the GBTS Academic Monitor Form (NFTC 258). Delegated monitoring will similarly be 
recorded on NFTC form 258. The completed form will be held in the GBTS Training file.
7. Classroom monitoring shall be carried out at regular intervals on all GBTS ground school 
instructors. The frequency of these monitor sessions will be every six months after initial 
qualification unless otherwise specified.
8. The following individuals may complete Ground School monitors: the Cmdt, CFI, 
CStdsO, Standards Officer, AOT SET staff or GBTS Standards. The aim of the monitor session 
is to check classroom proficiency and instructional ability. The monitor pilot shall complete the 
GBTS Academic Monitor form (NFTC 258).
9. The monitor session should be conducted in conjunction with a normal student-trg 
ground school class. All aspects of the Classroom lesson will be monitored, to include the lesson 
preparation and the ground school lesson. Classroom monitor sessions are not tests in 
themselves. The classroom monitor should refrain from disrupting the GBTS instructor’s lesson. 
If called upon, the monitor pilot may offer up testimonial or personal experience to assist the 
GBTS instructor in getting their teaching points across.
10. Classroom monitor sessions will be conducted as follows:
a. Pre-Lesson Briefing by the Monitor Pilot. The monitor pilot’s briefing should 
cover all aspects of the Classroom monitor session so the GBTS instructor has a 
full understanding of the monitor requirements;
b. Lesson Preparation by the Classroom instructor. The Classroom instructor should 
prepare for the lesson in their normal manner, explaining, when necessary, their
normal routine and rationalisation if required. The GBTS instructor is expected to 
use accepted lesson preparation standards while demonstrating a thorough 
knowledge of the lesson plan content;
c. Classroom Lesson. The objective is to monitor the Classroom instructor’s ability 
to impart knowledge in the classroom. When assessing specific points, the 
monitor pilot should consider the following: 
(1) the GBTS instructor’s ability to maintain clear communications with the 
students;
(2) effective use of trg aids;
(3) proficient use of the Classroom Equipment;

181
(4) effective use of the approved classroom instructional techniques;
(5) the validity of the classroom instructor’s questioning and teaching 
techniques; and
(6) whether the classroom instructor makes efficient use of time and 
classroom resources.
d. Classroom Debrief. The monitor pilot will give a complete debrief of the 
Classroom monitoring session. If doubtful instructional ability is encountered, 
proceed as per the guidelines established in this chapter; and
e. Classroom Observation. If the lesson conducted was being monitored by GBTS 
Standards and observed by a qualified DND Observer, then the Observer is 
responsible to de-brief the monitor on their overall assessment of the instructor’s 
lesson. In addition to the classroom monitor form, completed by GBTS Standards, 
another form (NFTC 258) will be completed by the Observer. It should contain 
appropriate comments/recommendations based upon the GBTS Standard’s 
conduct and assessment of the monitoring session. It shall be emphasized that the 
intent of the classroom monitoring session is to ensure that the GBTS instructor 
can provide the students with competent and professional instruction. If, at any 
point, the monitor pilot considers that the students are receiving inadequate 
instruction, the monitor pilot should intervene to ensure that the Learning 
Objective is met for the students. However, it must be kept in mind that 
intervention carries significant implications and should only be exercised in 
situations where instruction is very inadequate.
FTD Instructor Monitoring
11. The purpose of FTD instructor monitoring is to ensure a high standard of FTD instruction 
is being provided to all NFTC student pilots, regardless of their phase of training. To this end, 
the primary focus during FTD instructor monitoring will be on the quality of instruction being 
provided. Additionally, the CStdsO or delegate will ensure that the FTD instructor is 
administering all aspects of the FTD mission (i.e. mission briefing, lesson plan and mission 
debriefing) in accordance with the applicable TP and supporting training documentation. 
Furthermore, the monitor pilot will ensure that the subject matter is accurate, relevant, and 
appropriate to the FTD training environment.
12. Formal FTD monitoring will be coordinated through the CStdsO and the GBTS 
Supervisor. After the initial 2 CFFTS/AOT SET checkout, formal monitoring of individual 
instructors will normally occur on an annual basis, however, more frequent monitoring may be 
dictated by the demonstrated proficiency. Whenever the quality of instruction demonstrated by 
the instructor is such that recurring monitoring is scheduled annually, GBTS Standards will 
normally be delegated the responsibility to perform subsequent monitoring sessions. GBTS 

182
Standards personnel who are assigned the responsibility of monitoring their staff will also be 
observed performing this role on an annual basis.
13. The monitor pilot will record all details of the monitoring session on the GBTS FTD 
Instructor Standardization Report (NFTC 158). Internal monitoring will similarly be recorded on 
NFTC forms and placed within the GBTS Training File.
14. IOT ensure the standard of training is being met, the CStdsO may at any time schedule 
additional FTD monitoring of GBTS staff, to be conducted by appropriate 2 CFFTS/AOT SET
staff. In the absence of suitable GBTS Standards personnel, 2 CFFTS/AOT SET staff will 
conduct annual monitoring of GBTS staff.
15. FTD monitoring shall be carried out at regular intervals on all FTD instructors. The 
frequency of these monitor sessions will be every six months after initial qualification unless 
otherwise specified.
16. The following individuals may complete FTD monitors: the Cmdt, CFI, CStdsO, 
Standards Officer, AOT SET staff and GBTS Standards. The aim of the monitor is to check FTD 
proficiency and instructional ability. The monitor pilot shall complete the GBTS FTD Monitor 
form (NFTC 158).
17. In most cases, the monitor mission should be conducted in conjunction with a normal 
student-trg mission. Alternatively, the FTD instructor will conduct the ground briefing for a 
specific trip selected by the monitor pilot, with the monitor pilot acting as the student. All aspects 
of the FTD mission will be monitored, to include the briefing, the FTD mission and the student 
debrief. FTD monitor mission are not tests in themselves and dual instruction may be given by 
the monitor pilot during the FTD mission to bring the instructor’s capabilities up to the desired 
standard.
18. FTD monitor missions will be conducted as follows:
a. Pre-FTD Briefing by the Monitor Pilot. The monitor pilot’s briefing should cover 
all aspects of the FTD monitor mission so the FTD instructor has a full 
understanding of the monitor requirements;
b. FTD Briefing by the FTD instructor. The FTD instructor should use correct 
instructional techniques while demonstrating a thorough knowledge of procedures 
outlined in the appropriate Simulator Instructor Guide. Assessment of the briefing 
is accomplished by forming an impression of the whole briefing. The monitor 
pilot should not debrief the FTD instructor on the ground briefing until the 
completion of the FTD monitor trip. Should a very important item be taught 
incorrectly, then that one item may constitute an unsatisfactory FTD monitor 
mission, unless the FTD instructor corrects the error in the subsequent FTD 
mission and debriefing;

183
c. FTD Lesson. The objective is to monitor the FTD instructor’s ability to impart 
knowledge in the FTD. When assessing specific points, the monitor pilot should 
consider the following: 
(1) ability to maintain clear, two-way communications with the student;
(2) effective use of “flight freeze” and repositioning functions;
(3) proficient use of the FTD IOS;
(4) effective use of the tools of instruction;
(5) validity of the fault analysis; and
(6) efficient use of time and the FTD.
d. FTD Debrief. The FTD instructor’s debriefing should form the conclusion of the 
pre-FTD briefing and air lesson. This is the final opportunity to ensure that the 
lesson has been absorbed and to explain points or questions arising during the 
FTD lesson. Additionally, the monitor pilot will assess the FTD instructor’s 
progress card for accuracy and completeness. After the student has been 
dismissed, the monitor pilot will give a complete debriefing of the FTD 
monitoring session. If doubtful instructional ability is encountered, proceed as per 
the guidelines established in this chapter; and
e. FTD Observation. If the mission conducted was being monitored by GBTS 
Standards and observed by a qualified DND Observer, then the Observer will be 
responsible to de-brief the monitor on their overall assessment of the instructor’s 
lesson. In addition to the FTD monitor form, completed by GBTS Standards, 
another form (NFTC 158) will be completed by the Observer. It should contain 
appropriate comments/recommendations based on GBTS Standard’s conduct and 
assessment of the monitoring session.
19. It shall be emphasized that the intent of the FTD monitoring session is to ensure that the 
FTD instructor can provide the student with competent and professional instruction. If, at any 
point, the monitor pilot considers that the student, if present, is receiving inadequate instruction, 
the monitor pilot should intervene to ensure that the Learning Objective is met for the student. 
However, it must be kept in mind that intervention carries significant implications and should 
only be exercised in situations where instruction is very inadequate.
Doubtful GBTS Instructional Ability
20. This section outlines the process in the event that doubtful instructional ability is 
observed during a formal monitoring session. The applicable Standards instructor will inform the 
CStdsO of the circumstances of the monitoring session. If doubtful instructional ability is 
confirmed, the CStdsO will inform the Cmdt and the GBTS supervisor, who will suspend the 

184
applicable GBTS instructor from further student training. The CStdsO and the GBTS Supervisor 
will determine suitable remedial training for the GBTS instructor. This remedial training will be 
a joint Standards/GBTS effort, the aim of which will be to assist the GBTS instructor in 
correcting their deficiencies.
21. Upon completion of the remedial training the GBTS instructor will undergo another 
formal GBTS monitoring session, administered by Standards. If successful, the GBTS instructor 
will be returned to normal instructional duties. If doubtful instructional ability is still present, the 
CStdsO will inform the Cmdt who will inform the SSO AOT. AOT SET will conduct a formal 
assessment of the GBTS instructor’s instructional abilities.
22. CAE will determine follow-on actions regarding the GBTS instructor; however, the 
GBTS instructor will not be returned to normal instructional duties until demonstrating 
satisfactory performance on a formal assessment by AOT SET.
Daily Quality Assurance Issues
23. This section addresses routine quality assurance issues regarding daily classroom and 
FTD instruction. This may include, but is not limited to, substandard grading practices, 
questionable classroom instruction, inappropriate FTD mission conduct, non-standard progress 
card procedures, etc.
24. If, during normal operations, a Flt Comd is confronted with substandard training practices 
as outlined above, they will pass the applicable information to the CStdsO. If the issue is of a 
non-serious nature and can be handled through normal administrative actions, the CStdsO will 
inform the GBTS supervisor, who will handle the administrative details internally to GBTS. If 
the issue is serious in nature, the CStdsO will inform the GBTS Supervisor and follow -on 
administrative actions will be determined. Additionally, a formal GBTS monitoring session may 
be administered at the discretion of the CStdsO.
25. Regardless of the severity of the substandard training practices observed, a GBTS 
Standardization report will be completed and kept on the GBTS instructor’s training file. If 
repeated incidents occur with the same GBTS instructor, the GBTS instructor’s performance will 
be formally monitored in accordance with the guidelines of this chapter, the frequency of which 
will be at the discretion of the CStdsO.

185
CHAPTER 10 – STUDENT STANDING ORDERS
Liaison with Course Directors
1. Students are reminded that one of the primary duties of the Course Director is to help and 
guide students when problems arise either on the flight, in ground school or elsewhere. For the 
benefit of all concerned, it is strongly recommended that a student with a problem discuss the 
matter with their Course Director before any other action is taken.
Military Dress
Refs: A. 15 Wing Dress Regulations
B. RCAF FOM 
C. Canadian Air Division Orders (CADO)
2. Uniform of the day [Operational Dress (Flying/Field Combat/ Extreme Cold-Wet 
Weather), No. 3 (Service Dress), No. 4 (Base/Garrison Dress) depending on the season] may be 
worn on the wing or off the wing unless circumstances dictate otherwise.
3. Flying clothing may be worn IAW Refs. This pertains to all times on the flight line and in 
the mess or elsewhere on the wing as allowed by appropriate regulations.
4. Flying clothing may be worn while transiting to and from work or while conducting 
business in public (downtown, banking, etc.). Each student is reminded that they are responsible 
at all times for ensuring the highest standards of dress and deportment while in uniform and 
those who do not will be held accountable for their shortcomings.
5. Zippers and all pockets must be kept closed.
Moving About the Wing
6. When moving about the Wing in military dress, students shall walk smartly, refrain from 
loitering, smoking, chewing gum, walking with hands in pocket, using cell phones, and other 
unmilitary-like practices.
7. Walk two abreast only, in consideration for traffic between barracks and flight line. 
Students on the more senior courses must remember they are setting the example for their junior 
course peers. Stay on paved surfaces or sidewalks at all times when moving about the Wing.
Saluting - General
8. The military salute is a traditional demonstration of trust and respect. Although the 
method of saluting varies with circumstances, the paying of compliments by saluting is a 
fundamental requirement that is indispensable to service discipline.
9. Routinely, individual compliments shall be paid as follows:

186
a. Officers shall salute all officers of higher rank and shall return all compliments 
paid to them;
b. NCMs shall salute all commissioned officers; and
c. An officer accompanying an officer senior to him will not salute officers equal or 
junior in rank to the accompanied officer. The foregoing also applies to an NCM 
accompanying an officer.
10. Appropriate compliments (salute) shall be paid when recognizing an officer dressed in 
civilian clothing.
Saluting - Special Circumstances
11. Buildings: Salutes are not given indoors in either public or service buildings except on 
parades, ceremonial occasions, in areas so designated by the WComd, or when entering or 
leaving the office of an officer who should be paid compliments by virtue of their rank or 
appointment. NOTE: When not wearing head-dress when visiting the office of a higher ranking 
officer, compliments are paid by briefly coming to attention at the office door, and again when 
exiting.
12. Lecture Rooms: When a visiting officer or dignitary senior to the instructor enters a 
lecture room, theatre, etc, the instructor or senior member present shall call the group to attention 
by giving the command "Room". All members of the class shall sit at attention, arms straight at 
the side, head and eyes to the front, and heels together. 
13. Vehicles: Compliments shall be paid to passengers in staff cars bearing distinguishing 
flags (such as the Wing Commander) or general officer car plates. NOTE: Officers and NCMs 
driving a vehicle, motorcycle or bicycle shall not salute.
Military Courtesy
14. Normal military courtesy is expected and required at the Flight as well as in Ground 
School. When entering any office or the instructors' crew area, all students are required to stop, 
stand to attention and state their business. Officers cross training will address instructors of equal 
or lower rank by rank and surname while in the flight area.
15. The student first aware that the Flight Commander has entered the student crew area for 
the first time during the shift (am, pm, night) will call the room to attention.
16. The student first seeing any officer of the rank of LCol or higher enter the student crew 
area will call the room to attention.
17. Students will stand when addressed by an officer of the rank of Major or above in the 
student flight lounge.

187
Cross-Trainees
18. As serving officers, all cross-training personnel are responsible for normal rules and 
regulations that have governed them during their service to date and will adhere to the Student 
Standing Orders.
19. Cross-trainee Officers holding the rank of Captain for 2 years or longer prior to the 
course start date are not required to attend Officer Development classes, however they will be 
assessed on Officer Development as per 2 CFFTS Orders, Book 3 - FLODA.
20. It is incumbent on all cross-trainees to establish and maintain a high standard of military 
dress and deportment in order to set an example for their course mates.
21. Training Attendance, Leave and Special Authorization. Normal weekend procedures will 
apply for weekend leave.
22. Paying of Compliments - The standard military policy with regards to the payment of 
compliments applies to all cross-trainees.
Duty Hours
23. Duty hours are from 0800 hrs to 1700 hrs, or as modified by the flight line schedule. 
Dismissals from duty may be authorized as outlined in para 1 of 1.08.
Attendance and Absence from Place of Duty
24. Any time it is necessary for a student to be absent from duty, the student shall leave 
notice as to where they may be located. During normal ground school hours this involves 
informing the Course Director, GBTS Instructor and course senior. When on the flight line, the 
student must leave notification in accordance with applicable Flight SOPs.
25. The only acceptable reasons for not being at work and on time are:
a. sickness supported by a medical disposal slip;
b. personal emergency having notified supervisory personnel; or
c. dismissal granted by supervisory personnel.
26. The following directives are the otherwise mandatory attendance orders:
a. all students will attend a Met briefing in accordance with the standard operating 
procedures of their respective Flight;
b. students will be released from the flight line at the discretion of the Flight 
Commander, Deputy Flight Commander or the scheduler; and

188
c. the duty student is to be kept advised of the whereabouts of any excused student.
27. Further to the above, all students will ensure that they are fully prepared for their preflight briefing at least 90 minutes prior to their scheduled launch time. The exception to this rule 
is when instructors have made prior arrangements with their students for the briefing time.
Ground School
28. Ground school classes are mandatory for all course members on course at 2 CFFTS. If a 
ground school class must be missed, students must make arrangements with their respective 
Course Director and GBTS instructors. If a number of ground school classes or tests are missed, 
the Course Director will inform the change of command and provide recommendations for a 
possible recourse for the student.
Compliments in Ground School
29. Prior to the designated lecture commencement time all students should be in their proper 
place for that period of instruction. When the instructor is a commissioned officer who is ready 
to start the lesson, the Course Senior will call the room to attention. All students will sit to 
attention unless standing at the time. The Course Senior will then report to the instructor any 
absentees and give the reason for their absence.
30. Unless told to "carry on" by the instructor, the Course Senior will call the room to 
attention when the instructor leaves at the end of the period.
Classroom Procedures
31. Food or beverages are not normally to be consumed during formal lecture periods, 
however, during prolonged work periods, food or beverages may be permitted at the instructor's 
discretion.
32. The last class of the day is to ensure that all lights are turned off and that no training aids 
are left unsecured. The Course Senior is to ensure that the classroom is left in a neat and orderly 
condition. This specifically includes ensuring that chairs are pushed up to the tables and that 
whiteboards are cleaned at the instructors’ discretion. Instructors are to ensure that these duties 
are completed prior to the commencement or at the end of each period as applicable.
Excused Duty
33. Personnel authorized to excuse a student from duty are: (1) Wing Commander; (2) Cmdt 
2 CFFTS; (3) CFI, Adjt; (4) Medical and Dental Officers; and (5) Flight Commander, Flight 
Line Instructor or CD.
34. Students need to be aware that their course timelines are very tight and they need to make 
every effort to be present for groundschool and FTDs during their course. However, illness 
cannot be avoided and at times it will be best to stay home to fight an illness (especially when 

189
contagious). Students are to report to MIR when ill and report back to their flight or CD 
immediately after being diagnosed. Students are to be reminded that fighting a common cold 
could prevent them from flying but that this would normally not prevent a student from attending 
groundschool classes and FTD trg. If the illness is contagious and the MIR recommends bed rest, 
then students shall stay at home.
Sick Parade
35. Clinic sick parade hrs are from 0730 - 0900 hr. Walk-ins are possible prior to 1530 but it 
is best to phone the clinic for an appointment prior to showing up and waiting or being turned 
away. Medical Emergencies will be seen up until 1600. After 1600 hrs, members requiring care 
will report to civilian clinics/emerg departments if a medical issue can't wait until the following 
day. Ground school students, on sick parade, shall inform their course senior who will report to 
the instructor before each class. On the flight line, students will inform their primary instructor 
and/or scheduler. 
36. Special appointments are to be met if at all possible. If a student is prevented from 
keeping an appointment, he or she is to cancel the appointment as soon as practicable. 
37. On dismissal by the Medical Officer, a student will report with their disposition slip to 
the applicable Course Director, Flight Commander and /or scheduler. If hospitalised, students are 
to ask the hospital staff to advise the applicable Flight Commander, the Adjt or the student's 
Course Director.
38. Notwithstanding, in cases of emergency, the student may report to the hospital at any 
time but he shall notify the course senior or deputy before doing so or as soon as possible 
afterwards.
39. The annual aircrew medical examination for all students will be carried out during the 
month of their birth, unless course start dates necessitate an earlier medical. Students are to 
arrange their own appointment through the hospital Orderly Room, ideally one month in 
advance. These appointments will be made for morning periods as twelve hours fasting may be a 
pre-requisite of the examination.
Dental Treatment
40. Students requiring dental treatment shall report to dental sick parade between 0730-0830 
hours. Students must inform their course senior when in Ground school or their individual 
instructor when on the flight line.
41. On dental parade, students will likely have appointments made unless emergency 
treatment is required. No cancellation of appointments may be made without the approval of the 
dental officer. A request for change of appointment must be made more than 24 hours in 
advance.

190
42. Emergency dental treatment will be given at the dental clinic without appointment during 
working hours although students should endeavour to visit the dental clinic for this reason 
between 0800 hrs and 0830 hrs daily. During non-working hours, students suffering acute pain 
may request the telephone operator to contact the dental officer. If he is not available, students 
may report to the hospital for relief of pain.
43. When and if students do require dental treatment, B-GA-100-003/AA-000 (Flying Orders 
Vol. 3) Art 404, Item 2, applies.
Use of Wing Institute Facilities
44. The quarters of living-in officers who are on staff at 15 Wing are out of bounds to 
students unless the staff member concerned has issued a personal invitation. Students are not to 
attend non-commissioned members' social functions unless the event has been advertised as a 
Wing or all-ranks function.
Prohibited Establishments
45. The Cmdt may, at their discretion, prohibit undergraduate pilots from entering certain 
establishments in order to protect them from what has become an unsatisfactory threat to their 
personal safety. Currently there are no prohibited establishments. Should the need arise to 
designate one as such, the restriction will be passed to Course Seniors via their respective Course 
Directors. In any case, undergraduate pilots must always exercise good judgment and avoid 
where possible unnecessary confrontation while in public.
Officers Mess Dress Code
46. The following table shall be used as a guide for dress when attending mess events based 
on the types of occasions listed:
Females Males
Formal Mess kit or Dress No. 3 with white shirt and black bow tie for 
military members and appropriate black tie equivalent attire for 
civilians
Semi-Formal
Business-like attire such as 
dress pants, dress, or skirt and 
appropriate blouse
Business-like attire such as slacks 
with a dress shirt, suit jacket, and 
tie.
Informal
Dress, dress pants, or skirt, with 
suitable blouse or shirt as 
required
Dress pants and dress shirt
Casual Clean tasteful attire of neat and conservative appearance (No flipflops)
Prohibited Clothing
Mixed military/civilian attire, generally tasteless clothing, worn 
clothes, clothes with rips, tears or patches, athletic wear and clothing 
exposing the midriff

191
46. Outerwear shall be left in the cloakrooms. Headwear is to be removed upon entering the 
Mess and not replaced until leaving the Mess. Headwear shall not be worn in transit from 
entrances to cloakroom or cloakrooms to the exits.
Mess Dinners - Etiquette
47. Mess dinners are a normal part of military life. It is essential that all staff and students be 
familiar with the etiquette pertaining to them and that the highest level of decorum is maintained 
throughout the entire period of assembly and in the dining room. To this end, the CD is to ensure 
that all students attending mess dinners are thoroughly briefed on the etiquette points contained 
in this section.
48. Officers and others are required to assemble in the designated lounge not later than onehalf hour before the dinner (it is wise to have already noted your place on the seating plan prior 
to assembly).
49. During the period before dinner, members should pay their respects to the PMC. Should a 
member arrive after the Wing Commander and their guests have arrived, it is mandatory that 
they make apologies to the PMC at some time during the evening.
50. When the Wing Commander and guests arrive, the first officer who notices their arrival 
shall call the assembly to attention by calling out the order "Room".
51. On entering the dining room, all shall stand behind their chairs until Grace has been said.
52. The loyal toasts take place at the conclusion of the meal. Port decanters will be placed on 
the tables. The PMC will indicate that the port may be passed by pouring a small amount of port 
into their glass, tasting it, and passing the decanter to the left. Port decanters shall be passed to 
the left, and the decanter shall not be placed on the table until it requires replenishment or until 
all glasses are charged. Once port has been poured into the glass, it shall not be touched until it is 
time for the Loyal Toast.
53. When all glasses have been filled (it is permissible to drink water for the toast), the PMC 
rises and addresses the Vice/PMC with: "Mr. Vice, the Queen" or "ladies and gentlemen, the 
Queen". The Vice/PMC then rises and replies "Mesdames et messieurs, la Reine du Canada". 
Everyone shall then rise and stand at attention. If a band is present, it shall play the first verse of 
God Save the Queen. The playing of the first note will be this signal for all to raise their glasses 
waist high. When the band has stopped playing, all will say in a clear tone, "the Queen", then 
raise their glasses and drink. If a band is not present, all will rise to attention after the Vice/PMC 
says, "Gentlemen, the Queen", raise their glasses, repeat the toast, "the Queen", and drink. All 
will then take their seats. When representatives of other countries are present and additional 
toasts are proposed, the same procedure will be followed.
54. After the toasts, the PMC may indicate that it is permissible to leave for a break and 
return at a specified time. If there are any speeches, they follow at this point. Anyone who 
speaks, when referring to the PMC, shall address him as "Mr. President" or “Mr. PMC”.

192
55. Indication that the dinner is officially over will be given by the PMC standing up until 
noticed by all members. It is customary for the members to stand until the Wing Commander and 
guests have left the room.
56. Members shall retire to the lounge and should not leave the Mess without paying respects 
to the Wing Commander or senior officer present and the PMC.
57. During the post-dinner amusements in the designated lounge, friendship and high spirits 
are in order, but rowdiness in all its verbal and physical aspects is not in any way acceptable.
Mess Decorum
58. The decorum of Mess membership shall be commensurate with the conduct expected of 
an Officer. Furnishing and utilization of associated accoutrements throughout the Mess are 
property of all Mess members and should be treated accordingly. Membership in the Mess does 
not grant authority to abuse or misuse the facilities of the Mess. Loud, boisterous, vulgar and/or 
disruptive behaviour will not be tolerated. Swearing will not be tolerated. Members are reminded 
that the decorum of the Mess membership is the responsibility of all mess members and that 
unacceptable behaviour by a member may lead to restriction of Mess privileges in accordance 
with the Mess by-laws.
Automobile Parking
59. Personal automobiles may only be parked in the parking lots adjacent to Building 162 
and behind Building 160. Students may not use the parking lots on the west or north sides of 
Building 160 during the weekdays as they are reserved for Bombardier and 2 CFFTS staff as 
well as visiting guests.
60. During winter months, plug-ins are available to living-in personnel. As part of the inclearance procedure at ARAMARK, students are required to become conversant with the 
location and use of various plug-ins throughout the Wing. Living-in students are reminded to not 
use plug-in parking spots that have not been allocated to them.
61. Students owning and driving automobiles are to become conversant with and conform to 
applicable Wing and provincial regulations concerning: obtaining a driver's license; car 
licensing; and, car registration.
Limitations on Personal Vehicles and Bicycles
62. Students owning and driving motorcycles on base are to become conversant with and 
conform to applicable Wing and provincial regulations concerning obtaining a driver's license, 
motor-cycle licensing, and motor-cycle registration.
63. Private motor vehicles shall not be dismantled or repaired in parking areas. 

193
64. Bicycle racks are provided at the rear of the Multi-Purpose Facility (BLDG 160) and in 
the main parking lot. Bicycles will be placed only on the rack provided at each building. They 
will not be stored in student rooms. A bicycle storage room has been designated for each floor 
during the off season.
65. For safety reasons, saluting is not required when riding a bicycle in uniform. However, 
students, when walking, shall salute officers on bicycles.
66. Bicycles, for the purposes of traffic regulations, are vehicles. All regulations applying to 
vehicles apply to bicycles. Bicycles shall not be ridden on footpaths and rules of the road apply.
67. Cyclists are strongly encouraged to wear a safety helmet at all times while riding 
bicycles.
General Barrack Regulations
68. These regulations apply to all students and in particular to living-in students. In addition 
to the orders contained here-in, all living-in students will adhere to 15 Wing Standing Order 28-
1, "Conditions of Occupancy for Single Quarters." 
69. Single/unaccompanied 2 CFFTS trainees will normally be assigned rooms in the NFTC 
Student Housing Facility by the Housing Office (Aramark). 
70. Students are not permitted to change rooms without prior permission of the Wing 
Housing Office (Aramark). The request shall be made through the student's Course Director. A 
march-out will be scheduled through Aramark. These can be done only on the actual day of the 
move. Students intending on moving out must contact Aramark no less than 30 days prior to the 
move.
71. Students shall not remove furniture from that room for which it is identified on the 
inventory.
72. Barrack damage will not be tolerated. Such occurrences shall be reported to the Adjt 
through the Course Director for the appropriate administrative and recoverable action. All 
barrack complaints shall be reported through the above chain of command. Unserviceable or 
damaged furniture will remain in the designated room and shall be reported as soon as 
practicable.
73. Halls and stairways are not to be used as storage areas.
74. Fire doors shall be kept closed during silent hours (1630 hrs - 0800 hrs).
75. Students must read the Fire Safety Plan for Building 162, located on the back of the door 
of each room. Contravening the Fire Safety Plan will lead to administrative sanctions, which 
could include charges under the Code of Service Discipline and eviction. 

194
76. All rooms in Building 162 are subject to no notice inspections, for fire and safety 
hazards, condition and general upkeep at the Wing Commanders discretion.
Student Rooms
77. Students are responsible for the safekeeping of their personal equipment. Rooms are to be 
kept locked when unoccupied.
78. Room layout is left to the discretion of the occupant; however, cleanliness, and good 
judgement shall prevail:
a. no bicycles are permitted in the buildings; other than in the storage rooms;
b. vacuum cleaners may not be removed from the buildings;
c. damage to furniture and/or fixtures will be recovered from the individual student 
who caused the damage;
d. mounting of pictures in student’s rooms or any decorating must be in accordance 
with Aramark regulations and procedures;
e. furniture is not to be abused or used for anything other than the designed purpose; 
and
f. waterbeds are only permitted in single occupancy rooms with the approval of the 
Wing Accommodation Manager (Aramark).
78. All living-in students shall observe the following points:
a. rooms are to be dusted regularly;
b. garbage cans must be emptied each morning;
c. windows are to be kept clean as required;
d. walls shall be kept clean;
e. clothing shall be hung neatly in the closets or stored in drawers;
f. books and mementoes may be kept on shelves but shelves must be kept neat and 
tidy; and
g. a nameplate will be placed on the outside of a member's door indicating their 
name and course number. 

195
Electrical Appliances
79. Students are supplied with fridges, microwaves, coffee makers, toasters and kettles. No 
other implements for cooking may be used in the room that are not listed in the Fire Safety Plan 
for Bldg 162, or specifically approved by the Fire Inspection Bureau in accordance with the Fire 
Safety Plan for Building 162. Air conditioners may be used during the summer months once 
permission is granted by Aramark on an individual basis. 
Furniture
80. Furniture will not be removed from the room. 15 Wing does not have storage space for 
privately-owned furniture. All students are required to store excess personal furniture at their 
own expense with a downtown company. 
Baggage Rooms
81. Large pieces of personal equipment such as trunks, suitcases, golf clubs and bicycles 
shall be stored in baggage rooms.
82. Flammable equipment may not be stored in quarters.
Private Firearms
83. Private firearms and offensive weapons shall not be kept in barracks. Living-in personnel 
shall register and store their private firearms or offensive weapons at the Military Police Section.
Private Telephone/Cable Television
84. Responsibility for installation and cancellation rests with the individual and their personal 
arrangements with SaskTel and/or the local cable supplier company.
Living Off Wing Policy
85. Permission to live off of the Wing while on course at 2 CFFTS is a privilege. This 
privilege may be denied or revoked if a student's performance is not maintained at a satisfactory 
level.
Marriages
86. If a student is contemplating marriage while on course, a memorandum of intentions must 
be submitted to the Course Director. Normally, leave will not be granted for marriage. NOTE: 
Students considering marriage should report to the Wing Orderly Room a minimum of one 
month prior to the marriage to receive information on documentation changes that must be made 
to accompany the new marital status.

196
Change of Address
87. Prior to any change of address either on or off the Wing, a change of address memo will 
be filled out at the 2 CFFTS OR. Ensure that your Course Director is aware of any such change. 
Unavoidable Delays Returning to 15 Wing
88. Students unable to return to the Wing due to unforeseen circumstances should carry out 
the following procedure:
a. contact the Wing Operator, phone # (306) 694-2222 and ask to speak to one of the 
following: Course Director, Deputy Flight Commander, Flight Commander, or 
Adjt. If after normal working hours, leave a voice message and establish contact 
as soon as possible on the first working day;
b. advise the person contacted of your whereabouts, reason for not returning to the 
Wing on time, and the length of time you expect to be delayed;
c. if a telephone is not available, proceed to the nearest civil authority (RCMP, City 
Police, etc.) for assistance; and
d. report to your CD immediately upon returning to 15 Wing. NOTE: This order is 
not to be interpreted as a convenient means to get additional time off.

197
CHAPTER 11 - STUDENT APPOINTMENTS AND TERMS OF REFERENCE
General
1. The Student Appointments (SA) described in this chapter have been created for Officer 
Development purposes. Students may be appointed to any SA while on course at 2 CFFTS.
2. Student Appointments have been grouped under the categories of course and flight-line.
3. The following course Student appointments will be made by the Course Director within 
two weeks of course commencement:
a. Course Senior;
b. Deputy Course Senior;
c. Course Entertainment/Sports Officer;
d. Course Project Officer;
e. Course Historian; 
f. Course Critique Officer;
g. Course Officer - Andrew Naismith Fund;
h. Course Crest Officer 
4. Duties associated with the above appointments are detailed in Articles 8.02 to 8.09.
5. The following flight line student appointments will be made by the respective Flight
Commander:
a. Senior Student;
b. Deputy Senior Student;
c. Student Flight Publications Officer;
d. Student BMS officer;
e. Student Unit Flight Safety Officer;
f. Student Flight Training Officer;
g. Student Entertainment/Sport Officer;

198
h. Student Security/Fire Prevention Officer;
i. Student Flight Historian;
j. Duty Student; and
k. Briefing Room Monitor.
6. Duties associated with the above flight line appointments are detailed in Articles 8.10 to 
8.20. These appointments, and others, are further detailed in individual Flight SOPs.
Course Senior
7. The Course Senior is appointed by and responsible to the Course Director.
8. Responsibilities: The Course Senior is responsible for:
a. calling the class to attention when a lecturer or Senior Officer arrives and leaves.
b. maintaining order when no instructor is in the classroom and ensure classroom 
equipment is handled with care.
c. ensuring classrooms are left tidy after each lecture.
d. checking the master ground school schedule located on the LAN before the first 
period and after each class. The course mailbox is to be checked at the same time. 
When a course has completed ground school, the mailbox shall be checked every 
day during lunch hour period;
e. contacting Program Services for instructions if the lecturer has not arrived within 
five minutes after the scheduled time of the lecture; and
f. maintaining familiarity with the disposal and whereabouts of all members of his 
course during working hours and report absenteeism and tardiness to his Course 
Director immediately (attempt to locate any absent members). Not applicable 
while course on flight line.
Deputy Course Senior
9. The Deputy Course Senior is appointed by and responsible to the Course Director.
10. Responsibilities: The Deputy Course Senior is responsible for:
a. familiarizing himself/herself with the duties of the Course Senior;
b. carrying out the duties of the Course Senior during their absence; and

199
c. assisting the Course Senior when required.
Course Entertainment/Sports Officer
11. The Course Entertainment/Sports Officer is appointed by and responsible to the Course 
Director.
12. Responsibilities: The Course Entertainment/Sports Officer is responsible for:
a. providing liaison as course representative to the Wing, school and mess functions 
(i.e.: Winter Carnival);
b. the overall planning and organising of course social functions;
c. co-ordinating the use of Wing or other facilities for course social/sports events;
d. soliciting and fostering course interest and support for all Wing, School and mess 
functions;
e. organizing the welcome party for the next incoming course, and ensuring that one 
of the Course Directors of the courses partaking in the welcome party will be
present; and
f. Other duties as assigned by the Course Director
Course Project Co-ordinator
13. The Course Project Co-ordinator is appointed by and responsible to the Course Director.
14. Responsibilities: The Course Project Co-ordinator is responsible for:
a. submitting a proposed course project and an alternate course project to the Cmdt 
for approval (via memorandum through the Course Director and Adjt);
b. planning, co-ordinating and overall management of the course project;
c. ensuring that the total cost of the project remains within reason. Any costs 
incurred not covered through donations will be covered by the course, and should 
not normally exceed $150;
d. ensuring that all course members participate to the maximum extent possible;
e. delegating tasks to and ensuring equal participation from course members;
f. keeping their Course Director informed as to the progress of the project; and

200
g. appointing a fellow student in the capacity of Deputy Course Project Coordinator, if required.
Course Officer - Andrew Naismith Fund
15. The Course Officer - Andrew Naismith Fund is appointed by and responsible to the 
Course Director.
16. Responsibilities: The Course Andrew Naismith Fund Officer is responsible for:
a. organizing a fund raising event in support of the Andrew Naismith Foundation;
b. planning and implementing the event with the support of course members, the 
course director, and the 2CFFTS Andrew Naismith Fund OPI on fundraising 
event;
c. ensuring appropriate requests on approvals are done through appropriate chain of 
command; and
d. collecting and submitting funds raised to the 2CFFTS Andrew Naismith Fund 
OPI with a Summary Report completed. This report is to include a detailed 
description on the event, break down of funds raised, successes from the 
fundraiser, and points to improve upon.
Course Crest Officer
17. The Course Crest Officer is appointed by and responsible to the Course Director.
18. Responsibilities: The Course Crest Officer is responsible for:
a. designing a crest that will be worn on the flight suit in accordance with 1 Can Air 
Div orders;
b. submitting the crest design for approval to the Cmdt via the Adjt; and
c. coordinating the financing and purchase of the crest.
Senior Student
19. The Senior Student is appointed by the Flight Commander and is responsible to the 
Deputy Flight Commander.
20. The Senior Student is a member of one of the senior courses.
21. Responsibilities: The Senior Student is responsible for:

201
a. setting an example in dress, deportment and professionalism;
b. advising fellow students with regard to proper military procedures and etiquette;
c. assigning duty student, briefing room monitors, and other student responsibilities 
as required;
d. ensuring that the duty students, briefing room monitors and other students 
assigned various responsibilities and complete their duties in an effective and 
thorough manner;
e. ensuring that the ready room is kept clean and tidy;
f. monitoring the Duty Student and providing guidance if required;
g. ensuring that monthly student activity reports are submitted to D/Flight 
Commander for each student;
h. ensuring that the Aircrew Allowance forms are completed accurately and 
promptly at the end of each month. The completed forms should be turned in to 
the Deputy Flight Commander prior to the end of the first week of the next 
month;
i. ensuring that the D/Senior student is fully briefed and prepared to carry out their 
duties if absent on cross-country training or on leave; 
j. assigning weekend practice FTD slots with D/Senior student IAW the flight 
schedulers guidance; and
k. completing any tasks as assigned by the Flight Commander, Deputy/Flight 
Commander or Scheduler.
Deputy Senior Student
22. The Deputy Senior Student is appointed by the Flight Commander and is responsible to 
the Senior Student and the Deputy Flight Commander.
23. The Deputy Senior Student should be a member of one of the senior courses; however, 
this does not preclude the selection of a mature and responsible member one of the intermediate 
courses.
24. Responsibilities: The Deputy Senior Student is responsible for:
a. setting an example in dress, deportment and professionalism;
b. advising fellow students with regard to proper military procedures and 
responsibilities as required;

202
c. assisting the Senior Student when necessary, and in their absence, assuming the 
duties and responsibilities;
d. ensuring that a replacement is appointed to carry out their duties if absent on 
cross-country or on leave; and
e. completing additional tasks as assigned by the Flight Commander, Deputy Flight 
Commander, Flight Scheduler, or Senior Student.
Student Flight Publications Officer

responsible to the Flight Publications Officer.
26. Responsibilities: The Student Flight Publications Officer shall be responsible for:
a. amending publications under the direction of the Flight Publications Officer; 
b. picking up and distributing pubs to the flight for each issue period; and
c. performing any additional duties assigned by the Flight Publications Officer.
Student BMS Officer

28. Responsibilities: The Student BMS Officer has signing authority for receipt of BMS

a. receipt and storage of weekly BMS;
b. ensuring the canteen area is maintained in a clean and orderly manner;
c. ensuring that a replacement is appointed to carry out BMS duties if absent on 
cross-country training or on leave; and
d. any additional duties as assigned by the Flight BMS Officer.
Student Flight Training Officer

203

log books and the X board;

c. assisting the Flight Training Officer with implementing and updating the Flight 
"Bad Weather Program" as required;
d. performing any additional duties assigned by the Flight Training Officer.
Student Entertainment/Sports Officer

32. Responsibilities: The Student Entertainment/Sports Officer is responsible for:
a. assisting the Flight Entertainment/Sports Officer in planning, organizing, coordinating and staging all Flight social functions including solo parties and 
staff/student mixed parties;
b. assisting in the preparations for solo parties in particular, ensuring that solo 
certificates and Big 2 crests are on hand for the occasion;
c. assisting in the planning, co-ordination and conduct of the Bad Weather Sports 
program;

e. performing any additional duties assigned by the Flight Entertainment/Sports 
officer.
Student Security/Fire Prevention Officer
33. The Student Security/Fire Prevention Officer is appointed by the Flight Commander and 
responsible to the Flight Security/Fire Prevention Officer.
34. Responsibilities: The Student Security/Fire Prevention Officer is responsible for:
a. assisting the Flight Security/Fire Prevention Officer to ensure that the security and 
fire practices of the students within the Flight are in accordance with 2CFFTS 
Orders, 15 Wing Standing Orders and Wing Directives;

204
b. ensuring that the students' Flight key is returned each day to its place of normal 
storage;
c. advising the Flight Security Officer on all matters pertaining to security and fire 
prevention;
d. ensuring that a Fire Safety Check is performed at the completion of each working 
day;
e. ensuring all required items on the Duty Student security checklist are completed 
at the end of each working day;
f. assisting in practice fire drills as required and reporting deficiencies in training 
and/or equipment to the officer-in-charge; and
g. performing any additional duties assigned by the Flight Security/Fire Prevention 
Officer.
Student Flight Historian
35. The Student Flight Historian is appointed by the Flight Commander and is responsible to 
the Flight Historian.
36. Responsibilities: The Student Flight Historian is responsible for:
a. assisting the Flight Historian in the preservation and maintenance of the Flight's 
history;
b. ensuring that photographs are taken to record such Flight events as: solo parties, 
sports days, farewells and seasonal parties;
c. maintaining the Flight album;
d. assisting the Flight Historian with the preparation and submission of articles in the 
Wing newspaper; and
e. performing any additional duties assigned by the Flight Historian.
Duty Student
37. The Duty Student is appointed by the Flight Commander and is responsible to the 
Scheduler and the Senior Student.
38. Responsibilities: The Duty Student is responsible for:

205
a. log ready room computer to the LAN and configure system for the morning 
briefing;
b. call the room to attention upon the arrival of the Flight Commander or any officer 
of the rank of Major or higher;
c. keep the ready room and canteen area clean and tidy & labelling/sorting box 
lunches IAW flight policy;
d. ensuring the student and staff mail is distributed;
e. ensuring that a replacement is appointed to carry out their duties prior to leaving 
the flight for any reason;
f. carrying out a fire and security check of the flight at the end of each working day; 
and
g. any other tasks assigned by the Senior Student/Flight Scheduler.
Briefing Room Monitor
39. The Briefing Room Monitor is appointed by the Flight Commander and is responsible to 
the Senior Student.
40. Responsibilities: Students on the junior course will be assigned responsibility for the 
upkeep and tidiness of a specific briefing room. These specific duties include ensuring their 
name is displayed in their assigned briefing room(s), ensuring an adequate supply of training aids 
is available in each room, and cleaning the whiteboards regularly.

ANNEX D
2 CFFTS SECONDARY ADP DUTIES
Unit ADP Officer (UADPO)
1. The Unit ADP Officer is appointed by the Commandant, and is chiefly responsible to the 
Commandant on issues regarding 2 CFFTS ADP. The UADPO is also directly responsible to the 
Commandant in all matters dealing with the security of the systems within the unit. He/she is 
responsible for all SADP Reps.
2. Responsibilities include, but are not limited to:
a. recommendations to Commandant regarding any ADP issue when required;
b. advise for Section ADP Reps;
c. establishing and maintaining policies approved by the Commandant, including 
amendments to 2 CFFTS orders Chapter 5 (ADP Admin);
d. co-ordinating the security measures with:
(1) the office and system staff;
(2) Wing MAN ISSO; and
(3) the WSAMPO.
e. familiarising himself/herself with system security safeguards;
f. responsible for the preliminary investigation of all IS security incidents;
g. ensuring commercial software licenses are not violated;
h. ensuring unit personnel are aware of IS security requirements;
i. familiarizing oneself with and remaining current with the Wg MAN IS Security 
Orders;
j. reporting all ADP security incidents to the Military Police; and
k. maintaining possession of the ADP Security Accountability Form.

212
Section ADP Representative - (SADP Rep)
3. The SADP Rep is appointed by the Flight/Section Commander, and is responsible to the 
UISSO, with regard to ADP security, and to the Flight/Section Commander on all other matters.
4. All sections and flights will have their own ADP representative.
5. Responsibilities include:
a. making trouble calls for any malfunctioning computer;
b. providing direction to ADP users on the handling of media;
c. becoming familiar with system security safeguards;
d. reporting ADP security incidents to the Unit ADP Officer;
e. promoting security awareness to all users;
f. educating users on the respect of the confidentiality of information on or relating 
to the system including personnel data, passwords, keys and codes; and
g. policing computers to ensure that users respect the restriction of use and copying 
of copyright-protected programs and data.

• ANNEX F
2 CFFTS PERSONAL RELATIONSHIP POLICY
References: A. DAOD 5019-1, Personal Relationships - Canada.ca
B. DAOD 5014-0, Workplace Harassment and Violence Prevention - Canada.ca
C. CANFORGENS 076/26 CMP 034/26 281844Z APR 26
D. Canadian Armed Forces Ethos: Trusted to Serve - Canada.ca
Purpose
1. Inappropriate personal relationships can negatively impact morale, undermine confidence
in leadership and reduce operational effectiveness. Due to the unique instructor–student dynamic 
at 2 CFFTS, strict expectations are required to preserve professionalism, fairness, and the 
integrity of the Chain of Command. 
2. Additionally, personal relationships among staff - even when appropriate - can still create 
real or perceived imbalances in the workplace. These relationships may be viewed by students or 
peers as preferential treatment, potentially eroding trust and cohesion. Furthermore, should such 
relationships deteriorate, they can negatively affect workplace dynamics, morale, and overall unit 
effectiveness.
Policy Overview
3. Romantic or sexual relationships between staff and students are strictly prohibited
due to the power imbalance.
4. Personal relationships may exist or develop; however, they must not compromise:
a. Professional conduct;
b. Objectivity in supervision or assessment;
c. Good order and discipline.
5. Pre-existing relationships (family or otherwise) must be managed transparently.
Definition
6. As per Ref A.
Disclosure Requirements
7. Any relationship could create a real or perceived bias in the workplace and therefore is 
subject to this policy and must be disclosed.

215
8. If members are uncomfortable reporting directly to their immediate supervisor, they may 
disclose the relationship through the Flt Comd, CFI, DCO, SCWO, to the Cmdt. 
Regardless of the avenue of disclosure, the member’s Flt Comd will be informed to ensure 
appropriate monitoring and management.
Management of Relationships
9. All disclosed relationships will be assessed by the Chain of Command. Mitigation 
measures are required to prevent bias and preserve fairness, as well as operational 
effectiveness and personal integrity, not to penalize members who act in good faith.
Non-Compliance
10. Failure to disclose relationships may result in investigation and administrative or 
disciplinary action as appropriate.
11. Inappropriate conduct will be dealt with in accordance with relevant CAF policies.
Acknowledgement
12. All 2 CFFTS personnel are required to:
a. Read Ref A, understand and comply with this policy;
b. Disclose any personal relationships, if applicable.

“C” CATEGORY MONITORING FORM
1. The intent of C CAT Monitoring is to prepare for the upgrade to a B CAT Instructor. 
2. The Instructional Scenario training events are mandatory training prior to the B CAT 
upgrade. They may been flown on a Supervised Trainer with Standards or as a C Category 
Training flight with any B Sup or A CAT instructor. 
3. Recording Real Student Experience is a simple way to ensure you have completed 
instructional missions in all phases of the syllabus. Flying a variety of mission types frequently 
will best prepare you for the B CAT upgrade.
4. The Administrative Tasks are optional for the B CAT upgrade but provide valuable 
experience as you prepare for duties as an IR or PRB chair.

PHASE 2 QFI PROFICIENCY FORM
1. Phase 2 QFI’s must maintain a high level of proficiency on all sequences testable on the 
FIS Proficiency Check. They may also qualify to practice Phase 3 QFI syllabus sequences 
beyond those taught during FIS, for the sake of professional development or in anticipation of 
qualifying as a Phase 3 QFI. These sequences are listed below.
2. To qualify to practice the following Phase 3 syllabus sequences solo, a Phase 3 QFI must 
certify the staff member as safe for solo on that sequence during a Supervisory Trainer, C CAT 
Trainer, B CAT Trainer, or during FIS syllabus training. The certification “safe to practice _____ 
solo” shall be indicated on the mission card in the overall comments and recorded below. 
3. Phase 2 QFIs are not required to seek certification to practice additional Phase 3 
sequences solo. However, once certified, they are responsible to perform these additional 
sequences to a Level 3 on any future APC or Supervisory Trainer.

ANNEX H
SOLO AUTHORIZATION FORM – HVD II
Duty Ops Officer:
I hereby authorise _________________ to act as aircraft captain for the purpose of 
 Rank/Name/Initials
(mission description) on (Date).
I certify that the requirements of 2 CFFTS Flying orders Art 1.08 have been met and that the 
student has not flown the FTD in the last 12 hours. I request that you authorise the msn on my 
behalf.
PROHIBITED SEQUENCES
1. line-up behind other aircraft on the runway;
2. rolling take-off;
3. spins or U/A recoveries;
4. practice stall recoveries below 10,000’ MSL;
5. practice stall recoveries where symptoms are permitted to progress beyond stick shaker 
and/or 15 and one half units of AOA;
6. aerobatics below 10,000’ MSL;
7. practice forced landing in which high key cannot be reached;
8. flying outside the traffic pattern during CH8A, except in an emergency situation;
9. exiting the runway at FOXTROT taxiway when using rwy 11L, and exiting at runway 
03/21 when using runway 29L or R;
10. vertical aerobatics or Split-S;
11. closed patterns from the outer to the inner and vice versa;
12. take-off or landing on Runway 03/21;
13. closed pattern to high key (until certified in progress book);
14. intentionally exceeding 4G; and

223
15. landing when there is greater than 15 kt crosswind component.
16. practice approaches to ‘local’ uncontrolled aerodromes below 200’AGL;
17. early out to pattern or area;
18. any sequence that has not previously been performed to a level 3 or higher; and 
19. any sequence not included in the trg plan.
