ANNEX L
COURSE DIRECTORS’ GUIDE
1. Course Directors (CD) are the primary administrative link between students and staff 
during their course at 2 CFFTS in conjunction with Flt Comds and the 2 CFFTS Adjt. Flt Comds 
will be assigned by the Cmdt or CFI to provide personnel for selection to CD. The Course 
Director Guide (Annex L) provides guidance and reference for CDs to ensure standardized 
administration and guidance on disciplinary procedures. Upon being selected, it is imperative 
that Course Directors (CDs) become familiar with the 2 CFFTS Student Standing Orders 
(SSOs),CD terms of reference, and the 2 CFFTS Orders (with particular attention to Book 3).
2. CDs are to ensure that their FlightPro status and ADP access has been changed with their 
new position. This can be confirmed through the 2 CFFTS Adjt.
3. Student In-Clearance Week. Prior to the arrival of the assigned course, CAE Program 
Services will schedule Course syllabus execution in conjunction with GBTS, 2 CFFTS and 15 
Wing. The following events will be scheduled by CAE Program Services during the first week, 
normally beginning at 0800hrs on the Monday:
a. CD’s Welcome Brief. This outlines areas of dress and deportment, military 
courtesy and other topics outlined in SSOs;
b. CD Interviews. Prior to the interview it is advisable to have students fill out the 
Student Information Form located in the CD folder at 
\\Hercules\coursedirectors\Student In-Routine. The intent of the interview is to get 
to know the background and potential issues that may affect the completion of the 
course;
c. Official Welcome Brief. Scheduled by Cmdt’s EA and CAE Program Services. 
CDs are to be in attendance for all welcome briefs; and,
4. Flight Assignments. Courses will be divided amongst the Flights during their first week 
by the CFI. Call signs shall be the letter designation of the flight, followed by a 3 digit code. The 
first number represents the course serial, with the remaining two indicating the SP’s alphabetical 
ordering on the course. For example, the 4th student of course 1206 shall be B-606. The 11th 
student on course 1209 shall be C-911. 
5. Student Appointments. The CD will assign course appointments as per the 2 CFFTS 
Student Standing Orders. The Course Senior and deputy should be determined early to provide a 
single point of contact. This is an opportunity for the students to display initiative. Other duties 
within the flights, 2 CFFTS and the Wing will also be available to ensure all students have an 
opportunity to demonstrate leadership and initiative.
6. Course Crest Officer (Crest O). The Crest O can be appointed early as ordering of the 
patches can take 4-6 weeks. Ensure that the crest is in good taste and follows the guidelines as 

235
outlined in the Wing Standing Orders (WSO 1000-1) and the 1 Cdn Air Div Orders (CADO Vol 
1-006). The Crest O will prepare a memo addressed to the 2 CAD DCWO (via WCWO thru 
CoC) that explains the concept and meaning of the various elements. The memo shall be 
accompanied by a colour and subdued rendition of the crest.
7. Student Training File. The primary reference for the training file is the CPACM 14-
240(2) Part III, Ch 10. It is imperative that these files are set up properly since these files will 
follow the student throughout their training to wings standard. If the student has completed PFT 
at 3 CFFTS the student will have a training file which should be reviewed by their CD. For 
students without training files (bypass), the FTS OR will construct the files. These training files 
will be held with the Adjt. It is important that the training file contain the following:
a. academic summary (including academic counselling forms);
b. Course Report (CF377);
c. officer development (OD) (FLODAs);
d. disciplinary documentation (UASARs, Statements of Formal Warning, and 
counselling documentation);
e. IRs/PRBs; and
f. ALL administrative documentation (posting messages, bypass letters, etc.).
8. Student Leave. The CD is responsible to direct and supervise leave in conjunction with 
Flt Comds and the Adjt. Leave procedures for students including recommendations and 
requirements are contained in the 2 CFFTS Orders. There may be requirements for students to 
use compassionate leave. This may be granted by the Cmdt on a case-by-case basis. The purpose 
of this leave is to provide the student with leave in exceptional circumstances IAW the Leave 
Policy Manual. The CD should interview the student to assess the circumstances and brief the Flt 
Comd who will brief the Cmdt on the student’s circumstances.
9. Physical Development and Conditioning (PD&C). Guidelines for PD&C are contained in 
the 2 CFFTS SSO. The CD in conjunction with the Adjt will monitor student attendance at 
PD&C sessions to ensure the requirements are being met. Monthly reports will be sent by PSP 
staff. Any inconsistencies without adequate explanation shall be administered through UASAR 
and remedial trg.
10. Officer Development (OD). CDs will ensure the accurate completion of the mid-course 
and end-course FLODAs in a timely manner as per 2 CFFTS Orders. Individual QFIs should 
write the FLODAs with the CD correlating and ensuring standardized grading in conjunction 
with D/Flt Comds. Guidance on the FLODA completion and grading can be found later in this 
annex. Flt Comds and CDs shall ensure students are debriefed on FLODAs and are given 
adequate time to improve unsatisfactory behaviour.

236
11. Course Project. Each course is responsible to undertake a project as a gesture of 
appreciation that will benefit the school, Wing and/or City of Moose Jaw. The Course Project O 
will use this as an opportunity to display their organizational skills. A memo will be submitted to 
the CFI through the CD and Adjt detailing the nature and extent of the project. Cost, course 
member involvement, and time allocated must be considered in choosing a course project. In 
addition to the course project, a fundraiser for the Andrew Naismith Memorial Fund shall be 
organized.
12. Discipline. Although Flt Comds will generally be directly involved in the disciplining of 
students within their flight, the CD should make recommendations and proceed with the 
administration required. The measure used to discipline students is addressed in the 2 CFFTS 
Orders pertaining to student counselling and remedial training. These measures include:
a. Undergraduate Aircrew Significant Activity Report (UASAR) (Annex M);
b. Statement of Formal Warning (Annex N);
c. Academic or Flying Discipline Counselling Form (Annex O); and
d. CAF disciplinary actions.
13. If the student informs the CD of any significant incident, ensure that the Chain of 
Command (CoC) is informed as soon as possible and any subsequent disciplinary actions are 
taken as described above. Students may need to make court appearances. The CD is responsible 
to inform the CoC and escort the student. 
14. Cease Trg (CT) and Voluntary Withdrawal (VW). The CD shall be prepared to counsel 
and advise students faced with being CT’d or requesting VW. Information on methods of 
counselling can be found in Module 13 of the Flight Instructor Handbook. If unsure of 
information, seek guidance from a Flt Comd, the Adjt, or the CFI. Do not hesitate to recommend 
the student talk to WSWO, Chaplain or PSO. Students that choose to VW must submit a memo 
to the Cmdt through the CD stating the reasons which led to their decision. The Cmdt will 
interview the student prior to convening a PRB to be chaired by the CD. 
Course Completion Procedure
15. When a student has either completed flying training, been cease-trained or voluntarily 
withdrawn from course, Flt Comds are to ensure that the following administrative steps have 
been carried out within three working days:
a. the students flying hrs as depicted in FlightPro and the logbook must be in 
agreement;
b. Course Report (CF377) must be completed and forwarded to the CD, along with 
the student’s logbook;

237
c. the appropriate CD, Adjt and Cmdt’s EA shall be notified that the student has 
completed this phase of flight training; and
d. the student shall be instructed to meet with the Adjt in order to complete the PH II 
course completion checklist.
16. CDs will ensure that the logbook and End of Course Report (CF377) are forwarded to the 
Cmdt through the EA.

Course Reports (CF 377) - General
17. The following flying times shall be contained on student Course Reports from 2 CFFTS 
as applicable:
a. Ph II: Total Course Duration, Dual, Solo, ineffective time; and
b. Ph III: Total Course Duration, Dual, Solo, ineffective time.
18. Annex L contains guidance for writing the Course Report (CF377). Once a student has 
completed a course, the CD will work with the D/Flt Comd to complete the course report. The 
CD will submit all the completed Course Reports to the Adjt NLT five working days after the 
last student has completed training for processing. The order of signatures shall be the Cmdt, Flt 
Comd and lastly, the SP. In circumstances where this time line is not reasonable then a new 
deadline shall be agreed upon by the Adjt.
19. The Course Report will be distributed to the student by the 2 CFFTS Student 
Administrator (CT’d/VW students and FIS CMs by Cmdt’s EA) as expeditiously as possible 
after the course completion date. 
Course Reporting (CF377) - Linguistic Ability
20. The ability of officers to express themselves in either or both official languages has an 
important bearing on selection for positions that require linguistic ability. Therefore, reporting 
officers at 2 CFFTS should comment on observed linguistic ability in Section 14 of the Course 
Report (CF 377) whenever possible.
21. When student pilots are taking instruction in their secondary language, reporting officers 
should comment on the student's effort to overcome any difficulties in self-expression or in 
comprehending instructions. When an assessment of ability or effort has been made, the student's 
primary language must be clearly stated. It is equally important to state the language in which 
course instruction was given and the language in which comprehension or self-expression is 
being assessed.
22. In summary, whenever comments on a student's ability to comprehend instruction are 
made in Sections 14 and 17 of the Course Report (CF377), they shall include statements as to:
a. the student's primary language - English, French or other (to be specified);
b. the language of instruction on the course - English or French; and,
c. the language in which comprehension or self-expression is being 
assessed - English, French, or both.
Canadian Student Pilots - Employment Selection

240
23. Course Selection. Selection normally occurs before all students have finished crse 
although usually at least three tests have been completed by all students. In preparation for the 
selection meeting, the CFI will inform the CD of the course slot allotment to assist the CD in 
making recommendations for selection. Student interviews shall be conducted to finalize student 
posting preferences. The CD will confirm a date and time for the selection meeting with the 
Cmdt’s Secretary and CFI and will prepare the briefing using. Discussion between the CD and 
Flt Comds prior to the meeting may enable fine tuning of the selection. The Cmdt will chair the 
selection meeting however the CD will be the primary presenter. The CD must ensure he has 
adequate information (FLODAs, etc) to react to any discussion about particular student 
selections. Following selection, the CD will prepare and submit the course selection memo to the 
Cmdt’s secretary for signature. In addition, the CD will equally be responsible to inform the 2 
CFFTS Student Administrator of the requirement to load students selected for Harvard II PH III 
trg onto the next available High-G Indoctrination Course. The CD should be prepared to speak to 
the following points during a selection:
a. background and justification for OD scores;
b. explain circumstances surrounding failed tests or unusually low grades (what was 
the error, were there any contributing external factors); and
c. does the student have any previous flying experience.
24. Selection Boards are held to select pilots for Rotary Wing Training, Multi-Engine 
Training, and Jet Training. Selection Boards are chaired by the Cmdt and normally consist of the 
CFI, Adjt, Ops O, Flt Comds, CStdsO and the appropriate CD. The CD is responsible for 
supplying each Board member with a copy of the Course Merit List, and a short narrative on 
each student identifying those factors that make that student a viable candidate for a particular 
form of employment.
25. Selection should be made using the following criteria:
a. the exigencies of the CF;
b. the trainee's demonstrated abilities;
c. the trainee's performance; and
d. the trainee’s employment preferences.
26. Specific details concerning the methodology of posting selection is contained in the CD 
Guide.
27. Course Directors shall ensure that students on their course selected for jet training are 
loaded on the Centrifuge course as soon as practical following the selection board. 

241
28. Ph III Wings Graduation Parade. The graduates will do most of the coordination for the 
graduation parade, however the Adjt is ultimately responsible for the conduct of the parade. A 
graduation parade planning guide is located at SCommon\\Hercules (P:)\Phase III Graduation 
Planning Folder for ease in planning including guidelines for trophies and awards.
29. Wings Graduation Formal Dinner. The graduates will organize the details of the dinner in 
conjunction with the OMess PMC and Mess Manager. The Cmdt’s AA shall give necessary 
guidance with planning the dinner and invitation of head table guests. Course videos are 
becoming the norm at dinners, however it is the responsibility of the CD to ensure course videos 
are tasteful and do not contain any prohibited footage.
Course Critiques
30. The student course critique is a valuable tool, necessary to assist the organization in 
identifying and resolving administrative and training issues that may arise throughout the course.
31. It is not the intent of this outline to restrict those areas worthy of comment on a course 
critique, however, guidelines and suggestions are offered:
a. criticism of a department's personnel or their functions should be backed up by 
specifics such as name, time, and place. This evidence should not be detailed in 
the critique but should be at hand to provide justification for a criticism. Most 
often such criticism can be rectified if the information is passed to the appropriate 
Course Director for immediate handling; and
b. the critique should be written to assess the overall system, not to evaluate the 
traits of specific individuals. It is only when truly special recognition is warranted 
that an individual's name should be mentioned in a critique. In no instance should 
criticism of an individual person occur.
32. The Course Director for each course, upon course commencement, will select a Course 
Critique Officer. It will be the duty of the appointed student to co-ordinate the timely submission 
of the critique and act as point of contact should the school require clarification on certain items 
of their critique.
33. The course critique will consist of two parts in the format of an online survey. The first 
will envelop areas of CAE responsibility (ground school, simulators, welcome packages etc. The 
second will cover the remaining aspects of student training to include flight line OD, instruction 
and tests. In both instances, the online surveys will be administered by CAE Program Services. 
The deadline for CAE online surveys is within a week of receiving an email notification from 
CAE Program Services.
34. All criticisms should be constructive in nature and, where possible, a solution should be 
proposed. When a particular facility is operating efficiently, a statement such as "no comment" is 
sufficient.

242
35. In the event that it is deemed necessary by the Cmdt, a course debriefing during the final 
week of the course will be scheduled to discuss the critique points and what action is to be taken. 
All submissions are to be handled to ensure that misconceptions are not left in their minds which 
would render the critique procedure as meaningless.

243
COURSE REPORT (CF 377) WRITING GUIDELINES
1. The purpose of a Course Report is to provide a summary of the overall flying and OD 
assessments and potential:
a. DO:
• be concise (attempt to use 100 words or less);
• comment on above and below average performance;
• use proper English;
• use FLODAs to assist in OD portion;
• be both objective and honest; and
• finish with a comment on the potential of the trainee for either the Advanced 
Crse or future postings, as applicable.
• Provide linguistic ability feedback for Non-English Students (1.06)
b. DON'T:
• be verbose, repetitive;
• repeat what can be determined from the grading;
• use jargon or abbreviations; and 
• forget to summarize student's potential.
2. CFAO 26-12 specifically outlines the requirements and methodology in preparing crse 
reports. The following grades will be given based on student performance:
a. "A" - achieved all performance objectives without difficulty (overall flying 
average of 4.0 or better) and demonstrated exceptional ability in some areas;
b. "B" - achieved all performance objectives with little difficulty (overall flying 
average of 3.0 – 3.99) or any test fail. Some weak areas and some strong areas 
which offset each other; and
c. "C" - achieved all performance objectives but had difficulty in some areas. This 
would be defined as any phase rated as Low Standard or overall flying average 
less than 3.0.
3. Enter the applicable course codes as follows:
a. Phase II Hvd 102192;
b. Phase III Hvd 102193;
c. Hvd FIC 102194;
d. Hvd Transition 102195 (ME/Helo Transition to FIC);
e. Hvd Conversion 102196 (Course for utility captaincy).

244
4. Phase grading is based on the following:
a. Marginal 0.00 – 1.49;
b. Low Standard 1.50 – 2.49;
c. Achieved Standard 2.50 – 3.49;
d. High Standard 3.50 – 4.49; and
e. Standard Exceeded ≥ 4.50.
Examples of Flying Assessments:
5. High Standard Student (Phase II).
CH - HS / IF - SE / NAV - HS / NT - AS / FM - HS
OCdt Alpha's overall performance easily exceeded TP requirements for the Phase II Crse at 2 
CFFTS. In the last half of the crse he had a clear enough grasp on the tasks that he often 
recognized and corrected their own errors without guidance. Instrument flying was their 
strongest, where he showed very good attitude flying, coupled with excellent decision-making 
and situation awareness. OCdt Alpha's anticipation and accurate aircraft control made 
navigation an easily handled phase. Night flying was well handled. OCdt Alpha displayed a 
very high standard of flying ability throughout the crse; he has the potential to do very well on 
the Advanced Crse.
6. Achieved Standard Student (Phase III).
CH - A / IF - A / NAV - A / NT - A / FM - LS / CM - NG
OCdt Bravo applied himself well during their flying trg. He experienced minor problems 
with aerobatics, which he rectified. Their Instrument work was initially weak because of 
problems with attitude flying but improved with experience. Composite missions were well 
handled and he demonstrated their ability to maintain situational awareness. Their formation 
was below average due to station keeping errors. He quickly adapted to new and difficult 
situations, although at times he was slow to apply himself. He performed well under stress. 
With further effort and experience, OCdt Bravo will be an asset to the Canadian Forces.
7. Below Average Student (Phase II).
CH - LS / IF - LS / NAV - LS / NT - A / FM - LS
OCdt Charlie had difficulty meeting the requirements of the TP and extra time was required 
in all phases. He demonstrated good judgement, initiative and procedural knowledge 

245
throughout the crse, but their aircraft performance was consistently degraded by weak 
aircraft control. He had difficulty achieving acceptable levels of precision in formation and 
clearhood flying, and maintaining acceptable aircraft control when dealing with instrument 
and visual navigation problems. OCdt Charlie is highly motivated towards flying, but needs 
to persevere in developing their piloting ability if he is to be successful on the Multi-Engine 
Crse
FLIGHT-LINE OFFICER DEVELOPMENT ASSESSMENT (FLODA) FACTORS
8. Each assessment factor (AF) is defined in this section as a guide to rating a student's 
effectiveness. The definitions are not all-inclusive and reporting officers must use their 
judgement in applying them, but should not use any one relevant consideration in isolation as a 
basis for assigning a rating.
ASSESSMENT FACTORS
9. AF 1 - Appearance and Bearing. How does the student's appearance and bearing compare 
with other officers of the same rank? Some relevant considerations are as follows:
a. Do they maintain a high standard of dress on and off duty?
b. Do they maintain a high standard of personal hygiene and grooming?
c. Do they have good military bearing?
d. Do they act in a confident and professional manner?
e. Does their dress, grooming, bearing and manner reflect credit on themselves and 
the officer corps?
10. AF 2 - Attitude/Motivation. How does the student's attitude and motivation compare with 
that of other students of the same rank? Some relevant considerations are as follows:
a. Does the student display a positive and motivation to the course material and the 
manner in which it is presented?
b. Is the student putting forth sufficient effort to fully achieve their potential?
c. Does the student display an attitude which is supportive of their peers and 
superiors?
11. AF 3 – Cooperation. How effectively did the student cooperate with and gain the support 
of others in comparison with other officers of the same rank? Some relevant considerations are 
as follows:

246
a. How readily did they cooperate with others by offering advice or assistance, by 
being receptive to their ideas, advice, etc?
b. How successful was they in enlisting the cooperation of others by using tact, 
promoting understanding, encouraging their involvement, instilling teamwork?
c. Was they able to gain the cooperation of other organizations or personnel by 
establishing and maintaining a good rapport, using appropriate liaison methods?
d. Did they increase overall effectiveness in accomplishing goals, missions, tasks, 
etc, by promoting cooperation and maintaining good work-relationships?
12. AF 4 – Conduct. How does the student's conduct compare with that of other officers of 
the same rank? Some relevant considerations are as follows:
a. Do they conduct himself/herself in accordance with high moral standards and the 
customs of the Service at all times?
b. Is their behaviour on and off duty appropriate to the occasion?
c. Do they show proper respect to those senior in rank to him/her?
d. Is they gracious, tactful, diplomatic in associating with others?
e. Do they practice moderation with respect to the use of alcohol?
13. AF 5 – Reliability. How loyal and dedicated to the military profession is the officer in 
comparison to other students of the same rank? Can the officer be relied upon in any situation? 
Some relevant considerations are:
a. Does the student support the decisions and priorities of their superiors?
b. Does the student stand up for the reputation of their superiors, their unit, the 
Canadian Forces and their country?
c. Does the student's display of reliability set a good example for others to follow.
d. Does the student conscientious carry out all assigned duties and responsibilities? 
and
e. Does the student work extended hours or volunteer their own time, when 
necessary, to get the job done.
14. AF 6 – Initiative. How competently did the student make decisions and take action in 
comparison with other students of the same rank? Some relevant considerations are as follows:

247
a. Did the student make timely and competent decisions to the limit of their 
authority?
b. Did the student make timely and valid recommendations in areas beyond their 
authority?
c. Where circumstances dictated, did the student willingly make viable decisions on 
limited available information?
d. Did the student step in and take decisive action on their own to solve the problems 
or situations at hand?
e. Where the student's decisions, recommendations necessary, rational, correct, just, 
consistent and timely? and
f. Did the student propose new ideas or methods?
15. AF 7 - Learning Ability (Mental Alertness). How does the student's ability to 
comprehend, to reason, and to judge compare with other officers of the same rank? Some 
relevant considerations are as follows:
a. How alert and perceptive is the student?
b. How quickly and instinctively do they understand new ideas and concepts?
c. Do they learn from their mistakes and those of others?
d. How good is the student's memory, both short term and long term?
e. Does the student indicate that they has good powers of concentration in listening 
and thinking?
f. Does the student reason swiftly, deductively, logically, thoroughly and 
accurately?
g. Does the student show good common sense, initiative and adaptability in their 
judgments?
16. AF 8 - Self Confidence. How does the student's self-confidence compare with that of 
other students of the same rank? Some relevant considerations are as follows:
a. Is the student generally lacking in self-confidence and/or requiring of extensive 
encouragement to the extent that it is often detrimental to their job performance?
b. Does the student require occasional encouragement to complete assigned tasks in 
the most effective manner?

248
c. Is the student normally sufficiently self-assured in their dealings with peers and 
those who may be senior or junior?
d. Is the student always at ease, able to justify their actions and thorough in their 
convictions?
e. Is the student so over-confident of their own abilities that it is detrimental to their 
ability to complete tasks or deal with others?
17. AF 9 - Compliance with Rules and Orders. How readily did the student accept or assume 
their assigned responsibilities or additional duties in comparison with other students of the same 
rank? Some relevant considerations are as follows:
a. Did they accept or assume the full responsibilities of their task and get on with the 
job without prompting? 
b. Did they willingly accept or assume additional responsibilities without direction?
c. Did they carry out responsibilities and duties in an enthusiastic and energetic 
fashion without supervision?
d. Did they complete tasks promptly?
18. AF 10 - Power of Expression. How effectively did the student express himself/herself in 
discussions, or in giving orders, instructions, briefings, lecture, etc, in comparison with other 
officers of the same rank? Second language students are to be given due consideration. Some 
relevant considerations are as follows:
 
a. How confidently, coherently, concisely, and accurately did they present ideas, 
information, orders, instructions, problems and plans, etc?
b. Did they convey the proper feeling and intent in their presentations?
c. Were their presentations well received and understood? and
d. How competently did the student prepare written correspondence, memorandums, 
letters, appreciations, orders, instructions, directives, reports, manuals, and 
briefings, etc, in comparison with other officers of the same rank? Some relevant 
considerations are how accurate, logical, literate, clear, concise, and complete was 
the written work?
19. AF 11 – Integrity. How just, honest, and honourable is the student in comparison to other 
officers? Some relevant considerations are as follows:

249
a. Do they refuse to deceive others in any way, no matter what the circumstances or 
consequences?
b. Do they accept full responsibility for their own decisions and acts, including the 
blame for their mistakes?
c. Can they be relied upon to fulfil their obligations to the Service, their 
subordinates, peers, and superiors to the best of their ability?
d. Does their integrity set a good example for others to follow?
e. Do they treat others equitably and without prejudice, ensuring that every person is 
treated fairly?
20. AF 12 - Performance Under Stress. How effectively did the student perform under 
pressure or conditions of stress in comparison with other officers of the same rank. Some 
relevant considerations are as follows:
a. How did they react to extra, exceptional work demands, including the amount of 
effort and persistence they applied and the personal emotional control they 
maintained to achieve a high standard of work or meet non-routine but essential 
deadlines?
b. How did they react to real, non-routine physical danger to personnel or 
equipment, including the degree of personal emotional control they maintained, 
the timeliness and correctness of emergency procedures that they executed or 
directed, and how well did they control the situation and, where appropriate, other 
personnel?
c. How did they adapt to hardships, personal problems, changes in objectives, goals, 
procedures, or new situations, including the personal emotional control and the 
level of personal performance effectiveness that they maintained.
21. Overall Scoring will be assessed using the following grades:
a. 12-29 Unsatisfactory;
b. 30-32 Marginal;
c. 33-35 Low Average;
d. 36-40 Average;
e. 41-45 High Average;
f. 46-52 Above Average; and

250
g. 53-60 Superior

ANNEX P
AIRCREW FILE ADMINISTRATION
IRT (456) Administration
1. The following procedure shall be followed for IRT administration:
a. Complete 456/457 using MESH routing to 2 CFFTS UICP;
b. File name must follow File Naming Convention, with the date corresponding to 
the date flown. For example:
YYYYMMDD-PA-4520_6-2CFFTS-CFIR_CF457_RANK_NAME
20230525-PA-4520_6-2CFFTS-CFIR_CF457_CAPT_BLOGGINS
d. 2 CFFTS UICP will review, reroute documents for signatures, distribute 
paperwork to the Hvd Standard Supervisor and archive document to 15 Wing CR.
Category Ride (T-21) Administration
2. The following procedure shall be followed for T-21 administration:
a. The Flt Comd will submit the T-21 via MESH to the CStdsO prior to the Cat Ride 
being flown. This action will serve the purpose of the Flt Comd's 
recommendation. 
b. File name must follow File Naming Convention, with the date given by the 
anticipated day flown. For example: 
YYYYMMDD-PA-4520_6-2CFFTS-T21_RANK_NAME
 20230525-PA-4520_6-2CFFTS-T21_CAPT_BLOGGINS 
c. The CStdsO will reroute to the evaluating officer as required;
d. Following the ride, the testing officer will complete the T-21 and reroute 
document to the Hvd Standard Supervisor;
e. Hvd Standard Supervisor will review, reroute documents for signatures, distribute 
paperwork into the training files and archive document to 15 Wing CR.
Unsatisfactory IRT or Category Ride

255
3. The 456 or T-21 for an unsatisfactory check ride will be treated as Protected B. Use PKI 
encrypted e-mail rather than MESH to complete the administration. Submit a printed copy for 
the aircrew file.

ANNEX V
PROGRESS CARD ADMINISTRATION
General
1. This Annex adapts the guidance and procedures for progress card administration 
previously described in MSJPC-1 (28 Nov 12) and replaces that document. 
2. Progress cards are written and stored in FlightPro software, and are accessed through the 
“Student Results” section of the Personal Tools interface. Progress cards shall be completed for 
all dual trips (including tests).
3. Progress Cards should be completed immediately following the mission debriefing.
 
FlightPro Administration
4. The “Release Card” option in FlightPro shall only be ticked once the progress card is 
ready for Student/Course Member viewing. It is the responsibility of the last individual in the 
Workflow chain prior to the Student/Course Member to ensure that the Release Card option has 
been ticked. The Release Card option is not normally checked before this time. The primary 
instructor will be responsible for releasing any cards for Student/Course Member solo missions. 
5. The “Create Extra” option is used in conjunction with the “Extra Required?” drop down 
window in order to create additional missions related to the original syllabus mission. The 
options available are as follows:
a. ED – Extra Dual Mission;
b. RE – Repeat Mission and/or Re-Test;
c. RV – Review Mission; and
d. XS – Simulator Extra Dual.
6. The following guidelines apply:
a. Extra Dual Missions (Including FTDs):
(1) A workflow must be attached to the parent unsatisfactory mission that 
properly authorizes the Extra Dual training and clearly states the number 
and type of ED’s that are approved/created.
(2) If the Extra Dual mission(s) is the result of an Unsatisfactory rating on an 
already created Extra mission, then the Extra Dual(s) will be created from 
the original syllabus mission progress card.

266
b. Repeat Missions and Re-Tests:
(1) An applicable workflow must be attached to the original partial mission or 
Test that includes the appropriate authorization for the repeat mission(s).
(2) If the Repeat mission is the result of an incomplete Extra mission or 
additional Re-Test, then the new extra mission(s) will be created from the 
original syllabus mission progress card.
c. Review Missions:
(1) Review missions will be created from the last completed, original, 
syllabus mission progress card in the applicable phase.
(2) Composite review missions will be created off the last completed, original, 
syllabus mission in the student’s main phase of instruction. Additional 
phases of review sequences will be added to the progress card when 
completing the mission write-up. See the Tick and Flick guidelines below 
for additional information.
(3) The applicable Review workflow will be attached to the progress card that 
includes proper authorization for the review mission. See the guidelines 
below for additional information.
7. Every completed dual mission must have an overall score other than “None” assigned. 
The following guidelines apply:
a. For any Dual Syllabus Mission, assign the applicable overall grade as described in 
the appropriate ITP. 
b. No Grade (NG) shall be assigned for all ED, RV, DNCO, RE.
c. Any time a Grade is assigned or changed in the Score box drop down, select the 
“Save” option immediately to the right of the score, in order to avoid lost data. 
8. The following additional guidelines apply:
a. Extra Dual (FTD or Flight):
(1) Can only follow an Unsatisfactory trip.
(2) Must be approved in a workflow attached to the Unsatisfactory mission 
progress card leading to the Extra Dual requirements.
(3) All extra dual missions will be given a No Grade (NG) overall rating.

267
(4) All sequences will be graded as per normal missions.
(5) “Extra Dual” shall be written in the Overall Comments box along with 
whether it was acceptable or unacceptable. 
(6) The applicable ED workflow will be attached to all Extra Dual missions, 
which includes Flight Commander review and recommendations.
b. Unsatisfactory Trip:
(1) A Workflow must be attached that includes the Flight Commander’s 
review and number of ED’s assigned/created.
(2) If the previous dual syllabus mission in the same phase was 
Unsatisfactory, it is considered to be Unsatisfactory Flying Progress and 
actions IAW TP are to be carried out.
c. Marginal Trip:
(1) A marginal trip cannot follow an ED or another marginal trip in the same 
phase.
(2) If the trip prior to a test is Marginal, the trip shall be rated and 
administered as an Unsatisfactory mission.
(3) Marginal Mission workflow will be attached to the progress card that 
includes Flight Commander review and recommendations.
d. Unsatisfactory Sequence:
(1) Any unsatisfactory sequence in review work must be upgraded on the next 
trip in phase. 
(2) A test may not be attempted with outstanding unsatisfactory sequences in 
that phase.
e. DNCO/DPCO:
(1) A workflow must be attached to the progress card that includes the Flight 
Commander’s signature block for review.
(2) Sequences flown will be graded and commented on as required.
(3) Any DPCO mission card must be assigned a grade of NG. A RE mission 
will be created once completed it will be graded as NG and the applicable 
UMAS grade will be assigned to the original syllabus mission. A new 

268
workflow will be attached to that mission annotating the change to the 
mission grade.
(4) The reason for the DNCO/DPCO will be annotated in the Overall 
Comments section. 
(5) If the DNCO/DPCO mission was a syllabus solo, the follow-on mission 
must be approved and annotated in an attached workflow by the 
appropriate authority.
(6) A DNCO/DPCO workflow will be attached to the progress card that 
includes Flight Commander approval and recommendations. 
f. Review Trip:
(1) Sequences flown will be graded and commented on as required.
(2) If a solo is authorized next, it must be annotated in an attached workflow 
by the appropriate authority.
g. Composite Mission:
(1) Sequences flown will be added to the progress card and commented on as 
required.
h. Formation Solo or Supervised Solo:
(1) General overview of the mission and any significant events will be 
included in the Overall Comments box.
(2) A workflow must be attached to the solo progress card that includes 
authorization by the appropriate authority for supervised solo missions.
(3) If a solo is authorized following a Supervised Solo, it must be annotated in 
an attached workflow by the appropriate authority.
9. At least one workflow that includes the Student/Course Member’s signature block must 
be attached to every dual mission progress card. When creating a workflow the list of available 
workflows is grouped by event type under the Key column (Flight, Admin, Academics, AOT 
SET, FTD, etc.). Within each Key area, choose the Workflow title that applies to your specific 
mission (ED, Unsatisfactory, Instructor/Student, etc.). Multiple workflows can be assigned to 
one progress card. The following guidelines apply to workflow creation and completion:
a. Once a workflow has been assigned, the workflow steps are completed in the 
resulting “Workflow Inquiry” window. The upper Notes section is used to record 
any supporting details of the subject workflow. Individual 

269
remarks/requests/recommendations are also included in this section, by each 
applicable person in the workflow chain, for viewing and/or action by the next 
authority. The upper save button must be pressed to save any additional notes in 
this workflow header.
b. All syllabus missions with a rating of “A-LS” or higher will have, as a minimum, 
the Instructor/Student workflow attached which has the mission instructor and 
Student/Course Member’s signature blocks.
c. The action of approving the workflow, by each individual, is sufficient authority 
for the purposes of approval/action. (i.e. Flt Comd approves request for DNCO or 
ED by virtue of actioning/approving the workflow). 
d. Only applicable workflows will be attached to progress cards. Non applicable 
workflows can be deleted using the delete button in the Workflow Inquiry 
window.
e. Additions, changes or deletions to any completed progress card after release to the 
Student/Course Member requires a new workflow to be attached. This will alert 
the Student/Course Member of the change requiring that the new progress card be 
printed to replace the previous progress card in any hard copy record.
f. This area shows all effective/ineffective time allocated to the Student/Course 
Member at mission sign in. If the displayed time does not reflect total mission 
time on dual instructional missions then a flying code has been assigned to the 
instructor and will not be credited towards the Student/Course Member. Any 
corrections must be made from the Outcome tab of the mission sign-in screen.
10. The “Allocated Hours” area shows all effective/ineffective time allocated to the 
Student/Course Member at mission sign in. If the displayed time does not reflect total mission 
time on dual instructional missions then a flying code has been assigned to the instructor and will 
not be credited towards the Student/Course Member. Any corrections must be made from the 
Outcome tab of the mission sign-in screen.
11. The Tick and Flick Summary tab provides an overview of the entire phase of the progress 
card being viewed under the Details tab. QFIs are reminded that to obtain all pertinent 
information for mission planning, they must tick the “Show Mins” and “Common” box.
Grading and Comments
12. The Progress Card is the means by which QFIs carry out Regular Progress Review, and 
serves as the foundation for higher levels of progress review, such as an IR or PRB. It is a formal 
evaluation of student performance and shall be addressed to the Flight Commander and not to the 
student. 

270
13. QFIs should write the Progress Card with the Pilot Core Competencies in mind. 
Whenever possible, QFIs should provide evidence in their overall comments of exceptional or 
potentially inadequate Pilot Core Competencies. This evidence is important at higher levels of 
progress review, where a more thorough assessment of Pilot Core Competencies can occur. 
14. The Overall Grade shall be assigned in accordance with the definitions provided in the 
relevant ITP. 
15. The Overall Comments box should adhere to the following format:
a. If applicable, the first line of the overall comments shall contain any special 
comments, including DNCO, DPCO etc. For an Extra Dual, an overall rating of 
either Acceptable or Unacceptable shall be included.
b. The next line shall contain the weather using METAR or plain English.
c. The next line will describe the profile for using plain English or easily understood 
abbreviations.
d. A short paragraph shall be written to describe your overall impression of the trip. 
The format shall include student/course member strengths and areas for 
improvement. Evidence of deficient or exceptional Pilot Core Competencies 
should be included here. 
Example:
0.2 DNCO for assistance to another aircraft.
Weather/Runway: 13010G15KT 15SM FEW300 M02/M06, or Winds medium down the 
runway. SKC. RWY11
Profile: CYMJ.8 YMJ ODGOV VLN // NO233 A080 YMJ or Pattern first then Area 
work, spin and aeros.
2Lt Johnny continues to progress well. Area work was very solid, with aerobatics 
particularly well handled. Traffic pattern work was generally well done, however poor 
wind assessment caused issues during final turns and PFL. 
16. The grades assigned to each sequence shall be assigned in accordance with the definitions 
provided in the relevant ITP. 
17. On End of Block missions, where a new task or an upgrade sequence is not completed for 
reasons unrelated to the Student/Course Member’s performance, such as unserviceable 
equipment, unsuitable environmental conditions or the situation not presenting itself on the trip, 
the task shall be annotated as missed using the “Missed” button at the lower right corner of the 
scoring screen. The reason for not completing the task will be annotated in the Comments area 

271
for that task. A Missed Sequence workflow will be attached to the progress card that includes 
Flight Commander approval and recommendations.
18. Where a task on an airborne mission has been graded to a proficiency level equal to or 
higher than that required for subsequent FTD missions, then the FTD Instructor is not obligated 
to repeat that task for the purposes of upgrading. This will be annotated in the Comments area of 
the specific task to state that it was not flown due to a previous upgrade during the air mission.
19. Sequence comments shall adhere to the rules below to the maximum extent possible:
a. Comments are to be written as if addressed to the Flight Commander, for use by 
the follow-on QFI in error analysis and mission planning. 
b. Instructional guidance or praise is to be avoided; the comments must remain 
factual.
c. Avoiding the use of colloquialisms or slang. 
d. Comments shall reflect the level awarded.
e. Clearly and concisely state the error(s) (the what) and, where possible, make 
reference to cause factors (the why) in brief detail. 
f. Comments are required for all sequences graded as level 1, level 2, or below the 
EOB level. 
g. Comments are required for all sequences where a repeat was required or difficulty 
attaining the level was observed.
Example: Watch-Map-Ground (4): _______________
Example: Final Turn (3): High on glide path as a result of early perch for conditions of 
the day.
Example: Pt-Pt (2): Large DME error due to incorrect parallel check, verbal guidance 
required to correct.
Example: Normal Landing (1): IP took control on final attempt. SP excessive drift due to 
incorrect crosswind controls despite significant guidance.
20. Sequences flown as an interactive demo may be graded as a level 2 and indicated as such 
in the comment box.
21. When a sequence is only demonstrated on the mission the sequence shall not be graded 
and shall be marked by ticking the D column. If not demonstrated where required, then the 
missed button shall be used and the reason will be annotated in the Comments area for the task.
22. Appropriate ground-based emergencies shall be completed on each mission. The overall 
grade will be entered in the Emergencies row. The individual emergencies and their associated 

272
comments shall be added to the card using the Additional Categories option. In the event an 
emergency is not conducted, it is to be marked down as missed on the progress card, and a 
missed sequence workflow completed.
23. The private comments may contain mission planning information that pertains to what 
the QFI is recommending the student/course member should be exposed to on the next mission.
24. While the Student/Course Member is on course, the Course Comment tab provides an 
area for Student/Course Member Activity Record. Information should be entered in 
chronological order and should include the following:
a. Any Instructor change, including the date and reason for the change.
b. Any “Good Shows”, Airsickness, Incidents, Progress Rides, Test failures with 
particulars, Long Layoffs and Problem areas. The name of the person who 
entered each comment should be clearly identified with each entry and dated.
c. The details of the event should be clear, factual and to-the-point. Remember that 
the Student/Course member has access to this tab.

273

ANNEX X
PHASE 3 HVD QFI UPGRADE SYLLABUS
Ref: A. Briefing Note, CT156 Instructional Categories, 22 Nov 23
1. IAW Ref A, Cmdt 2 CFFTS may authorize a CT156 QFI to teach on additional training 
plans based on various criteria. This memo aims to solidify the upgrade process for Phase 2 to 
Phase 3 Hvd QFIs. 
2. The proposed training for the upgrade is as follows: 
a. A ground brief to cover PH3 mission handling (ACH, AFM, CM); 
b. An Advanced CH Trainer (50 % Proficiency 50% Instructional); 
c. An Advanced FM Trainer (50 % Proficiency 50% Instructional); and 
d. A Phase 3 QFI Instructional Check. 
3. Instructors will be nominated through CUB for Cmdt approval prior to commencing the 
Phase 3 Upgrade process. The scheduling of each trainer will be coordinated through FIS. Prior 
to the first trainer, the IP’s respective flight is to ensure Level 4 Proficiency in all advanced 
handling items from the Rear Seat. The Phase 3 Upgrade Sequences card below lists the 
mandatory sequences to be flown with their respective levels. 
4. The advanced CH and FM Trainers will be flown by FIS Instructor Pilots holding a BSup 
Category or higher. The Instructional Check will be flown by Standards or a Standards Affiliate 
using either an ACH or AFM scenario. If required, any additional sorties will be granted at the 
discretion of the CFI. The sorties listed above will be added to the Staff Progression Course in 
Flight Pro under the “Phase 3 Upgrade” folder. 
5. IP’s who are unsuccessful on their initial attempt may re-attempt no earlier than 30 days 
once appropriate remedial training has been conducted, at the discretion of the CFI.
